# G300-3/4

## embellish

Let's break down the word "embellish" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Embellish**

**Meaning:**

"Embellish" has two primary meanings, both important to understand, especially for the GRE:

1.  **To make something more attractive or beautiful by adding decorative details or features.**
    *   **Think:** Decorating, adorning, ornamenting.
    *   **Example (literal):** You might embellish a dress with lace, or a room with flowers.

2.  **To make a story, report, or account more interesting or entertaining by adding extra details, especially ones that are not true.**
    *   **Think:** Exaggerating, fabricating, embroidering, spicing up.
    *   **Example (figurative):** Someone might embellish a story of a mundane event to make it sound more exciting, often by stretching the truth or adding fictional elements.
    *   **GRE Note:** This second meaning is very common in GRE Verbal Reasoning questions. It often carries a connotation of deceit or unreliability, implying that the speaker or writer is distorting the truth.

**Common Collocations:**

When studying collocations, think about *what* is embellished and *how* it is embellished.

*   **For meaning 1 (decorate):**
    *   **Embellish a dress/gown/garment:** 裝飾禮服/服裝
    *   **Embellish a manuscript/text:** 潤飾手稿/文本
    *   **Embellish a room/interior:** 佈置房間/室內
    *   **Embellish with details/ornaments/flourishes:** 用細節/裝飾品/華麗辭藻點綴

*   **For meaning 2 (exaggerate/fabricate) - *Highly relevant for GRE*:**
    *   **Embellish a story/tale:** 誇大故事/傳說
    *   **Embellish an account/report:** 誇大陳述/報告
    *   **Embellish the truth/facts:** 誇大真相/事實 (implies distortion or lying)
    *   **Embellish one's achievements/résumé:** 誇大成就/履歷
    *   **Embellish liberally/significantly:** 大幅/顯著誇大

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples demonstrating both meanings, with a focus on the more nuanced second meaning for GRE relevance:

1.  **Context: Decorative (physical object)**
    *   **English:** The seamstress decided to **embellish** the simple white wedding gown with delicate pearls and intricate lace.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 裁縫師決定用精緻的珍珠和複雜的蕾絲來**點綴**這件簡單的白色婚紗。

2.  **Context: Exaggeration (for entertainment, somewhat neutral)**
    *   **English:** My grandfather loves to **embellish** his old war stories, adding a few dramatic details to make them more entertaining for his grandchildren.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我爺爺很喜歡**誇大**他過去的戰爭故事，添加一些戲劇性的細節，讓它們對孫子們來說更有趣。

3.  **Context: Deception/Distortion (negative connotation, common in GRE)**
    *   **English:** The politician was accused of **embellishing** his economic achievements to win votes during the election campaign.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政治家被指控在競選期間**誇大**他的經濟成就以贏得選票。

4.  **Context: Distortion of truth (negative connotation)**
    *   **English:** While the core facts of her testimony were true, the lawyer suspected she had **embellished** certain details to make her client appear more sympathetic.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然她證詞的核心事實是真實的，但律師懷疑她**誇大**了某些細節，以使她的客戶看起來更值得同情。

5.  **Context: Literary/Artistic (adding creative but non-essential elements)**
    *   **English:** The author chose to **embellish** the historical narrative with fictional dialogues and vivid descriptions, blurring the line between fact and imagination.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 作者選擇用虛構的對話和生動的描寫來**潤飾**這部歷史敘事，模糊了事實與想像之間的界線。

6.  **Context: Professional/Academic (negative connotation of dishonesty)**
    *   **English:** Companies often conduct thorough background checks because they know some applicants tend to **embellish** their work experience on their résumés.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 公司通常會進行徹底的背景調查，因為他們知道有些申請人傾向於在履歷上**誇大**他們的工作經驗。

---

By understanding both facets of "embellish" and practicing with these varied examples, you'll be well-prepared for how it might appear on the GRE!

## amiable

Let's break down "amiable" for your GRE prep!

### Meaning of "Amiable"

**Meaning:**
"Amiable" (pronounced AY-mee-uh-bul) is an adjective used to describe someone who is **friendly, pleasant, good-natured, and easy to get along with**. It implies a gentle, agreeable, and generally cheerful disposition. It's about having a pleasant and friendly personality that makes others feel comfortable and welcome.

*   **Synonyms (for GRE prep):** congenial, genial, affable, good-natured, charming, pleasant, agreeable.
*   **Antonyms:** hostile, disagreeable, surly, grumpy, ill-tempered, standoffish.

### Common Collocations for "Amiable"

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these helps you use the word more fluently and accurately.

*   **Amiable person/individual:** Describes someone who has an amiable character.
*   **Amiable disposition/nature/temperament:** Refers to someone's inherent friendly personality.
*   **Amiable smile/expression/demeanor:** Describes the outward manifestation of someone's amiability.
*   **Amiable conversation/chat:** A friendly and pleasant discussion.
*   **Generally/usually/always amiable:** Indicates a consistent friendly nature.
*   **Remarkably/exceptionally amiable:** Highlights an unusually high degree of friendliness.
*   **To remain amiable:** To keep a friendly attitude, even in difficult situations.

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences to illustrate the use of "amiable," along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Describing a person's general character**
    *   **English:** The new neighbor was an **amiable** woman who always had a kind word for everyone on the street.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位新鄰居是個**和藹可親**的女士，她總是對街上的每個人說些好聽的話。

2.  **Context: In a professional or formal setting, describing an atmosphere**
    *   **English:** Despite the intense negotiations, both parties maintained an **amiable** atmosphere, which ultimately led to a successful agreement.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管談判激烈，雙方仍保持著**友好**的氣氛，最終促成了一項成功的協議。

3.  **Context: Highlighting a specific trait or disposition**
    *   **English:** His **amiable** disposition made him incredibly popular among his classmates, as he was always willing to lend a hand.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他**和藹可親**的性格使他在同學中極受歡迎，因為他總是樂於助人。

4.  **Context: Describing someone remaining pleasant despite difficulty**
    *   **English:** Even after hours of frustrating delays, the flight attendant remained remarkably **amiable**, soothing agitated passengers with a calm smile.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使經歷了數小時令人沮喪的延誤，這位空服員依然保持著顯著的**和善**態度，用平靜的微笑安撫著焦躁不安的乘客。

5.  **Context: Describing a physical manifestation of amiability**
    *   **English:** The old professor, with his **amiable** smile and twinkling eyes, was a beloved figure on campus.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 老教授帶著他那**和藹**的笑容和閃爍的眼睛，是校園裡一位備受愛戴的人物。

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to encounter "amiable" on the GRE!

## tenuous

Okay, let's break down the word "tenuous" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Tenuous"

**I. Meaning**

"**Tenuous**" (pronounced /ˈten.ju.əs/) is an adjective that primarily means:

1.  **Very weak or slight:** Lacking substance, strength, or certainty. It suggests something is flimsy, fragile, or insubstantial.
2.  **Having little substance or significance:** Not firm, solid, or well-supported; easily broken, disproven, or lost.
3.  **Thin, slender, or delicate:** (Less common in modern usage, but relates to its Latin root "tenuis" meaning "thin").

Think of it as something that's barely there, hanging by a thread, or easily challenged.

**Synonyms:** weak, flimsy, fragile, delicate, insubstantial, slender, precarious, doubtful, uncertain, vague, unconvincing, shaky, unsubstantiated.

**Antonyms:** strong, firm, substantial, solid, robust, convincing, well-supported.

---

**II. Common Collocations**

"Tenuous" frequently appears with nouns that denote connections, evidence, support, or abstract concepts that can be weak or unstable.

*   **Tenuous connection/link/thread:** A weak or easily broken bond.
*   **Tenuous evidence/argument/claim:** Evidence or a point that is weak, unconvincing, or easily disproven.
*   **Tenuous grasp/hold:** A weak or insecure grip, either literally or figuratively (e.g., a tenuous grasp on reality).
*   **Tenuous hope/chance:** A very slight or unlikely possibility.
*   **Tenuous existence/position/control:** A situation that is unstable, vulnerable, or barely maintained.
*   **Tenuous distinction/line:** A very fine or subtle difference that is hard to maintain or define.
*   **Tenuous relationship:** A fragile or unstable relationship.

---

**III. Example Sentences in Various Contexts & Traditional Chinese Translations**

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Context: Weak Evidence/Argument**
    *   **English:** The prosecutor's case was built on such **tenuous evidence** that the jury quickly acquitted the defendant.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 檢察官的案子建立在如此**薄弱的證據**之上，陪審團很快就宣判被告無罪。
    *   *(Explanation: The evidence was insufficient and easily challenged.)*

2.  **Context: Fragile Connection/Relationship**
    *   **English:** After years of neglect, the **tenuous connection** between the estranged siblings finally broke.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年的疏遠，這對疏遠的兄弟姊妹之間**脆弱的連結**終於斷裂了。
    *   *(Explanation: The bond was very weak and easily severed.)*

3.  **Context: Precarious Hold/Grasp (Figurative)**
    *   **English:** Living on a fixed income, the elderly couple had only a **tenuous hold** on their financial stability.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 靠著固定收入過活，這對年邁的夫婦對他們的財務穩定只有**微乎其微的掌控**。
    *   *(Explanation: Their financial security was very fragile and could easily collapse.)*

4.  **Context: Slim Hope/Chance**
    *   **English:** Despite the bleak outlook, they clung to a **tenuous hope** that a rescue team would eventually find them.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管前景黯淡，他們仍緊抱著**一線微茫的希望**，期待救援隊最終能找到他們。
    *   *(Explanation: The hope was very faint and unlikely to be realized.)*

5.  **Context: Unstable Existence/Control**
    *   **English:** After the revolution, the new government's control over the outlying regions remained **tenuous**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 革命後，新政府對邊遠地區的控制依然**不穩固**。
    *   *(Explanation: The control was not firm or secure; it could easily be lost.)*

6.  **Context: Philosophical/Abstract Distinction**
    *   **English:** The **tenuous distinction** between art and craft often leads to endless debate among critics.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 藝術與工藝之間那**模糊的界線**，常引發評論家們無休止的爭論。
    *   *(Explanation: The difference is very fine, subtle, and hard to define clearly.)*

---

By understanding these examples and collocations, you'll be well-prepared to encounter "tenuous" on the GRE and use it accurately in your writing!

## deflect

Okay, let's break down the word "deflect" for your GRE studies.

---

### Understanding "Deflect"

**Meaning:**

"Deflect" primarily means **to cause something to change direction**, especially after hitting something, or **to turn aside** something (like criticism, questions, or attention).

Think of it in two main ways:

1.  **Physical:** To make a physical object move in a different direction after it strikes a surface or object.
    *   *Example:* A shield *deflects* a sword blow. A bulletproof vest *deflects* a bullet.

2.  **Figurative/Abstract:** To skillfully avoid or redirect something non-physical, such as an argument, blame, criticism, or unwanted attention. This often implies a strategic maneuver to prevent something from hitting its intended target or having its intended effect.
    *   *Example:* A politician *deflects* difficult questions. Someone *deflects* blame onto another.

**Part of Speech:** Verb

---

### Common Collocations for "Deflect"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you use "deflect" naturally and understand it in various contexts, especially on the GRE.

**Physical Contexts:**

*   **Deflect a blow/punch/kick:** To turn aside a physical attack.
*   **Deflect a bullet/missile/projectile:** To cause a fast-moving object to change course.
*   **Deflect a ball/shot:** In sports, to change the direction of a ball or puck.
*   **Deflect light/heat:** To cause light or heat to bounce off or bend away.
*   **Deflect impact/force:** To absorb or redirect the energy of a collision.

**Figurative/Abstract Contexts:**

*   **Deflect criticism/blame/responsibility:** To avoid or redirect negative judgment or accountability.
*   **Deflect a question/inquiry:** To avoid answering directly, often by changing the subject.
*   **Deflect an accusation/charge:** To deny or redirect an accusation made against oneself.
*   **Deflect attention:** To draw focus away from something or someone.
*   **Deflect an argument/challenge:** To avoid directly engaging with or accepting an argument or challenge.

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples to illustrate "deflect" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Sports (Physical redirection)**
    *   **English:** The goalkeeper managed to **deflect** the powerful shot over the crossbar, preventing a goal.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 守門員設法將那記強勁的射門**擋**出橫樑上方，避免了失球。

2.  **Context: Protection/Physics (Physical redirection of force)**
    *   **English:** The car's advanced crumple zone was designed to **deflect** the force of a head-on collision, protecting the passengers.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這輛車先進的潰縮區設計旨在**分散**正面碰撞的衝擊力，以保護乘客。

3.  **Context: Politics/Interviews (Figurative redirection of questions)**
    *   **English:** The politician skillfully **deflected** difficult questions about his past by immediately changing the subject to economic policy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位政治人物巧妙地**避開**了關於他過去的棘手問題，立刻將話題轉移到經濟政策上。

4.  **Context: Responsibility/Accountability (Figurative redirection of blame)**
    *   **English:** He tried to **deflect** the blame for the project's failure onto his team members, even though he was ultimately in charge.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他試圖將專案失敗的責任**推卸**給他的團隊成員，儘管他才是最終的負責人。

5.  **Context: Social Interaction/Conversation (Figurative redirection of attention/topic)**
    *   **English:** Whenever the conversation got too personal, she would **deflect** it with a witty remark or by asking someone else a question.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 每當對話變得過於私人時，她就會用一句機智的評論或向別人提問來**轉移**話題。

6.  **Context: Public Criticism (Figurative redirection of negative judgment)**
    *   **English:** Her calm and confident response helped to **deflect** much of the initial criticism she received.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她冷靜自信的回應幫助她**化解**了大部分最初受到的批評。

7.  **Context: Military/Defense (Physical redirection of projectiles)**
    *   **English:** The armored plating on the tank was thick enough to **deflect** most incoming anti-tank rounds.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 坦克上的裝甲板夠厚，足以**彈開**大部分來襲的反坦克炮彈。

---

**Key Takeaway for GRE:**

For the GRE, remember that "deflect" can be used both literally (for physical objects) and figuratively (for abstract concepts like criticism, blame, or attention). The common thread is always *changing direction* or *turning aside* something that is aimed at a particular target.

## deluge

Okay, let's break down the word "deluge" for your GRE prep!

---

### **DELUGE**

**Part of Speech:** Can be used as both a **noun** and a **verb**.

---

#### **Meaning:**

1.  **As a Noun:**
    *   **Literal Meaning:** A severe flood; an overwhelming rush of water. (Think of "The Great Deluge" from biblical stories.)
    *   **Figurative Meaning (Most Common in GRE Context):** An overwhelming quantity of something that arrives or occurs at the same time, often overwhelming or challenging. It suggests a vast, unmanageable amount.

2.  **As a Verb:**
    *   **Literal Meaning:** To flood with water; to inundate.
    *   **Figurative Meaning (Most Common in GRE Context):** To overwhelm (someone or something) with a large quantity of something; to swamp.

---

#### **Common Collocations:**

When "deluge" is used figuratively (which is most common on the GRE), it often collocates with:

*   **A deluge of + [plural noun/uncountable noun]:**
    *   *A deluge of emails / requests / questions / applications / complaints / information / criticism / data.*
*   **Verb + a deluge:**
    *   *Face a deluge / receive a deluge / be hit by a deluge / cause a deluge.*
*   **Be deluged with + [plural noun/uncountable noun]:** (Passive voice, verb form)
    *   *Be deluged with calls / work / mail / inquiries.*
*   **Deluge [someone/something] with + [plural noun/uncountable noun]:** (Active voice, verb form)
    *   *Deluge an office with protests / deluge a celebrity with fan mail.*

---

#### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations):**

1.  **Context: Natural Disaster (Noun - Literal)**
    *   **English:** The small town was devastated by a sudden **deluge** that caused the river to overflow its banks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個小鎮被一場突如其來的大洪水（或暴雨）摧毀，河水氾濫成災。
    *   *(Note: Here "deluge" refers to a literal flood/heavy rain.)*

2.  **Context: Information Overload (Noun - Figurative)**
    *   **English:** After the company's data breach, the customer service department received a **deluge** of angry calls and emails.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在公司數據洩露後，客戶服務部收到了排山倒海般的憤怒電話和電子郵件。
    *   *(Collocation: "received a deluge of calls and emails")*

3.  **Context: Work/Tasks (Verb - Figurative, Passive Voice)**
    *   **English:** During the Black Friday sale, the retail store was **deluged** with shoppers looking for bargains.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在黑色星期五促銷期間，這家零售店湧入了大量尋找特價品的顧客。
    *   *(Collocation: "was deluged with shoppers")*

4.  **Context: Overwhelming Attention (Noun - Figurative)**
    *   **English:** The young athlete, after winning the gold medal, faced a **deluge** of media requests for interviews.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位年輕運動員在贏得金牌後，面臨著媒體如潮水般的採訪請求。
    *   *(Collocation: "faced a deluge of requests")*

5.  **Context: Overwhelming Communication (Verb - Figurative, Active Voice)**
    *   **English:** Environmental activists **deluged** the senator's office with letters and petitions protesting the new logging policy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 環境保護主義者向參議員辦公室大量發送信件和請願書，抗議新的伐木政策。
    *   *(Collocation: "deluged the office with letters and petitions")*

---

**GRE Tip:** On the GRE, "deluge" is almost always used in its **figurative sense** to describe an overwhelming amount of something (information, requests, criticism, etc.), rather than a literal flood. Pay attention to context clues that indicate a large, overwhelming quantity.

## palpable

Let's break down the word "palpable" for your GRE studies.

---

## Palpable

**Meaning:**

"Palpable" primarily has two main meanings, both of which are useful to know for the GRE:

1.  **Literally:** Able to be touched or felt; tangible. (Less common in GRE, but important for foundational understanding).
    *   *Example:* A doctor might feel a palpable lump.

2.  **Figuratively (and more commonly used in the GRE context):** So intense as to be almost touched or felt; easily perceived, obvious, or noticeable. This refers to abstract things like emotions, atmospheres, or qualities.
    *   *Example:* The tension in the room was palpable.

**Etymology Note:** The word comes from the Latin "palpare," meaning "to stroke or touch," reinforcing its core meaning.

---

**Common Collocations:**

When studying for the GRE, focus on how "palpable" describes abstract nouns, often emotions or sensations that are very strong and noticeable.

*   **Palpable tension/anxiety/fear:** A strong, easily felt sense of nervousness, worry, or dread.
*   **Palpable relief/excitement/joy:** A strong, easily felt sense of ease, enthusiasm, or happiness.
*   **Palpable sense/feeling:** A very clear and strong impression or emotion.
*   **Palpable atmosphere:** A very distinct and pervasive mood or feeling in a place.
*   **Palpable difference/effect/improvement:** A clear, undeniable, and easily observed distinction, result, or betterment.
*   **Palpable truth/presence:** An undeniable reality or a very strong, noticeable existence of something or someone.

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are multiple examples showcasing both the literal and, more importantly for the GRE, the figurative use of "palpable," with Traditional Chinese translations.

1.  **Context: Intense Emotion (Tension)**
    *   **English:** A **palpable tension** filled the courtroom as the jury prepared to deliver its verdict.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 陪審團準備宣讀判決時，法庭內彌漫著一股**顯而易見的緊張氣氛**。
    *   *Explanation:* The tension was so strong you could almost feel it, making it obvious to everyone present.

2.  **Context: Intense Emotion (Relief)**
    *   **English:** After hours of frantic searching, a **palpable sense of relief** washed over them when the lost child was found safe.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過數小時的瘋狂搜尋後，當走失的孩子被平安找到時，一股**真實可感的如釋重負**感湧上他們心頭。
    *   *Explanation:* The feeling of relief was so overwhelming and clear that it was almost physical.

3.  **Context: Observable Improvement**
    *   **English:** The economic reforms brought about a **palpable improvement** in the living standards of the poor.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經濟改革為貧困人口的生活水準帶來了**顯著的改善**。
    *   *Explanation:* The improvement was clear, distinct, and easily noticeable to anyone observing.

4.  **Context: Pervasive Fear**
    *   **English:** During the war, a **palpable fear** gripped the city's inhabitants, making them hesitant to leave their homes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 戰爭期間，一股**真實可感的恐懼**籠罩著這座城市的居民，使他們不敢輕易離開家門。
    *   *Explanation:* The fear was so strong and widespread that its presence was undeniable and almost tangible.

5.  **Context: Distinct Difference**
    *   **English:** Even to a casual observer, there was a **palpable difference** in the team's performance after the coaching change.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使是對一個普通觀察者來說，教練更換後隊伍的表現也有著**明顯的差異**。
    *   *Explanation:* The change in performance was so clear that it was unmistakable.

6.  **Context: Literal (Medical)**
    *   **English:** The doctor noted a small, **palpable lump** during the physical examination, requiring further investigation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 醫生在體檢時發現了一個小而**可觸及的腫塊**，需要進一步檢查。
    *   *Explanation:* This is the literal meaning – the lump could be physically felt by touch.

---

By understanding both the literal and, more importantly, the figurative meaning of "palpable" and its common collocations, you'll be well-prepared for how it appears on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## illicit

Let's break down the word "illicit" for your GRE prep!

---

### **Illicit**

**Meaning:**
*   (Adjective) Not permitted by law or custom; forbidden, unlawful, illegal.
*   It often implies something done secretly or underhandedly, or something considered morally wrong, even if not always a major crime.

**Think of it as:** Against the rules, forbidden, or against the law.

**Common Collocations:**

"Illicit" frequently modifies nouns that describe activities, trade, or gains that are unlawful or forbidden. Some of the most common collocations include:

*   **Illicit trade/traffic:** (e.g., in drugs, weapons, or stolen goods)
*   **Illicit activities/dealings:** (any forbidden or illegal actions)
*   **Illicit affair:** (a secret, forbidden romantic relationship, usually extramarital)
*   **Illicit gains/profits:** (money or benefits obtained illegally or unfairly)
*   **Illicit use:** (using something in a forbidden or illegal way)
*   **Illicit goods/substances:** (forbidden items like drugs or counterfeit products)

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations):**

1.  **Context: Crime/Trade (very common usage)**
    *   **English:** The police launched a major operation to crack down on the **illicit trade** in stolen goods.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 警方發起了一項重大行動，以打擊贓物的**非法交易**。
        *   *Explanation:* Here, "illicit trade" refers to buying and selling things that are against the law.

2.  **Context: Relationships/Moral Transgression**
    *   **English:** Their **illicit affair** eventually came to light, causing a scandal in the community.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們的**不倫戀**最終被揭露，在社區中引起了一場醜聞。
        *   *Explanation:* "Illicit affair" points to a secret romantic relationship that is forbidden, often due to marriage vows.

3.  **Context: Finance/Corruption**
    *   **English:** The politician was accused of using his position to acquire **illicit gains** from government contracts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政治家被指控利用職位從政府合約中獲取**不法所得**。
        *   *Explanation:* "Illicit gains" means money or profits obtained through illegal or unethical means, like bribery or fraud.

4.  **Context: General Prohibited Actions/Rules**
    *   **English:** Students caught engaging in **illicit activities**, such as cheating on exams, faced severe disciplinary action.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 學生若被發現在考試中作弊等**違規活動**，將面臨嚴重的紀律處分。
        *   *Explanation:* "Illicit activities" here refers to actions that are against the rules of the institution, even if not necessarily criminal law.

5.  **Context: Technology/Forbidden Use**
    *   **English:** The authorities are concerned about the **illicit use** of drones near airports.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當局對機場附近**非法使用**無人機的情況表示擔憂。
        *   *Explanation:* "Illicit use" means using something in a way that is forbidden by regulations or law.

---

Remember, for the GRE, understanding "illicit" as forbidden, unlawful, or unauthorized will be key. Pay attention to how it's used in context, especially with the common collocations, as they often reveal the specific nature of the transgression.

## tirade

Okay, let's break down the word "tirade" for your GRE preparation.

---

## Understanding "Tirade"

**Meaning:**
A **tirade** (pronounced /ˈtaɪreɪd/ or /taɪˈreɪd/) refers to a **long, angry, and aggressive speech of criticism or accusation.** It's usually delivered with great force and emotion, often uninterrupted, and is intended to denounce or vehemently criticize someone or something.

Think of it as a prolonged rant or a verbal lashing. It's almost always used in a negative context.

**Key characteristics:**
*   **Lengthy:** Not a quick comment, but a sustained speech.
*   **Angry/Aggressive:** Full of strong negative emotion.
*   **Critical/Accusatory:** Designed to find fault or blame.
*   **Uninterrupted:** The speaker typically dominates the conversation.

**Synonyms:** diatribe, rant, harangue, fulmination, denunciation, verbal assault.

---

## Common Collocations for "Tirade"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you understand and use "tirade" more naturally.

**Verbs often used with "tirade":**
*   **Launch/unleash/deliver/go on a tirade:** To begin or give a tirade.
    *   *Example:* He **launched into a tirade** against the new policies.
*   **Endure/suffer/be subjected to a tirade:** To experience or have to listen to a tirade.
    *   *Example:* She had to **endure a tirade** from her angry boss.

**Prepositions often used with "tirade":**
*   **Tirade against [someone/something]:** The most common way to specify the target of the criticism.
    *   *Example:* A **tirade against** government corruption.
*   **Tirade about [something]:** To specify the topic of the criticism.
    *   *Example:* His **tirade about** the team's poor performance.

**Adjectives often used to describe "tirade":**
*   **Furious/angry/bitter/blistering/vehement tirade:** Emphasize the intensity of the anger.
*   **Lengthy/prolonged tirade:** Emphasize the duration.
*   **Verbal tirade:** Redundant but sometimes used for emphasis on the spoken nature.
*   **Political tirade:** Describing the context.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate "tirade" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Workplace/Professional Setting**
    *   **English:** The CEO **launched into a furious tirade against** the sales team for missing their quarterly targets.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長針對銷售團隊未達到季度目標，**展開了一場猛烈的咆哮**。
    *   *Explanation:* Shows a powerful figure delivering an angry, critical speech to subordinates.

2.  **Context: Personal/Relationship Setting**
    *   **English:** After discovering the broken vase, she **unleashed a tirade about** his carelessness that lasted for several minutes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 發現花瓶碎了之後，她對他的粗心大意**展開了一番連珠炮似的痛罵**，持續了好幾分鐘。
    *   *Explanation:* Illustrates a personal outburst fueled by frustration or anger over a specific incident.

3.  **Context: Public/Political Setting**
    *   **English:** During the press conference, the senator **went on a lengthy tirade**, accusing his opponents of corruption and incompetence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在記者會上，那位參議員**進行了一場冗長的抨擊**，指責他的對手腐敗無能。
    *   *Explanation:* Depicts a public figure delivering a sustained, accusatory speech, often with political motivations.

4.  **Context: General/Descriptive**
    *   **English:** He had to **endure a five-minute tirade from** his father for coming home late without explanation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他因為晚歸又沒有解釋，不得不**忍受父親長達五分鐘的訓斥**。
    *   *Explanation:* Focuses on the experience of being on the receiving end of a tirade.

5.  **Context: Literary/Figurative**
    *   **English:** His speech, initially calm and collected, quickly **devolved into a bitter tirade against** the injustices of the system.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他的演講，起初平靜而從容，很快就**演變成對體制不公的激烈抨擊**。
    *   *Explanation:* Shows how a speech can transform into a tirade, highlighting the shift in tone and content.

By understanding these nuances and seeing "tirade" in action, you'll be well-prepared for any GRE question that involves this word!

## caustic

Okay, let's break down the word "caustic" for your GRE prep!

---

## Understanding "Caustic" for the GRE

The word "caustic" has two primary meanings, both of which you should be familiar with for the GRE.

### 1. Literal Meaning: Corrosive, Burning (Chemical)

*   **Meaning:** Describes a substance that is capable of burning, corroding, or destroying organic tissue by chemical action. Think of a substance that "eats away" at something.
*   **Synonyms:** Corrosive, erosive, burning, acidic, alkaline (if referring to a strong base like lye).
*   **Analogy:** Imagine a strong acid that melts through something.

### 2. Figurative Meaning: Biting, Sarcastic, Critical (Verbal/Social)

*   **Meaning:** Describes words, remarks, or a person's wit or humor that is sharp, critical, cutting, and often meant to sting or be hurtful. It's like a verbal "burn."
*   **Synonyms:** Sarcastic, biting, scathing, acrimonious, cutting, sharp, trenchant, acerbic.
*   **Analogy:** Imagine a verbal attack that leaves a metaphorical "burn" or wound.

---

## Common Collocations for "Caustic"

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these will help you recognize and use "caustic" correctly.

**For the Literal Meaning:**

*   **Caustic substance/chemical/liquid/solution:** Refers to the material itself.
*   **Caustic soda:** A common name for sodium hydroxide, a strong base.
*   **Caustic burn/injury:** The type of physical damage caused by such a substance.

**For the Figurative Meaning:**

*   **Caustic remarks/comments:** Sharp, critical, or sarcastic statements.
*   **Caustic wit/humor:** A form of humor that is sharp, ironic, and often cynical or critical.
*   **Caustic criticism/analysis:** Very harsh or severe judgment or examination.
*   **Caustic tone/voice:** A manner of speaking that is harsh, unpleasant, or critical.
*   **Caustic retort/reply:** A quick, sharp, and often critical or sarcastic answer.
*   **Caustic observation:** A sharp, critical insight.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations)

Here are multiple examples illustrating both meanings in different contexts:

### Literal Examples (Chemical/Physical)

1.  **Context: Industrial Safety**
    *   **Sentence:** The industrial cleaner contained a highly **caustic substance** that could burn skin on contact, so protective gloves were mandatory.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這種工業清潔劑含有一種高度**腐蝕性物質**，接觸皮膚會造成灼傷，因此必須戴防護手套。

2.  **Context: Medical/Accident**
    *   **Sentence:** He suffered **caustic burns** on his hands after accidentally spilling the strong alkaline solution in the laboratory.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他不慎在實驗室打翻了強鹼溶液，導致雙手遭受了**腐蝕性灼傷**。

3.  **Context: Metallurgy/Manufacturing**
    *   **Sentence:** The factory uses a **caustic solution** to strip the old paint from metal parts before repainting them.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家工廠使用一種**腐蝕性溶液**來剝除金屬零件上的舊漆，然後再重新噴漆。

### Figurative Examples (Verbal/Social)

1.  **Context: Criticism/Commentary**
    *   **Sentence:** Her **caustic criticism** of the government's new policy was widely published and sparked heated public debate.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對政府新政策的**尖刻批評**被廣泛發表，並引發了激烈的公眾辯論。

2.  **Context: Personality/Humor**
    *   **Sentence:** Despite his **caustic wit**, he was surprisingly beloved by his colleagues, who appreciated his honesty and unconventional perspective.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他有著**尖銳刻薄的幽默感**，但他出奇地受到同事們的喜愛，他們欣賞他的誠實和不落俗套的觀點。

3.  **Context: Literature/Rhetoric**
    *   **Sentence:** The satirist's play was filled with **caustic commentary** on social inequality, forcing the audience to confront uncomfortable truths.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位諷刺作家的戲劇充滿了對社會不平等的**辛辣評論**，迫使觀眾面對令人不舒服的真相。

4.  **Context: Interpersonal Communication**
    *   **Sentence:** She delivered a **caustic retort** to his rude comment, leaving him speechless and regretting his words.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對他的無禮評論做出了**尖刻的反駁**，讓他啞口無言，後悔自己的言辭。

---

**GRE Tip:** For the GRE, "caustic" often appears in reading comprehension passages or vocabulary in context questions, where understanding its dual nature (literal chemical vs. figurative verbal) is key. Pay attention to the surrounding words to determine which meaning is intended!

## cacophony

Okay, let's break down "cacophony" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Cacophony"

**Meaning:**
**Cacophony** (pronounced kuh-KOFF-uh-nee) refers to a harsh, discordant, or jarring mixture of sounds. It's not just "loud noise"; it's specifically noise that is unpleasant, chaotic, and lacking in harmony or order.

*   **Etymology Hint (useful for GRE):** The word comes from Greek roots: "kakos" (bad) and "phōnē" (sound). So, literally, "bad sound."

**Key Characteristics:**
*   **Discordant:** Lacks harmony.
*   **Harsh:** Unpleasant to the ear.
*   **Chaotic:** A jumble of sounds, not organized.
*   **Overwhelming:** Can be so intense that it's difficult to distinguish individual sounds.

**Opposite:** The opposite of cacophony is **euphony**, which refers to sounds that are pleasing, harmonious, and melodious.

---

### Common Collocations

A "collocation" is a natural pairing of words that often go together. Knowing these helps you use the word correctly and sound more natural.

The most common collocation for "cacophony" is:

1.  **"Cacophony of [Nouns/Sounds]"**: This describes the source or type of the unpleasant sounds.
    *   *Examples:* a cacophony of voices, a cacophony of horns, a cacophony of alarms, a cacophony of sounds, a cacophony of instruments.

Other useful collocations/phrases:

2.  **"Descend into cacophony" / "Erupt into cacophony"**: Describes a situation transitioning from order to chaotic noise.
3.  **"Sheer cacophony" / "Deafening cacophony"**: Emphasizes the intensity or complete disarray of the sounds.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "cacophony" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Music/Orchestra (before a performance)**
    *   **English:** Before the conductor appeared, the orchestra pit was filled with a **cacophony of tuning instruments** – the blare of trumpets, the screech of violins, and the rumble of timpani all at once.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在指揮家出現之前，樂團池裡充滿了**調音樂器的刺耳噪音**——小號的響聲、小提琴的尖銳聲和定音鼓的隆隆聲同時響起。

2.  **Context: City/Urban Environment**
    *   **English:** Living in the heart of the city meant constantly being exposed to the **cacophony of traffic**, sirens, and construction work, making quiet moments a rarity.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 住在市中心意味著不斷暴露在**交通、警報器和施工的刺耳噪音**中，寧靜的時刻變得十分稀有。

3.  **Context: Discussion/Meeting (metaphorical, or literal shouting)**
    *   **English:** The political debate quickly **devolved into a cacophony of interruptions** and personal attacks, making it impossible to hear coherent arguments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這場政治辯論很快就**演變成一片打斷和人身攻擊的嘈雜聲**，令人無法聽到連貫的論點。

4.  **Context: Nature/Animals (when sounds are unpleasant)**
    *   **English:** As the sun set, the usually peaceful forest was invaded by a **cacophony of cicadas**, their combined chirping a relentless, high-pitched drone.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 隨著太陽下山，原本寧靜的森林被**蟬鳴的刺耳嘈雜聲**入侵，它們結合的鳴叫聲是一種不間斷的、高音調的嗡嗡聲。

5.  **Context: Machinery/Industrial (old or broken equipment)**
    *   **English:** The antique printing press groaned and whirred, producing a continuous **cacophony of mechanical noises** as it slowly printed each page.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這台古老的印刷機發出咯吱聲和嗡嗡聲，在緩慢列印每一頁時，產生了連續不斷的**機械噪音大雜燴**。

6.  **Context: Children/Play (can be joyful but still noisy)**
    *   **English:** The daycare center at closing time was a delightful **cacophony of children's laughter**, cries, and the clatter of toys as parents arrived to pick them up.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 幼兒園在關門時間時是**兒童笑聲、哭聲和玩具碰撞聲**的愉悅**嘈雜交響曲**，當時父母們陸續來接孩子。

---

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "cacophony" on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## capricious

That's an excellent word for GRE prep! Let's break down "capricious."

### Meaning of "Capricious"

**Capricious** (adjective) describes someone or something that is given to sudden and unaccountable changes of mood or behavior. It implies unpredictability, inconsistency, and often unreliability.

Think of it as being:
*   **Fickle:** Changing frequently, especially as regards one's loyalties, interests, or affection.
*   **Whimsical:** Playfully quaint or fanciful, especially in an appealing and amusing way. (Often used for things like art or design, less so for people in a negative sense).
*   **Erratic:** Not regular or consistent in movement, behavior, or quality; unpredictable.
*   **Impulsive:** Acting or done without forethought.
*   **Unpredictable:** Not able to be predicted; liable to change suddenly and without warning.

**Core Idea:** Lack of consistency, sudden shifts without clear reason.

### Common Collocations for "Capricious"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing them helps you understand context and use the word naturally.

*   **Capricious nature/temperament/moods:** Describing a person's personality or emotional state.
*   **Capricious weather/climate:** Referring to unpredictable natural conditions.
*   **Capricious fate/fortune:** Describing luck or destiny as being unpredictable.
*   **Capricious market/demand:** Describing economic conditions that change suddenly.
*   **Capricious leader/boss/individual:** Describing someone in authority who makes inconsistent decisions.
*   **Capricious decisions/policies/rules:** Describing choices or guidelines that are inconsistent or arbitrary.
*   **Capricious justice:** Implying that fairness is applied inconsistently or arbitrarily.

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use:

1.  **Context: People's behavior/personality**
    *   **English:** The employees struggled to keep up with their CEO's **capricious** demands, which changed almost daily.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 員工們很難跟上他們執行長**反覆無常**的要求，那些要求幾乎每天都在變。

2.  **Context: Natural phenomena**
    *   **English:** Mountain climbers must always be prepared for the **capricious** weather, as conditions can shift dramatically without warning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 登山者必須時刻準備好應對**變幻莫測**的天氣，因為情況可能在毫無預警的情況下急劇變化。

3.  **Context: Abstract concepts (e.g., economics, fate)**
    *   **English:** Investing in the stock market can be risky due to its **capricious** nature; prices can swing wildly in a short period.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 由於股市**變化多端**的本質，投資股市可能存在風險；股價可能在短時間內劇烈波動。

4.  **Context: Rules/Justice/Decision-making (implying unfairness or arbitrariness)**
    *   **English:** The judge was criticized for his **capricious** rulings, which seemed to depend more on his mood than on legal precedent.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位法官因其**武斷任性**的判決而受到批評，這些判決似乎更多地取決於他的心情而非法律先例。

5.  **Context: Art/Fashion (sometimes a more neutral or slightly positive connotation)**
    *   **English:** Her artistic style is wonderfully **capricious**, evolving constantly and refusing to be confined to a single genre.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的藝術風格**奇思妙想**、**變化多端**，不斷演變，不願被限制在單一類型中。

By understanding these examples and collocations, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "capricious" on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## empirical

Okay, let's break down "empirical" for your GRE studies. This is a very common and important word, especially in academic contexts, so mastering it will definitely help.

---

### Meaning of "Empirical"

**Empirical** (adjective) primarily means:

1.  **Based on, concerned with, or verifiable by observation or experience rather than theory or pure logic.**
2.  **Derived from or guided by experience or experiment.**

In simpler terms, if something is "empirical," it means it's based on real-world evidence, data collected through senses (sight, hearing, touch), or through experiments. It's about what you can observe, measure, and test, rather than just what you can think up or deduce logically.

**Key Contrast:**
*   **Empirical** often stands in contrast to **theoretical**, **speculative**, or **hypothetical**. While theory might propose an idea, empirical work tests that idea against reality.

**Think of it this way:**
*   A *theoretical* physicist might propose a new theory about black holes using complex equations.
*   An *empirical* astrophysicist would use telescopes and other instruments to gather data to see if that theory matches actual observations of black holes.

---

### Common Collocations of "Empirical"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will make your writing and comprehension more natural and accurate.

The most common collocations for "empirical" are usually followed by nouns describing evidence, data, or methods:

*   **Empirical evidence:** Concrete proof or data gathered from observation or experiment.
*   **Empirical data:** Information or facts collected through direct observation or experimentation.
*   **Empirical research:** Studies or investigations that gather data through observation or experimentation.
*   **Empirical study:** Another term for empirical research.
*   **Empirical findings:** The results or conclusions derived from empirical research.
*   **Empirical analysis:** The process of examining empirical data to draw conclusions.
*   **Empirical observation:** The act of observing something directly to gather information.
*   **Empirical approach/method:** A way of dealing with something that relies on experience and observation.
*   **Empirical knowledge:** Knowledge gained through experience and observation.
*   **Empirical support:** Evidence that backs up a theory or claim, based on observation.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "empirical" in different contexts, with Traditional Chinese translations:

**1. Science/Research Context (Focus on Evidence/Data):**

*   **English:** The scientist's theory, while elegant, lacked sufficient **empirical evidence** to be widely accepted by the scientific community.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位科學家的理論雖然優雅，但缺乏足夠的**實證證據**，因此未能被科學界廣泛接受。
    *   *(Note: 實證證據 (shízhèng zhèngjù) is a common and precise translation for "empirical evidence" in scientific contexts.)*

*   **English:** Researchers collected vast amounts of **empirical data** through extensive field observations and laboratory experiments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 研究人員透過廣泛的實地觀察和實驗室實驗，收集了大量的**經驗數據**。
    *   *(Note: 經驗數據 (jīngyàn shùjù) is a direct translation for "empirical data" emphasizing data from experience.)*

*   **English:** Publishing **empirical research** requires rigorous methodology and transparent reporting of results.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 發表**實證研究**需要嚴謹的方法論和透明的結果報告。
    *   *(Note: 實證研究 (shízhèng yánjiū) is standard for "empirical research" in academic settings.)*

**2. Philosophy/Knowledge Context (Contrast with Pure Thought):**

*   **English:** Much of our understanding of the natural world comes from **empirical observation** rather than pure speculation or *a priori* reasoning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我們對自然界的許多理解，來自於**經驗觀察**而非純粹的臆測或先驗推理。
    *   *(Note: 經驗觀察 (jīngyàn guānchá) clearly conveys "empirical observation.")*

*   **English:** Philosophy often debates the extent to which human knowledge is **empirical** or inherently rational.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 哲學經常辯論人類知識在多大程度上是**經驗的**或天生理性的。
    *   *(Note: 經驗的 (jīngyànde) is a general adjective form meaning "of experience.")*

**3. Practical/Problem-Solving Context (Trial and Error):**

*   **English:** His approach to problem-solving was purely **empirical**, relying on trial and error until a solution emerged.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他解決問題的方法純粹是**依據經驗的**，依靠不斷試錯直到找到解決方案。
    *   *(Note: 依據經驗的 (yījù jīngyànde) means "based on experience," fitting the trial-and-error context.)*

**4. Social Sciences/Economics Context:**

*   **English:** Economists often use **empirical analysis** to test the validity of their theoretical models against real-world economic conditions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經濟學家經常使用**實證分析**來檢驗他們理論模型在現實經濟條件下的有效性。
    *   *(Note: 實證分析 (shízhèng fēnxī) is common for "empirical analysis" in social sciences.)*

*   **English:** There is growing **empirical support** for the effectiveness of early childhood education programs in improving long-term academic outcomes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 越來越多的**實證支持**表明，早期兒童教育計畫在改善長期學業成績方面是有效的。
    *   *(Note: 實證支持 (shízhèng zhīchí) translates directly to "empirical support.")*

---

By understanding "empirical" as being grounded in real-world observation and data, and familiarizing yourself with its common collocations and varied applications, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## amenable

Okay, let's break down the word "amenable" for your GRE prep! It's a fantastic word to know as it appears quite often in various contexts.

---

### Meaning of "Amenable"

"Amenable" (pronounced uh-MEE-nuh-bul) primarily has two core meanings, both of which are important for the GRE:

1.  **Willing to agree or be influenced/persuaded; responsive; cooperative.**
    *   This is the most common usage, usually referring to people or groups. It implies a readiness to accept suggestions, advice, or authority.
    *   **Synonyms:** compliant, cooperative, docile, agreeable, receptive, open.
    *   **Antonyms:** resistant, unwilling, obstinate, stubborn, recalcitrant.

2.  **Capable of being acted upon in a particular way; susceptible to; subject to.**
    *   This usage often refers to things, ideas, or conditions rather than people. It means something can be subjected to a specific process, analysis, or treatment.
    *   **Synonyms:** susceptible, subject, responsive (in a different sense than above), open (to).

**Key Grammatical Point:** "Amenable" is almost always followed by the preposition **"to"**.

---

### Common Collocations for "Amenable"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these helps you use the word naturally and understand it in context.

*   **Amenable to suggestion/advice:** Willing to take advice or listen to ideas.
*   **Amenable to persuasion:** Easily convinced.
*   **Amenable to change:** Open to adapting or altering things.
*   **Amenable to reason:** Willing to listen to and be guided by logic.
*   **Amenable to compromise:** Open to finding a middle ground.
*   **Amenable to discussion:** Ready to talk things over.
*   **Amenable to criticism:** Willing to accept and consider critical feedback.
*   **Amenable to treatment:** (Medical context) Responds well to a cure or therapy.
*   **Amenable to analysis/testing:** Capable of being analyzed or tested.
*   **Amenable to the law/rules:** Subject to or bound by laws/rules.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples showcasing both meanings in different scenarios:

**Context 1: Willingness/Cooperation (Common Meaning)**

1.  **English:** The committee proved surprisingly **amenable to** the new proposal, quickly approving the budget changes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 委員會出乎意料地**樂意接受**新提案，很快就批准了預算變動。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "amenable to" means they were open and willing to accept the proposal.

2.  **English:** As a good leader, she prided herself on being **amenable to** constructive criticism, always seeking ways to improve.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 作為一位優秀的領導者，她為自己**樂於接受**建設性批評而自豪，總是不斷尋求改進的方法。
    *   *Explanation:* She is open and receptive to feedback.

3.  **English:** After hours of negotiation, both parties finally became **amenable to** a compromise, and a deal was struck.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過數小時的談判，雙方最終**願意妥協**，達成了一項協議。
    *   *Explanation:* They became willing to reach a middle ground.

**Context 2: Subject to/Capable of (More Formal/Technical Meaning)**

4.  **English:** Not all historical data is **amenable to** precise statistical analysis; much of it requires qualitative interpretation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 並非所有歷史數據都**適合**精確的統計分析；其中大部分需要定性解釋。
    *   *Explanation:* The data isn't suited for or cannot be subjected to precise statistical analysis.

5.  **English:** Fortunately, the rare disease was found to be **amenable to** a new experimental drug, offering hope to many patients.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 幸運的是，這種罕見疾病被發現對一種新的實驗性藥物**反應良好**，為許多患者帶來了希望。
    *   *Explanation:* The disease responds positively to the drug.

6.  **English:** The complex philosophical concept was not easily **amenable to** a simple, concise definition.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個複雜的哲學概念不**容易被**簡潔地定義。
    *   *Explanation:* The concept cannot easily be subjected to or boiled down to a simple definition.

7.  **English:** All citizens are **amenable to** the laws of the land, regardless of their social status.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 所有公民都**必須遵守**國家法律，無論其社會地位如何。
    *   *Explanation:* Citizens are subject to and bound by the laws.

---

**GRE Tip:** When you encounter "amenable" on the GRE, especially in Reading Comprehension or Sentence Equivalence/Text Completion, pay close attention to the context to determine which of the two main meanings is intended. Often, the preposition "to" is a huge clue, and the word or phrase following "to" will specify what the subject is amenable to.

Good luck with your GRE studies!

## gaudy

Let's break down "gaudy" for your GRE prep!

---

## Gaudy

**Meaning:**
"**Gaudy**" (pronounced GAU-dee) describes something that is **excessively showy, flashy, or brightly colored, often in a tasteless or vulgar way.** It implies an attempt to be impressive or attractive that ultimately fails, coming across as cheap, overdone, or lacking in good taste. It's often used for things that are meant to be luxurious or grand but end up looking tacky or crude due to poor design or an overwhelming display.

**Think:** Bright, but *too* bright; decorated, but *over*-decorated; flashy, but *unpleasantly* flashy.

**Common Collocations:**

"Gaudy" frequently modifies nouns related to appearance, decoration, or display:

*   **Gaudy jewelry/accessories:** (e.g., a gaudy necklace, gaudy earrings)
*   **Gaudy decorations/display:** (e.g., gaudy Christmas decorations, a gaudy display of wealth)
*   **Gaudy outfit/clothes/dress:** (e.g., a gaudy suit, a gaudy wedding dress)
*   **Gaudy colors/patterns:** (e.g., gaudy floral patterns, gaudy neon colors)
*   **Gaudy design/architecture:** (e.g., a gaudy building, gaudy interior design)
*   **Gaudy trinkets/souvenirs:** (e.g., cheap, flashy items sold to tourists)

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are multiple examples with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Fashion/Personal Appearance**
    *   **English:** Her **gaudy** necklace, encrusted with oversized fake diamonds, clashed horribly with her simple black dress.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她那條鑲滿了碩大假鑽石的**俗豔**項鍊，與她樸素的黑色洋裝格格不入，顯得十分突兀。
    *   *Explanation:* The necklace is trying too hard to look expensive and grand, but its excessive and fake nature makes it tasteless.

2.  **Context: Interior Design/Decor**
    *   **English:** The hotel lobby was an assault on the senses, with its **gaudy** gold fixtures, purple velvet furniture, and flashing neon signs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這間旅館的大廳金碧輝煌得有些**俗氣**，紫色絨面家具和閃爍的霓虹燈都讓感官受到強烈衝擊。
    *   *Explanation:* The elements (gold, velvet, neon) are individually luxurious or striking, but their combination is overwhelming and in poor taste.

3.  **Context: Objects/Souvenirs**
    *   **English:** He found the souvenir shop filled with **gaudy** trinkets and tasteless mementos, none of which he wanted to buy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他發現這家紀念品店裡擺滿了**華而不實**的小飾品和庸俗的紀念品，他一樣都不想買。
    *   *Explanation:* The items are cheaply made but overly decorated or brightly colored to attract attention, resulting in a tacky appearance.

4.  **Context: Events/Displays**
    *   **English:** The politician's campaign rally featured a **gaudy** stage setup, complete with pyrotechnics and enormous flashing banners.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位政治人物的競選造勢晚會，舞台佈置**浮誇**而**炫目**，還配有煙火和巨大的閃爍橫幅。
    *   *Explanation:* The stage aims to be impressive and impactful but overshoots, becoming excessively showy and perhaps even a bit vulgar in its extravagance.

5.  **Context: Colors/Patterns**
    *   **English:** She recoiled from the **gaudy** floral wallpaper, which made the small room feel even more cramped and overwhelming.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對那張**花裡胡哨**的印花壁紙感到厭惡，它讓原本就小的房間顯得更加擁擠和令人窒息。
    *   *Explanation:* The pattern is too loud and busy for the space, making it visually unpleasant and claustrophobic.

---

Understanding "gaudy" is important for the GRE as it often appears in contexts related to aesthetics, criticism, and nuanced descriptions of taste or lack thereof. Good luck with your studies!

## burgeon

Okay, let's break down "burgeon" for your GRE prep!

### Burgeon

**Meaning:**

*   **Definition:** To grow or develop quickly and flourish. It often implies a new beginning, a rapid expansion, or a vigorous sprouting, much like a bud opening into a flower.
*   **Etymology:** The word comes from Old French "burjon," meaning "bud." This helps to remember its core sense of new growth.
*   **Connotation:** Generally positive, suggesting vitality, increase, and success. However, it can also be used neutrally or even slightly negatively if referring to something undesirable that is growing rapidly (e.g., "problems burgeoned").
*   **Grammar:** Primarily used as an **intransitive verb** (meaning it doesn't take a direct object). It's also very commonly seen as an **adjective**: **burgeoning** (e.g., "a burgeoning economy").

**Common Collocations:**

When something "burgeons," or is "burgeoning," it typically refers to things that grow or expand rapidly. You'll often see it paired with:

*   **Industries/Economies:** `burgeoning industry`, `burgeoning economy`, `burgeoning market`
*   **Populations/Cities:** `burgeoning population`, `burgeoning city`
*   **Abstract Concepts:** `burgeoning interest`, `burgeoning demand`, `burgeoning creativity`, `burgeoning opportunities`, `burgeoning reputation`, `burgeoning career`
*   **Problems (less common but possible):** `burgeoning problems`, `burgeoning concerns` (implies they are increasing rapidly)

### Example Sentences and Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples in various contexts:

1.  **Context: Business/Economy**
    *   **English:** The **burgeoning** tech startup quickly became a leader in its field, attracting massive investments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家蓬勃發展的科技新創公司迅速成為該領域的領導者，吸引了大量投資。
    *   *(Note: Here, "burgeoning" is an adjective describing the startup.)*

2.  **Context: Nature/Environment**
    *   **English:** After years of conservation efforts, the local wildlife population began to **burgeon** once again.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年的保育努力，當地野生動物的數量再次開始蓬勃增長。
    *   *(Note: Here, "burgeon" is a verb describing the population's growth.)*

3.  **Context: Abstract Concepts (Interest/Demand)**
    *   **English:** Student interest in renewable energy solutions has **burgeoned** over the past decade, leading to new academic programs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在過去十年中，學生對再生能源解決方案的興趣迅速增長，促成了新的學術課程。

4.  **Context: Social Issues/Problems (can be neutral or slightly negative)**
    *   **English:** Without proper oversight, the small local issues can quickly **burgeon** into widespread national crises.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 如果沒有適當的監管，這些地方性的小問題很快就會演變成蔓延全國的危機。

5.  **Context: Personal/Career Development**
    *   **English:** Her talent and tireless dedication allowed her career as a novelist to **burgeon** rapidly, earning her international acclaim.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的才華和不懈的奉獻精神使她的長篇小說家生涯迅速發展，贏得了國際讚譽。

6.  **Context: Urban Growth/Development**
    *   **English:** The once sleepy town has seen its population and infrastructure **burgeon** in response to the new factory.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 由於新工廠的建立，這個曾經沉寂的小鎮的人口和基礎設施都迅速擴展。

7.  **Context: Art/Creativity**
    *   **English:** The city's **burgeoning** art scene attracted artists from around the globe, transforming its cultural landscape.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這座城市蓬勃發展的藝術界吸引了來自全球的藝術家，改變了其文化面貌。

By understanding its core meaning of rapid, vigorous growth and paying attention to the common nouns it describes (especially in its adjective form "burgeoning"), you'll be well-prepared to encounter "burgeon" on the GRE!

## cajole

Okay, let's break down "cajole" for your GRE prep!

---

### **Word:** Cajole

*   **Part of Speech:** Verb
*   **Pronunciation:** /kəˈdʒoʊl/ (kuh-JOLE)

### **Meaning:**

To **cajole** means to persuade someone to do something by sustained flattery, coaxing, gentle urging, or promises. It often implies a certain amount of pleasant trickery or insincere sweet talk to overcome someone's reluctance or resistance. The goal is to charm or "talk someone into" a desired action, rather than using force or logical argument alone.

**Key nuances:**
*   It's a form of gentle persuasion, not coercion.
*   It often involves *insincere* compliments, deceptive promises, or persistent sweet-talking.
*   The person being cajoled is usually initially unwilling or hesitant.

**Synonyms:** coax, wheedle, entice, inveigle, persuade, sweet-talk, butter up, charm.
**Antonyms:** coerce, compel, force, strong-arm, demand.

---

### **Common Collocations:**

The most common collocation for "cajole" is with the preposition **"into"** followed by the action or state the person is persuaded to take.

*   **Cajole someone into (doing something):** This is the most frequent structure.
    *   *Examples:* cajole someone *into* agreeing, cajole someone *into* signing, cajole someone *into* coming, cajole someone *into* revealing.
*   **Cajole (someone) with (flattery/promises/sweet talk):** Describing the method used.
*   **Cajole (a reluctant/hesitant person):** Describing the typical target.

---

### **Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples showing "cajole" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Child/Parent (Classic gentle persuasion)**
    *   **English:** The child tried to **cajole** her parents into buying her a new toy by promising to be good for a whole week.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 孩子試圖透過承諾一整週表現良好來**哄騙**父母給她買新玩具。
        *   *(Hái zǐ shìtú tòuguò chéngnuò yī zhěng zhōu biǎoxiàn liánghǎo lái hōngpiàn fùmǔ gěi tā mǎi xīn wánjù.)*

2.  **Context: Professional/Business (Subtle manipulation)**
    *   **English:** He attempted to **cajole** his colleague into taking on the extra workload by complimenting his exceptional efficiency.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他試圖透過讚揚同事的卓越效率來**勸誘**他承擔額外的工作量。
        *   *(Tā shìtú tòuguò zànyáng tóngshì de zhuóyuè xiàolǜ lái quànyòu tā chéngdān éwài de gōngzuòliàng.)*

3.  **Context: Social/Personal (Overcoming reluctance)**
    *   **English:** It took a lot of effort to **cajole** my introverted friend into coming to the noisy party, as he preferred to stay home.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我花了很多力氣才**好言相勸**我內向的朋友來參加吵鬧的派對，因為他更喜歡待在家裡。
        *   *(Wǒ huāle hěnduō lìqì cái hǎoyán xiāngquàn wǒ nèixiàng de péngyǒu lái cānjiā chǎonào de pàiduì, yīnwèi tā gèng xǐhuān dài zàijiālǐ.)*

4.  **Context: Sales/Negotiation (Using offers to persuade)**
    *   **English:** The salesperson tried to **cajole** the hesitant customer into signing the contract with offers of a significant discount and an extended warranty.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 業務員試圖用大幅折扣和延長保固的誘惑，**說服**猶豫不決的顧客簽署合約。
        *   *(Yèwùyuán shìtú yòng dàfú zhékòu hé yáncháng bǎogù de yòuhuò, shuōfú yóuyù bùjué de gùkè qiānshǔ héyuē.)*

5.  **Context: Negative Connotation (Implying insincerity or deception)**
    *   **English:** Don't let him **cajole** you into making an unwise investment with his smooth talk and empty promises.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 別讓他用甜言蜜語和空頭承諾**哄騙**你做出不明智的投資。
        *   *(Bié ràng tā yòng tiányánmìyǔ hé kōngtóu chéngnuò hōngpiàn nǐ zuòchū bùmíngzhì de tóuzī.)*

6.  **Context: Figurative/Abstract (Less common, but shows depth)**
    *   **English:** You can't **cajole** nature into doing what you want; it follows its own immutable laws.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 你不能**勸誘**大自然照你的意願行事；它遵循自己不可改變的法則。
        *   *(Nǐ bùnéng quànyòu dàzìrán zhào nǐ de yìyuàn xíngshì; tā zūnxún zìjǐ bùkě gǎibiàn de fǎzé.)*

---

By understanding the subtle differences and various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "cajole" on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## sardonic

Okay, let's break down "sardonic" for your GRE prep! This is an excellent word to master as it frequently appears in contexts of tone, attitude, and character description.

---

### Meaning of "Sardonic"

**Sardonic** (adjective) describes a type of humor, wit, or expression that is **bitterly mocking, cynical, or scornfully humorous.** It implies a deep-seated contempt, disdain, or weariness, often masked by a dry, cutting wit or a sneering smile.

It's not just "funny"; it's a humor that intends to wound, expose flaws, or express a jaded outlook. There's often an underlying sense of hopelessness, disillusionment, or superiority.

**Key characteristics:**

*   **Mocking/Scornful:** It makes fun of something, but with a harsh, derisive edge.
*   **Cynical/Pessimistic:** It often reflects a belief that people are motivated by self-interest or that good things won't happen.
*   **Bitter/Jaded:** There's a sense of having been hurt or disappointed, leading to a sour outlook.
*   **Often subtle:** Unlike outright insults, sardonicism can be delivered with a straight face, a slight smirk, or a deceptively polite tone, making it even more biting.

**Etymology Note (helpful for memory!):** The word comes from the Greek "sardonios," referring to a poisonous plant from Sardinia (Sardinia herba) that was said to cause facial convulsions resembling a painful grin before death. This grim origin perfectly captures the bitter, twisted nature of a sardonic expression.

---

### Common Collocations for "Sardonic"

"Sardonic" frequently describes things related to expression, communication, and attitude:

*   **Sardonic smile/grin:** A smile that conveys contempt or bitter amusement.
*   **Sardonic humor/wit:** Humor that is sharp, cynical, and mocking.
*   **Sardonic remark/comment/observation:** A spoken or written statement that is cutting and scornful.
*   **Sardonic tone/voice:** A manner of speaking that implies a mocking or cynical attitude.
*   **Sardonic laugh:** A mirthless, scornful laugh.
*   **Sardonic expression:** A facial look conveying cynicism or disdain.
*   **Sardonic outlook/view:** A generally pessimistic or cynical perspective on life.
*   **Sardonic response:** An answer given with bitter sarcasm or mockery.

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples to illustrate "sardonic" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Character Description / General Demeanor**
    *   **English:** The old detective, known for his **sardonic wit**, always had a cutting remark ready for every cliché.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位以其**譏諷的智慧**聞名的老偵探，總是對每一個陳詞濫調準備好一句刻薄的評論。
    *   *(Note: Here, "sardonic wit" describes his general style of humor.)*

2.  **Context: Response to a Disappointing Situation**
    *   **English:** After the company announced another round of layoffs, her **sardonic laugh** filled the breakroom, a clear sign of her disillusionment.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 公司宣布又一輪裁員後，她**輕蔑的笑聲**充滿了休息室，清楚地表明了她的幻滅。
    *   *(Note: The laugh isn't joyful, but a bitter expression of disappointment.)*

3.  **Context: Critical Observation / Commentary**
    *   **English:** His **sardonic commentary** on modern politics spared no one, skewering both sides with equal disdain.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對現代政治的**尖酸刻薄的評論**不放過任何人，對雙方都毫不留情地予以鄙視。
    *   *(Note: The commentary is sharp, cynical, and shows contempt.)*

4.  **Context: Facial Expression / Body Language**
    *   **English:** She gave him a **sardonic smile** that conveyed her utter disbelief in his grand promises.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她給了他一個**譏笑的笑容**，表達了她對他宏偉承諾的全然不信。
    *   *(Note: The smile isn't friendly or genuine; it's tinged with mockery and doubt.)*

5.  **Context: A Character's Tone / Outlook**
    *   **English:** The protagonist's **sardonic outlook** on life made him a cynical but often compelling figure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 主角對生活的**冷嘲熱諷的態度**使他成為一個憤世嫉俗但往往引人注目的角色。
    *   *(Note: "Sardonic outlook" refers to a pervasive cynical view.)*

6.  **Context: A Specific Remark in Dialogue**
    *   **English:** "Of course, everything will be fine," he said with a **sardonic tone**, clearly implying the opposite.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 「當然，一切都會好起來的，」他用**挖苦的語氣**說道，顯然是話裡有話。
    *   *(Note: The tone makes the seemingly positive words convey a negative, mocking meaning.)*

---

By understanding these nuances and seeing the word in action, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "sardonic" on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## accretion

Let's break down the word "accretion" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Accretion**

**Meaning:**
*   **Definition:** The process of growth or increase by the gradual accumulation of additional layers or matter. It refers to the *act* or *process* of building something up slowly, piece by piece, or layer by layer.
*   **Core Idea:** Gradual accumulation, growth, or build-up.
*   **GRE Relevance:** Often appears in scientific contexts (astronomy, geology, biology), but can also be used in more abstract senses (e.g., accretion of knowledge, power, wealth). It implies a slow, progressive addition rather than a sudden, large increase.

**Common Collocations:**

*   **accretion of:** (most common)
    *   `accretion of wealth/capital/power/knowledge` (abstract concepts)
    *   `accretion of dust/sediment/ice` (physical matter)
*   **accretion disk:** (specific to astronomy)
*   **through accretion:** (describing the method of growth)
*   **gradual accretion:** (emphasizing the slow nature)
*   **land accretion:** (in geography, referring to land built up by natural forces)
*   **capital accretion:** (in finance, referring to the growth of capital)

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts & Traditional Chinese Translations**

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use:

**1. Scientific Context (Astronomy):**
*   **English:** Supermassive black holes grow by the **accretion of gas and dust** from their surroundings, often forming a glowing **accretion disk**.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 超大質量黑洞透過周圍氣體和塵埃的**吸積**而成長，通常會形成一個發光的**吸積盤**。
    *   *Note:* In astronomy, "accretion" is often translated as 吸積 (xījí).

**2. Scientific Context (Geology/Environment):**
*   **English:** The delta formed over centuries through the **accretion of sediment** carried by the river, constantly expanding into the sea.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該三角洲透過河流攜帶的泥沙**不斷堆積**，歷經數百年形成，並持續向海洋擴張。
    *   *Note:* Here, "accretion of sediment" is best translated as 泥沙不斷堆積 (níshā bùduàn duījí) or 泥沙的累積 (níshā de lěijī).

**3. Financial/Economic Context:**
*   **English:** Compound interest allows for the **gradual accretion of wealth** over time, as earnings generate further earnings.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 複利使得**財富能夠隨著時間逐漸累積**，因為收益會產生更多的收益。
    *   *Note:* 財富累積 (cáifù lěijī) is common for "accretion of wealth."

**4. Abstract/General Context (Knowledge/Skills):**
*   **English:** The **accretion of knowledge** is a lifelong process, occurring through continuous learning and experience.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** **知識的累積**是一個終身的過程，透過不斷學習和經驗積累而成。
    *   *Note:* 知識的累積 (zhīshi de lěijī) for "accretion of knowledge."

**5. Abstract/General Context (Power/Influence):**
*   **English:** Over his long career, the senator witnessed the **accretion of power** in the executive branch, leading to concerns about checks and balances.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 在他漫長的職業生涯中，這位參議員目睹了行政部門**權力的累積**，這引發了對權力制衡的擔憂。
    *   *Note:* 權力的累積 (quánlì de lěijī) for "accretion of power."

---

Understanding "accretion" as a *gradual, layer-by-layer build-up* will help you tackle various GRE questions, especially those related to scientific phenomena or processes of growth. Good luck with your studies!

## impartial

That's an excellent word to master for the GRE, as it frequently appears in both the Verbal Reasoning and Analytical Writing sections!

---

### Meaning of "Impartial"

**Definition:**
**Impartial** (adjective) means not favoring one person, side, or group more than another; treating all equally and fairly; unbiased; objective.

**Etymology Note:** The word comes from "im-" (meaning "not") and "partial" (meaning "favoring one part or side"). So, "impartial" literally means "not partial."

**Key Nuances:**
*   It implies a lack of prejudice or personal interest that might influence judgment.
*   It suggests a commitment to fairness and objectivity.

**Synonyms (Good for GRE):**
*   Unbiased
*   Neutral
*   Objective
*   Equitable
*   Even-handed
*   Dispassionate
*   Fair-minded

**Antonyms (Good for GRE):**
*   Biased
*   Prejudiced
*   Partial
*   Partisan
*   One-sided
*   Unfair
*   Discriminatory

---

### Common Collocations of "Impartial"

"Impartial" often describes people or entities whose role requires fairness, or the qualities/actions that demonstrate fairness.

1.  **Impartial + Person/Role:**
    *   **Impartial judge:** A judge who makes decisions without personal bias.
    *   **Impartial jury:** A jury that decides a case based only on evidence, not prejudice.
    *   **Impartial referee/umpire:** Someone who enforces rules fairly in a game.
    *   **Impartial observer:** Someone watching an event without taking sides.
    *   **Impartial arbitrator/mediator:** Someone who helps resolve disputes fairly.
    *   **Impartial journalist/reporter:** Someone who reports news objectively.

2.  **Impartial + Quality/Action/Concept:**
    *   **Impartial judgment/decision:** A decision made without bias.
    *   **Impartial review/analysis:** An examination free from personal opinions.
    *   **Impartial advice:** Advice given without favoring a particular outcome for the advisor.
    *   **Impartial treatment:** Treating everyone the same way.
    *   **Impartial investigation:** A probe conducted without preconceived notions.
    *   **Impartial reporting:** Presenting facts without bias.

3.  **Verb + Impartial:**
    *   **Remain impartial:** To stay unbiased.
    *   **Be impartial:** To act fairly.
    *   **Act impartially:** To behave in an unbiased manner (using the adverb "impartially").

---

### Example Sentences with Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples in various contexts:

1.  **Context: Legal System**
    *   **English:** The judge's primary duty is to remain **impartial** throughout the trial, considering only the evidence presented.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 法官的首要職責是在審判期間保持**公正**，只考慮所呈現的證據。
    *   *Note: Here, "公正" (gōngzhèng) is a perfect fit, meaning just/fair.*

2.  **Context: Sports/Competition**
    *   **English:** For a game to be fair, the referee must be completely **impartial**, making calls without favoritism.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了讓比賽公平，裁判必須完全**不偏不倚**，判決時不帶有偏袒。
    *   *Note: "不偏不倚" (bù piān bù yǐ) emphasizes not leaning to either side, highlighting neutrality.*

3.  **Context: Journalism/Media**
    *   **English:** **Impartial** reporting is crucial for a well-informed public and the health of a democracy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** **客觀**的報導對於資訊充分的公眾和民主的健全至關重要。
    *   *Note: "客觀" (kèguān) means objective, which is key in reporting.*

4.  **Context: Conflict Resolution**
    *   **English:** They appointed an **impartial** arbitrator to help resolve the dispute between the two companies.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們指定了一位**公正**的仲裁員來幫助解決兩家公司之間的爭議。
    *   *Note: "公正" (gōngzhèng) works well for someone mediating a dispute fairly.*

5.  **Context: Research/Analysis**
    *   **English:** Scientists strive for **impartial** data analysis, ensuring their conclusions are not influenced by pre-existing beliefs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 科學家努力進行**客觀**的數據分析，確保他們的結論不受預先存在的信念影響。
    *   *Note: "客觀" (kèguān) again fits the scientific context of objectivity.*

6.  **Context: Personal/Everyday**
    *   **English:** Parents should try to be **impartial** when dealing with arguments between their children.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 父母在處理孩子們之間的爭吵時應盡量保持**公正**。
    *   *Note: "公正" (gōngzhèng) is the most natural for fair treatment within a family.*

7.  **Context: Policy/Review**
    *   **English:** The committee called for an **impartial** review of the new policy to assess its effectiveness without bias.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 委員會要求對新政策進行一次**公正**的審查，以便在沒有偏見的情況下評估其有效性。
    *   *Note: "公正" (gōngzhèng) describes a fair and unbiased assessment.*

8.  **Context: Advice**
    *   **English:** When facing a difficult decision, it's often best to seek **impartial** advice from someone not personally involved.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當面臨困難的決定時，最好向沒有個人參與的人尋求**公正**的建議。
    *   *Note: "公正" (gōngzhèng) for unbiased advice.*

---

By understanding "impartial" through its definition, common collocations, and varied examples, you'll be well-equipped to tackle it on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## modicum

That's a great word for GRE prep! "Modicum" is a classic vocabulary word that often appears.

---

## Modicum

**Meaning:**

*   A **small amount** of something, especially something desirable or abstract.
*   It often implies an amount that is **barely sufficient** or **just enough**, highlighting a scarcity or lack. It suggests a minimum or trace amount.

**Etymology Note (for deeper understanding):**
The word comes from the Latin "modicus," meaning "moderate" or "little," which itself comes from "modus," meaning "measure." This connection reinforces the idea of a measured, small amount.

**Common Collocations:**

"Modicum" is almost always followed by the preposition **"of"** and then an **abstract noun**. You wouldn't typically use it for concrete, countable things (e.g., "a modicum of apples").

Here are its most common collocations:

*   **A modicum of common sense**
*   **A modicum of respect**
*   **A modicum of truth**
*   **A modicum of hope**
*   **A modicum of decency**
*   **A modicum of effort**
*   **A modicum of comfort**
*   **A modicum of justice**
*   **A modicum of success**
*   **A modicum of understanding**
*   **A modicum of knowledge**
*   **A modicum of skill**

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

1.  **Context: Decision-making/Judgment**
    *   **Sentence:** Even a **modicum of common sense** would have told him not to invest all his savings in that volatile stock.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 只要他有一點點常識，就知道不該把所有積蓄投資在那個波動的股票上。

2.  **Context: Professional/Academic Achievement**
    *   **Sentence:** The student, possessing only a **modicum of knowledge** in the subject, struggled to answer the professor's complex questions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 那位學生對這門科目只有些微的知識，因此很難回答教授的複雜問題。

3.  **Context: Emotional State/Resilience**
    *   **Sentence:** After weeks of despair, she clung to the **modicum of hope** that remained.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 經過數週的絕望後，她緊抓著那僅存的一點點希望。

4.  **Context: Fairness/Justice**
    *   **Sentence:** The victims felt that the legal process had offered them only a **modicum of justice** for their suffering.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 受害者覺得法律程序只帶給他們些許的公平正義，無法彌補他們的痛苦。

5.  **Context: Effort/Contribution**
    *   **Sentence:** If you want to achieve anything worthwhile, you must invest more than just a **modicum of effort**; consistent hard work is essential.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 如果你想要成就任何有價值的事情，就必須投入不只是一點點的努力；持之以恆的辛勤工作才是關鍵。

6.  **Context: Interpersonal Relations/Politeness**
    *   **Sentence:** Despite his rude behavior, he still expected a **modicum of respect** from his colleagues, which was largely unwarranted.
    *   **Traditional Chinese Translation:** 儘管他行為粗魯，卻仍期望同事能給予他些許的尊重，這點是完全沒道理的。

---

By understanding "modicum" as a "small, often insufficient amount of something abstract," and recognizing its common collocations, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## objective

Let's break down the word "objective" for your GRE studies. It's a versatile word that can function as both an adjective and a noun, with distinct but related meanings.

---

### **"Objective" as an Adjective**

**Meaning:**
When used as an adjective, "objective" means:
1.  **Not influenced by personal feelings, interpretations, or prejudice; based on facts; unbiased.** It describes something that is fair, impartial, and deals with reality as it is, rather than how one might wish it to be.
