2.  (Philosophical context) Existing independently of the individual mind or consciousness; real.

**Key Concept:** Think of "objective" as the opposite of "subjective."
*   **Objective:** Based on facts, reality, no personal bias.
*   **Subjective:** Based on personal feelings, opinions, interpretations.

**Common Collocations:**
*   **Objective analysis/report/assessment/evaluation/data/evidence/facts/criteria:** These are things that *should* be free from bias and based on reality.
*   **Remain objective/stay objective/be objective:** Actions one takes to maintain impartiality.
*   **Objective viewpoint/perspective:** A way of looking at things without personal bias.
*   **Objective truth:** A truth that exists regardless of what anyone thinks or believes.
*   **Objective reality:** The reality that exists independently of human thought.

---

### **"Objective" as a Noun**

**Meaning:**
When used as a noun, "objective" means:
1.  **A goal or aim; something one is trying to achieve.** It's a target or desired outcome.
2.  (Military context) A target or position to be reached or captured.

**Key Concept:** Think of "objective" as a synonym for "goal," "aim," or "purpose."

**Common Collocations:**
*   **Achieve an objective/reach an objective/meet an objective:** To successfully complete or attain a goal.
*   **Set an objective/establish an objective/define an objective:** To create or decide upon a goal.
*   **Primary/main/key/strategic/specific objective:** Different types or levels of goals.
*   **Business objectives/personal objectives/learning objectives:** Goals within specific contexts.
*   **Common objective:** A goal shared by multiple people or groups.

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translation)**

#### **"Objective" as an Adjective**

1.  **Context: Scientific/Research**
    *   **English:** Scientists strive to present **objective data**, free from personal bias, to ensure the validity of their research findings.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 科學家們力求呈現**客觀的數據**，不受個人偏見影響，以確保他們研究結果的有效性。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "objective data" means facts and figures that are not distorted by the researcher's personal opinions or expectations.

2.  **Context: Journalism/Reporting**
    *   **English:** It's crucial for a journalist to maintain an **objective perspective** when reporting on controversial issues.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 記者在報導有爭議的議題時，保持**客觀的視角**至關重要。
    *   *Explanation:* An "objective perspective" means the journalist presents information fairly, without taking sides or letting their personal feelings influence the narrative.

3.  **Context: Evaluation/Assessment**
    *   **English:** The manager offered an **objective assessment** of the employee's performance, focusing on measurable results rather than personal feelings.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經理對員工的績效提供了**客觀的評估**，專注於可衡量的結果而非個人感受。
    *   *Explanation:* An "objective assessment" is one based on facts (like sales figures or project completion rates) rather than subjective likes or dislikes.

#### **"Objective" as a Noun**

1.  **Context: Business/Project Management**
    *   **English:** The primary **objective** of the new marketing campaign is to increase brand awareness by 20% within six months.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這項新行銷活動的首要**目標**是在六個月內將品牌知名度提升20%。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "objective" refers to the specific, measurable goal the marketing team aims to achieve.

2.  **Context: Education/Training**
    *   **English:** Before starting the training, make sure you understand the learning **objectives** for each module.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在開始培訓之前，請確保您了解每個模塊的學習**目標**。
    *   *Explanation:* Learning "objectives" are the specific skills or knowledge trainees are expected to acquire by the end of a module.

3.  **Context: Teamwork/Collective Effort**
    *   **English:** Despite several setbacks, the team remained focused on achieving their common **objective**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管遭遇數次挫折，團隊仍專注於達成他們的共同**目標**。
    *   *Explanation:* A "common objective" is the shared goal that all members of the team are working towards.

---

Understanding "objective" in both its adjective and noun forms, along with its common collocations, will be very beneficial for your GRE vocabulary and reading comprehension sections. Pay attention to the context to determine which meaning is intended!

## alchemy

Let's break down the meaning of "alchemy," explore its common collocations, and provide example sentences with Traditional Chinese translations, keeping the GRE context in mind.

---

## Understanding "Alchemy" for the GRE

**Meaning of Alchemy:**

"Alchemy" has two primary meanings: a historical, literal one and a more common, metaphorical one in modern English. Both are relevant for the GRE as you might encounter it in various passages or even in a sentence completion context.

1.  **Historical/Literal Meaning:**
    *   Alchemy refers to a medieval philosophical tradition and a precursor to modern chemistry. Its main goals were:
        *   **Transmutation of Base Metals:** To transform "base" (common, inexpensive) metals like lead into "noble" (precious) metals like gold or silver. This was often called the "Great Work."
        *   **Elixir of Life:** To discover an "elixir of life" or "philosopher's stone" that would grant immortality or universal cure for diseases.
    *   It combined elements of chemistry, metallurgy, physics, medicine, astrology, spiritualism, and art. While not a "true" science by modern standards, it laid some groundwork for scientific inquiry and experimentation.

2.  **Figurative/Metaphorical Meaning (More Common in Modern English):**
    *   This is the meaning you'll most frequently encounter. "Alchemy" is used to describe:
        *   **A mysterious or magical process of transformation:** Especially one that takes something common, disparate, or unpromising and transforms it into something rare, valuable, or extraordinary.
        *   **A complex, often subtle, blending or combination:** The successful fusion of diverse elements to create a new, superior whole.
        *   **Any profound or transformative change, often for the better, that seems almost magical or inexplicable.**

**Key Idea to Remember:** The core concept across both meanings is **transformation** – taking something and changing it into something fundamentally different and usually *better* or *more valuable*.

---

## Common Collocations of "Alchemy"

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these can help with vocabulary, reading comprehension, and even writing.

*   **The alchemy of...**: This is the most common figurative use, indicating the transformative process of a particular thing (e.g., *the alchemy of love*, *the alchemy of innovation*, *the alchemy of nature*).
*   **Practice/pursue alchemy**: (Primarily literal/historical, but can be used metaphorically for a complex endeavor).
*   **Alchemical process/transformation**: (Referring to the act of transformation, literal or figurative).
*   **Culinary alchemy**: (Figurative, for highly skilled cooking that transforms simple ingredients).
*   **Political/social/financial alchemy**: (Figurative, for complex transformations in these fields).
*   **True alchemy / Pure alchemy**: (Emphasizing the profound or genuine nature of the transformation).
*   **Like alchemy**: (Meaning something *resembles* a magical transformation).

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences illustrating both literal and figurative uses of "alchemy," with Traditional Chinese translations.

1.  **Context: Historical/Literal (referring to the ancient practice)**
    *   **English:** Medieval alchemists tirelessly pursued the elusive goal of turning base metals into gold, a quest known as the "Great Work of alchemy."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 中世紀的煉金術士孜孜不倦地追求將賤金屬變成黃金的難以捉摸的目標，這項探索被稱為「煉金術的偉大功業」。
    *   *(Explanation: This sentence uses "alchemy" in its original, literal sense, highlighting the historical pursuit.)*

2.  **Context: Business/Innovation (figurative transformation of ideas/elements)**
    *   **English:** The CEO described their groundbreaking product as an "alchemy of innovative ideas, diverse talents, and relentless dedication," transforming a simple concept into a market leader.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長將他們劃時代的產品描述為「創新思想、多元人才和不懈奉獻的奇妙結合」，將一個簡單的概念轉化為市場領先者。
    *   *(Explanation: Here, "alchemy" refers to the magical or skillful combination of different elements (ideas, talents, dedication) to produce something highly valuable (a market-leading product).)*

3.  **Context: Personal Growth/Art (figurative transformation of experience)**
    *   **English:** Through the alchemy of suffering and reflection, the artist transformed his personal pain into a series of deeply moving sculptures.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 透過苦難和反思的奇妙轉化，藝術家將他的個人痛苦轉化為一系列深刻動人的雕塑。
    *   *(Explanation: This uses "alchemy" metaphorically for the profound psychological and creative process of turning negative experiences into something beautiful and meaningful.)*

4.  **Context: Social/Political Dynamics (figurative transformation through blending)**
    *   **English:** The diplomat's skill lay in the alchemy of compromise, deftly blending competing interests into a workable peace treaty that seemed impossible just weeks before.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位外交官的技巧在於懂得妥協的藝術，巧妙地將相互競爭的利益融合，促成了一項數週前看似不可能實現的、可行的和平條約。
    *   *(Explanation: "Alchemy" here signifies the almost magical ability to harmonize disparate or opposing elements (competing interests) into a cohesive and functional outcome (a peace treaty).)*

5.  **Context: Culinary Arts (figurative magical transformation in cooking)**
    *   **English:** In the kitchen, a true chef practices a form of culinary alchemy, transforming simple ingredients like flour, water, and yeast into a fragrant, crusty loaf of bread.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在廚房裡，一位真正的廚師會施展一種烹飪上的點金術，將麵粉、水和酵母等簡單食材轉化為香氣四溢、外脆內軟的麵包。
    *   *(Explanation: This example highlights the almost magical skill involved in cooking, where basic components are combined and changed through a process into something far more appealing and complex.)*

---

By understanding both the historical and figurative meanings, and recognizing common collocations, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "alchemy" on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## nonplussed

It's great you're focusing on words like "nonplussed" for the GRE, as it's a classic example of a word often misunderstood!

Let's break it down:

---

## **Nonplussed**

**Meaning:**

*   **Definition:** So surprised and confused that one does not know how to act or what to say; perplexed; bewildered; at a loss.
*   **Key Nuance:** The core meaning is one of **confusion leading to inaction or speechlessness**. It implies being "stuck" or unable to proceed due to being overwhelmed by something unexpected or puzzling.

*   **Common Misconception (CRUCIAL for GRE!):** Many people mistakenly use "nonplussed" to mean "unfazed," "unconcerned," or "not bothered." This is **incorrect**. If you see it used this way on the GRE, it's likely a trap. Always remember it means *perplexed and unable to react*. The "non-" comes from the Latin "non plus," meaning "no more," as in "no more can be done/said."

**Common Collocation:**

The most common way "nonplussed" is used is as a predicate adjective following a form of "to be," or a verb that implies a state of being or feeling. A common collocation that describes an action leading to this state is:

*   **"left [someone] nonplussed"**

    *   *Example:* "The sudden accusation **left him nonplussed**."
    *   *Example:* "Her eccentric behavior **left the audience nonplussed**."

You'll also frequently see it with verbs like:
*   **"looked nonplussed"**
*   **"seemed nonplussed"**
*   **"was/were nonplussed"** (most common)

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Unexpected Question/Information**
    *   **English:** When the professor asked an incredibly obscure question, the student was left utterly **nonplussed**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當教授提出一個極其冷僻的問題時，這位學生徹底地**不知所措**。
    *   *(Note: 不知所措 (bù zhī suǒ cuò) directly translates to "at a loss what to do/say," perfectly capturing the meaning.)*

2.  **Context: Startling Event**
    *   **English:** The sudden power outage left everyone in the theater **nonplussed**, unsure whether to stay or leave.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 突如其來的停電讓劇院裡的所有人都感到**困惑不解**，不知道是該留下還是離開。
    *   *(Note: 困惑不解 (kùn huò bù jiě) means "perplexed and unable to understand," suitable here.)*

3.  **Context: Unforeseen Behavior/Confrontation**
    *   **English:** The politician was clearly **nonplussed** by the unexpected heckling from the audience.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位政治家顯然被觀眾意想不到的起鬨弄得**啞口無言**。
    *   *(Note: 啞口無言 (yǎ kǒu wú yán) means "speechless" or "dumbfounded," which is a common outcome of being nonplussed in such a situation.)*

4.  **Context: Confusing Instructions/Situation**
    *   **English:** Even the experienced technician was **nonplussed** by the convoluted instructions for assembling the new machine.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使是經驗豐富的技術人員，也被這台新機器的複雜組裝說明搞得**不知所措**。
    *   *(Note: Again, 不知所措 works well for the inability to proceed due to confusion.)*

5.  **Context: Personal Reaction to an Absurdity**
    *   **English:** I was completely **nonplussed** when my usually quiet neighbor started singing opera at the top of his lungs in the middle of the night.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當我平常安靜的鄰居半夜突然高聲唱起歌劇時，我完全**目瞪口呆**。
    *   *(Note: 目瞪口呆 (mù dèng kǒu dāi) means "dumbfounded" or "agape with shock," conveying the surprise and inability to react.)*

---

Keep practicing these nuanced words, and you'll be well-prepared for the GRE!

## alacrity

Okay, let's break down "alacrity" for your GRE prep!

---

## Alacrity

**Meaning:**
**Alacrity** (noun) means **brisk and cheerful readiness, eagerness, or willingness.** It implies not just speed or quickness, but also a positive, enthusiastic, and spirited attitude towards an action. When someone acts with alacrity, they do so promptly and happily, without hesitation or reluctance.

**Key Components:**
*   **Speed/Promptness:** Doing something quickly.
*   **Eagerness/Willingness:** Doing something happily and readily.
*   **Enthusiasm/Cheerfulness:** Doing something with a positive spirit.

**Antonyms (for GRE context):** Reluctance, hesitation, apathy, sluggishness, disinclination.

---

**Common Collocation:**

The most common and natural collocation for "alacrity" is **"with alacrity."**
*   *Example:* He responded **with alacrity.**

Other verbs that often precede or describe it include:
*   **Show/Display alacrity:** *She displayed great alacrity.*
*   **Accept/Respond with alacrity:** *They accepted the challenge with alacrity.*
*   **Perform/Tackle with alacrity:** *He tackled the task with impressive alacrity.*

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples to illustrate its use in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Professional/Work Context (Responding to a task):**
    *   **English:** When the manager asked for volunteers for the challenging new project, Sarah raised her hand **with alacrity**, eager to prove herself.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當經理為具挑戰性的新專案徵求志願者時，莎拉欣然舉手，渴望證明自己的能力。

2.  **Service/Customer Relations Context (Prompt service):**
    *   **English:** The hotel staff greeted the new guests **with alacrity**, ensuring their check-in process was seamless and welcoming.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 飯店員工熱情地迎接新客人，確保他們的入住流程順暢而愉快。

3.  **Academic/Learning Context (Engagement with new material):**
    *   **English:** Despite the complexity of the new concept, the students tackled the assignment **with impressive alacrity**, demonstrating their genuine desire to learn.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管新概念很複雜，學生們仍以令人印象深刻的熱忱完成了作業，展現了他們真誠的求知慾。

4.  **Social/Personal Context (Helping others):**
    *   **English:** When asked to help move furniture, John, known for his generosity, agreed **with alacrity**, arriving even before the scheduled time.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當被要求幫忙搬家具時，以慷慨著稱的約翰欣然同意，甚至比預定時間更早抵達。

5.  **Problem-Solving/Crisis Context (Quick and willing action):**
    *   **English:** The IT support team responded to the urgent server issue **with remarkable alacrity**, resolving the problem within minutes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** IT支援團隊以驚人的敏捷性回應了緊急的伺服器問題，在幾分鐘內解決了故障。

6.  **General Acceptance/Agreement Context (Enthusiastic consent):**
    *   **English:** She accepted the invitation to the charity gala **with alacrity**, excited to contribute to such a noble cause.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她欣然接受了慈善晚會的邀請，很高興能為如此崇高的事業貢獻一份心力。

---

By understanding "alacrity" as "prompt and cheerful eagerness," you'll be well-prepared for any GRE questions involving this word! Good luck with your studies!

## edify

Okay, let's break down the word "edify" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Edify"

**Meaning:**
To **edify** means to instruct or improve (someone) morally or intellectually. It implies building up, uplifting, or enlightening a person's character, understanding, or spirit. It's not just about teaching facts, but about fostering wisdom, virtue, or personal growth.

**Etymology Note (helpful for memory):** The word "edify" comes from the Latin "aedificare," which means "to build" (specifically a house or building). So, think of "edifying" as "building up" a person's mind or character.

**Common Synonyms:**
*   Enlighten
*   Instruct
*   Teach
*   Educate
*   Uplift
*   Improve
*   Cultivate
*   Nurture

---

### Common Collocations for "Edify"

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these helps with both understanding and using the word correctly.

**1. As a Verb (to edify *someone/something*):**
*   **Edify the mind/soul/spirit:** (to improve one's intellectual or spiritual well-being)
*   **Edify the audience/readers/congregation:** (to uplift or instruct a group)
*   **Seek to edify:** (to attempt to improve someone)
*   **Designed to edify:** (intended to instruct or uplift)

**2. As an Adjective (edifying *something*):**
This is very common on the GRE! "Edifying" means "instructive or morally improving."
*   **Edifying experience:** (an experience that teaches or improves you)
*   **Edifying discussion/conversation:** (a conversation that is intellectually or morally enriching)
*   **Edifying book/literature/text:** (a piece of writing that is instructive or uplifting)
*   **Edifying lecture/sermon:** (a talk that is insightful or morally guiding)
*   **Edifying content:** (material that is beneficial for personal growth)

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "edify" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

**1. General Intellectual/Moral Improvement:**

*   **English:** The classic novel, with its profound insights into human nature, truly serves to **edify** its readers.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這部經典小說，以其對人性的深刻洞察，確實能**啟迪**讀者心靈。
    *   *(Note: 啟迪 (qǐdí) means to enlighten or inspire, fitting for intellectual and moral uplift.)*

**2. Education/Learning:**

*   **English:** A truly liberal education should not just impart knowledge but also **edify** the mind and spirit.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 真正的人文教育不應僅傳授知識，更應**陶冶**心智和精神。
    *   *(Note: 陶冶 (táoyě) means to cultivate or mold character, often through learning or influence.)*

**3. Public Discourse/Media:**

*   **English:** Many critics argue that reality television, while entertaining, does little to **edify** its viewers.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多評論家認為，真人實境秀雖然有娛樂性，但對觀眾的**教化**作用甚微。
    *   *(Note: 教化 (jiàohuà) means to enlighten and transform, often in a broader societal or moral sense.)*

**4. Religious/Spiritual Context:**

*   **English:** The pastor's sermon aimed to **edify** the congregation, encouraging them to reflect on their moral responsibilities.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 牧師的佈道旨在**教化**會眾，鼓勵他們反思自身的道德責任。
    *   *(Note: 教化 (jiàohuà) is again suitable here for the moral instruction provided by a sermon.)*

**5. As an Adjective ("edifying"):**

*   **English:** Engaging in thoughtful discussions with diverse perspectives can be a genuinely **edifying** experience.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 參與與不同觀點的深入討論，可能是一次真正**有益的**（或**啟迪心智的**）體驗。
    *   *(Note: 有益的 (yǒuyì de) means beneficial/helpful; 啟迪心智的 (qǐdí xīzhì de) specifically means enlightening to the mind.)*

*   **English:** She prefers to read **edifying** literature that challenges her thinking rather than purely escapist fiction.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她喜歡閱讀能夠挑戰思維的**有教益的**文學作品，而非純粹的消遣小說。
    *   *(Note: 有教益的 (yǒu jiàoyì de) means instructive or beneficial for learning.)*

---

Keep practicing these words in context, and you'll master them for the GRE! Good luck!

## constrain

Let's break down the word "constrain" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Meaning of "Constrain"

**Constrain** (verb) primarily means to **restrict, limit, or hold back** something or someone, often by force or necessity. It implies that something is **prevented from acting freely or developing fully**.

Think of it as putting a *constraint* (the noun form) on something.

Here are the key nuances:

1.  **To restrict or limit:** This is the most common usage. Something is confined within certain boundaries or parameters.
    *   *Example:* Budgetary issues *constrained* the company's growth.
2.  **To compel or force:** To make someone do something by force or strong influence. This usage is less common in everyday speech but appears in formal contexts.
    *   *Example:* He felt *constrained* to apologize, even though he didn't want to.
3.  **To hold back or suppress:** To prevent the free development or expression of something.
    *   *Example:* Social norms often *constrain* individual creativity.

**Synonyms:** restrict, limit, confine, curb, hinder, impede, compel, force, oblige.

**Antonyms:** free, liberate, allow, enable, facilitate, encourage.

---

### Common Collocations for "Constrain"

"Constrain" often appears with nouns that refer to:

*   **Resources:** *constrain* **budget**, *constrain* **funds**, *constrain* **resources**, *constrain* **time**, *constrain* **space**
*   **Freedom/Movement:** *constrain* **freedom**, *constrain* **movement**, *constrain* **choices**, *constrain* **options**
*   **Development/Growth:** *constrain* **growth**, *constrain* **development**, *constrain* **progress**, *constrain* **expansion**
*   **Behavior/Action:** *constrain* **actions**, *constrain* **behavior**, *constrain* **expression**, *constrain* **creativity**
*   **Often used with prepositions:**
    *   **constrained by:** e.g., *constrained by* regulations, *constrained by* lack of capital
    *   **constrained to:** e.g., *constrained to* stay silent, *constrained to* accept

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "constrain" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Financial/Business Limits**
    *   **English:** Limited funds **constrained** the company's expansion plans, forcing them to prioritize essential projects.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 有限的資金**制約**了公司的擴張計畫，迫使他們優先處理核心專案。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "constrain" means to *limit* or *restrict* due to a lack of resources.

2.  **Context: Physical/Environmental Limits**
    *   **English:** The narrow mountain pass **constrained** the river's flow, creating a series of rapids downstream.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 狹窄的山道**限制**了河流的流動，在下游形成了一系列急流。
    *   *Explanation:* "Constrain" refers to a physical barrier *limiting* or *confining* movement.

3.  **Context: Social/Behavioral Norms**
    *   **English:** Social expectations often **constrain** individuals from expressing their true feelings, leading to emotional suppression.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 社會期望常會**約束**個人表達真實感受，導致情感壓抑。
    *   *Explanation:* This usage implies that social norms *hold back* or *suppress* certain behaviors or expressions.

4.  **Context: Legal/Regulatory Restrictions**
    *   **English:** New government regulations will **constrain** how much pollution factories can emit, aiming to improve air quality.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新的政府法規將會**限制**工廠可以排放的汙染量，旨在改善空氣品質。
    *   *Explanation:* "Constrain" here means to *set legal limits* or *restrictions* on an action.

5.  **Context: Personal/Psychological Barriers**
    *   **English:** Fear of failure **constrained** her from taking risks and pursuing her entrepreneurial dreams.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對失敗的恐懼**束縛**了她，使她不敢冒險追求創業夢想。
    *   *Explanation:* In this context, "constrain" means to *hold back* or *hinder* someone due to an internal psychological factor.

6.  **Context: Compulsion/Necessity (less common but important for GRE)**
    *   **English:** He felt **constrained** to agree with the majority opinion, despite his personal reservations, to maintain harmony.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他個人有所保留，但他感到**不得不**同意多數人的意見，以維持和諧。
    *   *Explanation:* This usage signifies being *compelled* or *forced by circumstances* (or a strong internal sense of obligation) to do something. The Traditional Chinese "不得不" (have no choice but to) captures this well.

By understanding these nuances and seeing "constrain" in various contexts, you'll be better prepared to encounter it on the GRE, whether in vocabulary questions, reading comprehension, or sentence equivalence. Good luck with your studies!

## consonant

Okay, let's break down the word "consonant" for your GRE preparation. It has two main meanings, and one is far more common in the context of advanced vocabulary tests like the GRE.

---

### Understanding "Consonant"

**1. Primary Meaning (Linguistics/Phonetics):**

*   **Definition:** A speech sound that is produced by an obstruction of the breath in the vocal tract. In other words, the air flow from your lungs is partially or completely blocked as you make the sound.
*   **Examples:** The sounds of letters like B, C, D, F, G, H, J, K, L, M, N, P, Q, R, S, T, V, W, X, Y (sometimes), Z are consonants.
*   **Opposite:** Vowel (e.g., A, E, I, O, U).

**2. Secondary Meaning (Abstract/Figurative - **Highly Relevant for GRE**):**

*   **Definition:** In agreement or harmony with; consistent with; in accordance with. When something is "consonant with" something else, it fits well, doesn't contradict, and supports it.
*   **Etymology Hint:** The word comes from Latin *consonans*, meaning "sounding together," which gives a clue to its sense of harmony or agreement.
*   **Key Usage:** This meaning is almost always followed by the preposition **"with."**

---

### Common Collocations (for the Abstract Meaning)

The abstract meaning of "consonant" most commonly collocates with words describing principles, rules, values, or facts.

*   **Consonant with** principles
*   **Consonant with** values
*   **Consonant with** beliefs
*   **Consonant with** goals
*   **Consonant with** expectations
*   **Consonant with** evidence
*   **Consonant with** facts
*   **Consonant with** policy
*   **Consonant with** law
*   **Consonant with** the truth
*   **Consonant with** a strategy

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences illustrating both meanings, with an emphasis on the abstract sense, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

**Context 1: Business/Strategy (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** The company's new sustainability initiatives are entirely **consonant with** its long-term goals of ethical production and environmental responsibility.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家公司新的永續發展措施，與其在道德生產和環境責任方面的長期目標完全**吻合**。

**Context 2: Ethics/Personal Conduct (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** His actions were clearly not **consonant with** the values of integrity and honesty that he claimed to uphold.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 他的行為顯然與他聲稱堅持的正直和誠實價值觀**不符**。

**Context 3: Legal/Evidence (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** The new evidence presented by the defense was surprisingly **consonant with** the witness's original testimony, strengthening their case.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 辯方提出的新證據出乎意料地與證人原先的證詞**相符**，強化了他們的論點。

**Context 4: Design/Aesthetics (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** The architect ensured the modern extension was **consonant with** the historical character and existing style of the original building.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 建築師確保了現代擴建部分與原有建築的歷史特色和現有風格**保持協調一致**。

**Context 5: General Principles/Abstract Ideas (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** True leadership should always be **consonant with** the welfare and best interests of the people it serves.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 真正的領導力應始終與其所服務人民的福祉和最大利益**保持一致**。

**Context 6: Academic/Research (Abstract Meaning)**
*   **English:** The latest findings from the study are highly **consonant with** previous research in the field, suggesting a consistent pattern.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這項研究的最新發現與該領域先前的研究**高度一致**，顯示出一致的模式。

**Context 7: Linguistics/Grammar (Phonetic Meaning)**
*   **English:** In English, a syllable usually contains at least one vowel, and it may be surrounded by **consonants**.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 在英語中，一個音節通常至少包含一個母音，且可能由**子音**環繞。

**Context 8: Basic Education (Phonetic Meaning)**
*   **English:** Children first learn to distinguish between vowels and **consonants** when they begin reading.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 孩子們在開始學習閱讀時，首先學習區分母音和**子音**。

---

**Key Takeaway for GRE:** While "consonant" has a phonetic meaning, for vocabulary questions on the GRE, you should primarily focus on its abstract meaning: **"in agreement or harmony with; consistent with."** Always remember its common usage with **"with."**

## lax

Let's break down the word "lax" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Meaning of "Lax"

**Lax** (adjective) means:

1.  **Not sufficiently strict, severe, or careful.** It implies a lack of proper firmness, control, or diligence.
2.  **Careless or negligent.**
3.  **Loose or relaxed, not tense.** (This is a less common meaning in the context of the GRE, which usually focuses on the first definition).

In essence, if something is "lax," it's probably *too easy-going* or *not rigorous enough*, often leading to problems or a failure to meet standards. It carries a negative connotation.

**Think of it as the opposite of:** strict, rigorous, stringent, firm, diligent, meticulous.

---

### Common Collocations of "Lax"

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these will help you understand "lax" in context and use it naturally.

*   **Lax security:** Not strong or thorough enough security measures.
*   **Lax discipline:** Not enough control or enforcement of rules, especially in a school or organization.
*   **Lax standards:** Quality or performance requirements that are not strict enough.
*   **Lax enforcement:** Rules or laws that are not applied or followed strictly.
*   **Lax about (something):** Careless or not strict concerning a particular thing (e.g., *lax about deadlines*).
*   **Lax policies/regulations:** Rules that are too lenient or not well-defined.
*   **Lax oversight:** Insufficient supervision or monitoring.
*   **Lax moral/ethical standards:** Moral or ethical principles that are not strictly adhered to.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "lax" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Security**
    *   **English:** The company's **lax security** measures made it easy for hackers to access sensitive data.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該公司**安全措施鬆懈**，使得駭客輕易就能存取敏感資料。

2.  **Context: Discipline (e.g., school, parenting)**
    *   **English:** Some critics argue that the school's **lax discipline** has led to an increase in student misconduct.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一些評論家認為，該學校**紀律鬆散**，導致學生不當行為增加。

3.  **Context: Enforcement of rules/laws**
    *   **English:** Environmental groups criticized the government for its **lax enforcement** of pollution laws.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 環保團體批評政府對污染法律的**執行鬆懈**。

4.  **Context: Quality/Performance Standards**
    *   **English:** Consumers complained about the product's poor quality, attributing it to the manufacturer's **lax quality control standards**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 消費者抱怨產品品質不佳，將其歸因於製造商**寬鬆的品質管制標準**。

5.  **Context: Personal Attitude/Behavior**
    *   **English:** He was often criticized for being **lax about** meeting deadlines, which affected team productivity.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他常因**對截止日期不夠嚴謹**而受到批評，這影響了團隊的生產力。

6.  **Context: Morality/Ethics**
    *   **English:** The article suggested that the decline in public trust was partly due to the **lax ethical standards** within the financial industry.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該文章指出，公眾信任度下降的部分原因是金融業內**寬鬆的道德標準**。

---

By understanding these examples and collocations, you'll be well-prepared to encounter "lax" in your GRE readings and questions!

## tantamount

Let's break down "tantamount" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Meaning of "Tantamount"**

**Tantamount** (adjective) means **equivalent to** or **virtually the same as** something else, especially something undesirable or serious. It implies that while something might not be literally X, its effect, implication, or nature is so similar to X that it can be considered the same.

*   **Key Idea:** It's often used when an action, decision, or situation has consequences or implications that are as severe or significant as another, more explicitly defined, negative event.
*   **Origin:** From Old French "tant amont" meaning "to so much, as much."

### **Common Collocation**

The most common and almost exclusive collocation for "tantamount" is:

*   **tantamount to**

It is almost always followed by the preposition "to," which then precedes a noun, a gerund (-ing form of a verb acting as a noun), or a noun phrase.

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are multiple examples showcasing "tantamount" in different situations, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Legal / Ethical Implication**
    *   **English:** For a politician, accepting donations from a lobbying group with a clear conflict of interest could be **tantamount to** corruption.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對一位政治人物來說，接受與其有明顯利益衝突的遊說團體的捐款，可能**等同於**貪污。
    *   *Explanation:* The act of accepting donations isn't *literally* called corruption, but its implications and ethical violations are so severe that it's considered the same.

2.  **Context: Interpersonal / Communication**
    *   **English:** Her silence when asked about the missing funds was **tantamount to** an admission that she knew something.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當被問及遺失的資金時，她的沉默**無異於**承認她知情。
    *   *Explanation:* While she didn't confess verbally, her lack of response had the same effect as a confession.

3.  **Context: Policy / Social Impact**
    *   **English:** Cutting funding for essential public services would be **tantamount to** abandoning the most vulnerable members of society.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 削減基本公共服務的資金，將**形同**於拋棄社會上最弱勢的成員。
    *   *Explanation:* The policy decision (cutting funds) is presented as having the same negative impact as actively deserting people.

4.  **Context: Academic / Artistic Criticism**
    *   **English:** In some artistic circles, creating derivative work is **tantamount to** artistic plagiarism, even if not legally so.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在某些藝術圈子裡，創作衍生作品**幾乎等於**藝術剽竊，即便在法律上不構成剽竊。
    *   *Explanation:* The act might not meet the legal definition of plagiarism, but artistically, it's considered just as serious.

5.  **Context: Professional / Business**
    *   **English:** Missing that critical deadline without a valid reason was **tantamount to** professional negligence and cost him the promotion.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在沒有正當理由的情況下錯過那個關鍵期限，**無異於**專業上的疏忽，並讓他失去了晉升的機會。
    *   *Explanation:* The missed deadline, though not explicitly an act of negligence, had the same severe professional consequences.

---

**GRE Tip:** When you see "tantamount," think about the strong equivalence being drawn between two things, often highlighting a serious or negative implication of the first thing by comparing it to the second. It's a word that adds gravitas and emphasizes the weight of an action or situation.

## mollify

Let's break down the word "mollify" for your GRE prep!

---

### **Meaning of "Mollify"**

**Mollify** (verb) means to **appease the anger or anxiety of someone**, to **calm someone down**, or to **reduce the severity or intensity of something**.

Think of it as making something "soft" or "less harsh." The word comes from the Latin *mollis* (soft) and *facere* (to make).

**Key idea:** It's about easing negative emotions or situations.

**Synonyms:** appease, placate, pacify, assuage, conciliate, soothe, calm.
**Antonyms:** provoke, inflame, aggravate, incense, irritate.

---

### **Common Collocations for "Mollify"**

Collocations are words that often go together. Understanding them helps you use the word naturally and effectively.

*   **Mollify someone/something:**
    *   **Mollify the critics/the public/the angry customer/the disgruntled employees.** (You are calming down a group or person).
    *   **Mollify fears/concerns/anger/resentment/frustration.** (You are easing specific negative emotions or states).
*   **Attempts to mollify:**
    *   **An attempt to mollify.**
    *   **Efforts to mollify.**
*   **What mollifies:**
    *   **A gesture to mollify.**
    *   **Concessions to mollify.**
    *   **An apology to mollify.**
    *   **Words to mollify.**
    *   **A bonus to mollify.**
*   **How something is mollified:**
    *   **Successfully mollify.**
    *   **Easily mollify.**
    *   **Difficult to mollify.**

---

### **Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are example sentences demonstrating "mollify" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Personal/Interpersonal Conflict**
    *   **English:** He tried to **mollify** his girlfriend after their argument by bringing her flowers and writing a heartfelt apology.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在爭吵後試圖送花並寫一封真誠的道歉信來**安撫**女友。
    *   *Explanation:* "安撫" (ānfǔ) means to appease or soothe someone.

2.  **Context: Customer Service/Public Relations**
    *   **English:** The airline offered a generous travel voucher to **mollify** the passengers who were stranded overnight due to the flight cancellation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 航空公司提供了一張優厚的旅行代金券，以**平息**因航班取消而滯留過夜的乘客的怒氣。
    *   *Explanation:* "平息" (píngxī) means to quell or pacify, often used for anger or disturbances.

3.  **Context: Political/Social Issues**
    *   **English:** The government made several last-minute policy changes in an attempt to **mollify** the protesting citizens and avoid further unrest.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府在最後一刻做出數項政策調整，試圖**緩和**抗議民眾的怒氣，以避免進一步的動亂。
    *   *Explanation:* "緩和" (huǎnhé) means to ease, moderate, or alleviate, fitting for tensions or anger.

4.  **Context: Business/Employee Relations**
    *   **English:** Management announced a new bonus program hoping it would **mollify** the disgruntled employees who had been demanding better pay.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 管理層宣布了一項新的獎金計劃，希望能**平息**那些一直要求加薪的不滿員工的怨氣。
    *   *Explanation:* "平息" is again suitable for quelling grievances or discontent.

5.  **Context: Less Direct Anger (More General Distress/Concerns)**
    *   **English:** A warm cup of tea and a comforting presence can often **mollify** someone's anxieties after a stressful day.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一杯熱茶和一個令人感到慰藉的存在，通常可以在壓力重重的一天後**緩解**某人的焦慮。
    *   *Explanation:* "緩解" (huǎnjiě) means to relieve or alleviate, perfect for anxieties or fears.

6.  **Context: Media/Critics**
    *   **English:** The celebrity issued a public statement and apologized for his controversial remarks, hoping to **mollify** the furious critics.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位名人發表公開聲明，並為他具爭議性的言論道歉，希望能**安撫**憤怒的評論家。
    *   *Explanation:* "安撫" works well for appeasing upset individuals or groups.

---

By studying these examples and their translations, you'll not only grasp the meaning of "mollify" but also how to use it accurately in various situations, which is crucial for the GRE!

## fatuous

Okay, let's break down the word **fatuous** for your GRE preparation.

---

## Fatuous

**Meaning:**
**Fatuous** (adjective) describes something or someone that is **smugly foolish, complacently silly, or unintelligent in a way that is also self-satisfied and unaware of its own absurdity.** It implies a foolishness that is often accompanied by an air of empty pomposity or a lack of understanding that the person believes they possess. It's not just "stupid"; it's a specific kind of foolishness marked by a vacant or smug demeanor.

**Etymology:** From Latin *fatuus* meaning "foolish, insipid."

**Common Collocations:**

*   **Fatuous remark/comment/statement:** A silly, pointless, or idiotic thing said, often with the speaker believing it profound.
*   **Fatuous grin/smile/expression:** A vacant, foolish, or self-satisfied look.
*   **Fatuous idea/notion/plan:** A silly, impractical, or absurd concept.
*   **Fatuous individual/person:** Someone characterized by this type of smug foolishness.
*   **Fatuous display/performance:** An act or show that is foolish and pointless.
*   **Fatuous optimism:** A baseless and silly sense of hope.
*   **Fatuous pursuits:** Activities or goals that are empty and foolish.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Speech/Commentary**
    *   **English:** His **fatuous remarks** throughout the meeting only served to highlight his lack of understanding of the complex issue.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在會議中的**愚蠢言論**只會突顯他對這個複雜議題的缺乏理解。
    *   *Pinyin:* Tā zài huìyì zhōng de **yúchǔn yánlùn** zhǐ huì túxiǎn tā duì zhège fùzá yìtí de quēfá lǐjiě.

2.  **Context: Behavior/Appearance (describing a facial expression)**
    *   **English:** She wore a **fatuous grin**, seemingly oblivious to the sarcasm in their voices.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她帶著**癡傻的笑容**，似乎對他們話語中的諷刺毫無察覺。
    *   *Pinyin:* Tā dàizhe **chīshǎ de xiàoróng**, sìhū duì tāmen huàyǔ zhōng de fēngcì háowú chájúe.

3.  **Context: Ideas/Plans (abstract concepts)**
    *   **English:** The committee unanimously rejected his **fatuous plan** to solve the budget crisis with an imaginary lottery.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 委員會一致駁回了他那個**異想天開的愚蠢計劃**，該計劃打算用虛構的彩票來解決預算危機。
    *   *Pinyin:* Wěiyuánhuì yīzhì bóhuí le tā nàge **yìxiǎng tiānkāi de yúchǔn jìhuà**, gāi jìhuà dǎsuàn yòng xūgòu de cǎipiào lái jiějué yùsuàn wéijī.

4.  **Context: Character Description**
    *   **English:** Despite his privileged background, he remained a profoundly **fatuous individual**, incapable of critical thought or genuine empathy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他出身顯赫，他仍然是一個**愚不可及的人**，無法進行批判性思考或產生真正的同理心。
    *   *Pinyin:* Jǐnguǎn tā chūshēn xiǎnhè, tā réngrán shì yīgè **yú bù kě jí de rén**, wúfǎ jìnxíng pīpànxìng sīkǎo huò chǎnshēng zhēnzhèng de tónglǐxīn.

5.  **Context: Literary/Social Commentary**
    *   **English:** The novel satirized the superficiality of modern society, exposing its empty rituals and **fatuous pursuits**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這部小說諷刺了現代社會的膚淺，揭露其空洞的儀式和**無意義的追求**。
    *   *Pinyin:* Zhè bù xiǎoshuō fěngcì le xiàndài shèhuì de fūqiǎn, jiēlù qí kōngdòng de yíshì hé **wú yìyì de zhuīqiú**.

By understanding these examples and common collocations, you'll be well-equipped to recognize and use "fatuous" effectively on the GRE!

## bevy

Okay, let's break down the word "bevy" for your GRE studies.

---

## Word: **Bevy**

### Meaning:

1.  **Primary Meaning:** A large group of people or things of a particular kind. It often implies a **lively, attractive, or significant group**, especially of women, but it can be used more broadly.
2.  **Specific Animal Meaning:** A large group of birds, especially quail. (This is the original and more literal use, but less common in everyday modern English than the first meaning).

**In essence, think of "bevy" as meaning "a group," but usually a *sizeable* or *noteworthy* group, and often with a slightly positive or impressive connotation, especially when referring to people.**

### Common Collocations:

The most common collocation is **"a bevy of [plural noun]"**.

Here are common nouns that follow "a bevy of":

*   **People:**
    *   **a bevy of admirers / fans**
    *   **a bevy of beauties / supermodels** (classic, though sometimes seen as dated or objectifying, still encountered)
    *   **a bevy of journalists / reporters**
    *   **a bevy of assistants / staff**
    *   **a bevy of young women / girls**
    *   **a bevy of experts / specialists**
    *   **a bevy of onlookers / spectators**
    *   **a bevy of celebrities / stars**
*   **Animals:**
    *   **a bevy of quail**
    *   **a bevy of larks**
*   **Figurative/Abstract (less common but possible):**
    *   **a bevy of challenges / problems** (suggests numerous, perhaps overwhelming, issues)
    *   **a bevy of questions**

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Context: People (admirers/attendants)**
    *   **English:** Upon her arrival, the fashion designer was immediately surrounded by a **bevy of photographers and admirers**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她一抵達，立刻就被一群**攝影師和仰慕者**團團圍住。
    *   *(Tā yī dǐdá, lìkè jiù bèi yī qún shèyǐngshī hé yǎngmùzhě tuántuán wéizhù.)*

2.  **Context: People (professionals/staff)**
    *   **English:** The CEO entered the meeting room, accompanied by a **bevy of senior executives** ready to present their quarterly reports.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長進入會議室時，身後跟著一群**高階主管**，準備呈報他們的季度報告。
    *   *(Zhíxíngzhǎng jìnrù huìyìshì shí, shēnhòu gēnzhe yī qún gāojiē zhǔguǎn, zhǔnbèi chéngbào tāmen de jìdù bàogào.)*

3.  **Context: Animals (traditional usage)**
    *   **English:** As we walked through the meadow, a **bevy of quail** suddenly flew up from the tall grass, startling us.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當我們走過草地時，一群**鵪鶉**突然從高高的草叢中飛了起來，嚇了我們一跳。
    *   *(Dāng wǒmen zǒuguò cǎodì shí, yī qún ānchún túrán cóng gāogāo de cǎocóng zhōng fēile qǐlái, xiàle wǒmen yī tiào.)*

4.  **Context: Abstract/Challenges (figurative use)**
    *   **English:** The new software launch presented the development team with a **bevy of unexpected challenges**, requiring quick thinking and adaptability.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新軟體的推出給開發團隊帶來了**一系列意想不到的挑戰**，需要他們快速思考和適應。
    *   *(Xīn ruǎntǐ de tuīchū gěi kāifā tuánduì dài láile yī xìliè yìxiǎngbùdào de tiǎozhàn, xūyào tāmen kuàisù sīkǎo hé shìyìng.)*
    *   *Note:* Here, "一系列" (a series of) is a good fit for "bevy" in the sense of "a collection of many" abstract things.

5.  **Context: Large group of experts/distinguished individuals**
    *   **English:** The international conference attracted a **bevy of experts** from various fields, all eager to share their latest research.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這次國際會議吸引了來自各領域的**眾多專家**，他們都渴望分享最新的研究成果。
    *   *(Zhè cì guójì huìyì xīyǐnle láizì gè lǐngyù de zhòngduō zhuānjiā, tāmen dōu kěwàng fēnxiǎng zuìxīn de yánjiù chéngguǒ.)*
    *   *Note:* "眾多專家" (numerous experts) or "一群專家" (a group of experts) could both work here, with "眾多" slightly emphasizing the quantity.

---

Remember, for the GRE, understanding the nuances and common contexts of "bevy" is key. It's more than just "a group"; it implies a notable collection, often of people. Good luck with your studies!

## abdicate

Let's break down the word "abdicate" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Abdicate

**Meaning:**
To **abdicate** means to formally give up a position of power, especially a throne, or to fail to fulfill a responsibility or duty. It implies a voluntary or forced relinquishment.

*   **Formal Definition:** To renounce or relinquish a throne, high office, dignity, or responsibility.
*   **Key Nuance:** It's often used in formal or serious contexts, particularly when discussing royalty, political leaders, or significant duties. It's more weighty than just "resigning" or "quitting."

**Common Collocations:**

*   **Abdicate the throne/crown:** (Most common and literal usage) To give up one's position as king or queen.
*   **Abdicate responsibility/duties:** To fail to carry out or formally give up one's obligations.
*   **Abdicate power/authority:** To relinquish control or command.
*   **Abdicate a role/position:** (Less common than "resign," but possible, especially if the role is very significant).

**Synonyms:**
Renounce, relinquish, resign, step down, surrender, cede

**Antonyms:**
Assume, seize, usurp, claim, take on

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

1.  **Context: Royal/Political Power**
    *   **English:** King Edward VIII **abdicated the throne** in 1936 to marry Wallis Simpson, causing a constitutional crisis.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 愛德華八世國王於1936年為與華麗絲·辛普森結婚而**遜位**（或**退位**），導致了一場憲政危機。

2.  **Context: Personal Responsibility/Duty (often used disapprovingly)**
    *   **English:** Many critics argued that the government **abdicated its responsibility** to protect the environment by approving the controversial logging project.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多評論家認為，政府透過批准有爭議的伐木項目，**放棄了其保護環境的責任**。

3.  **Context: Leadership/Authority**
    *   **English:** By constantly deferring to his subordinates, the manager effectively **abdicated his authority** and lost control of the team.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 透過不斷地順從下屬，這位經理實際上**放棄了他的權力**，失去了對團隊的控制。

4.  **Context: Moral/Ethical Obligation**
    *   **English:** Some philosophers believe that if a society **abdicates its duty** to care for the vulnerable, it loses its moral compass.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一些哲學家認為，如果一個社會**放棄了其照顧弱勢群體的義務**，它就會失去其道德指南。

5.  **Context: Figurative use (less common but illustrates breadth)**
    *   **English:** After years of defending her rights, she finally chose to **abdicate her claim** to the disputed inheritance.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在多年捍衛自己的權利後，她最終選擇**放棄對這筆爭議遺產的聲索**。

---

Understanding "abdicate" in these varied contexts will help you not only grasp its meaning but also recognize its appropriate usage on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## condone

Let's break down "condone" for your GRE prep!

## Understanding "Condon"

**Meaning:**

To **condone** something means to accept and allow behavior that is considered morally wrong or offensive to continue. It implies treating something bad as if it were acceptable, not serious, or not needing to be stopped.

*   **Key Nuance:** It's not necessarily *approving* of something, but rather *overlooking*, *excusing*, or *failing to condemn* it, thereby implicitly giving it permission to persist. It often suggests a lack of moral backbone or a lowering of standards.

**Etymology Note (helpful for memory):** The word comes from Latin *condonare*, meaning "to give up, remit, forgive." This helps capture the sense of "letting go" or "overlooking" something wrong.

---

## Common Collocations for "Condon"

"Condon" is most often used with nouns referring to negative or undesirable actions/behaviors. It's also frequently seen in negative constructions (e.g., "cannot condone," "refuse to condone").

**Verbs/Phrases frequently used with "condone":**
*   **cannot condone** (very common)
*   **refuse to condone**
*   **unwilling to condone**
*   **tacitly condone** (implies condoning without explicitly saying so)
*   **effectively condone**

**Nouns that are often "condoned":**
*   **violence**
*   **discrimination**
*   **bullying**
*   **cheating / dishonesty**
*   **unethical behavior / practices**
*   **misconduct**
*   **abuse**
*   **plagiarism**
*   **corruption**
*   **illegal activities**
*   **racism / sexism**
*   **bad behavior / rude behavior**

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples of "condone" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Parenting/Child Behavior**
    *   **English:** Parents should not **condone** their children's rude behavior, as it teaches them that such actions are acceptable.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 父母不應該**縱容**孩子的無禮行為，因為這會讓他們以為這些行為是可以接受的。

2.  **Context: Workplace Ethics/Professional Conduct**
    *   **English:** The company's policy states that it will not **condone** any form of discrimination or harassment in the workplace.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 公司的政策聲明，絕不**縱容**任何形式的職場歧視或騷擾。

3.  **Context: Social Justice/Human Rights**
    *   **English:** Many human rights organizations argue that silence in the face of injustice can often be interpreted as **condoning** it.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多人權組織認為，面對不公保持沉默，往往會被解讀為**縱容**。

4.  **Context: Academic Integrity**
    *   **English:** The university has a strict policy against plagiarism and will not **condone** any instances of academic dishonesty.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該大學對抄襲有嚴格政策，絕不**縱容**任何學術不誠實的行為。

5.  **Context: Personal Morality/Friendship**
    *   **English:** While I understand his frustration, I cannot **condone** his decision to resort to violence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然我理解他的挫敗感，但我不能**縱容**他訴諸暴力的決定。

6.  **Context: Political/Government Action**
    *   **English:** Critics argue that the government's inaction on environmental pollution effectively **condones** the destruction of natural habitats.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 批評者認為，政府對環境污染的不作為，實質上**縱容**了自然棲息地的破壞。

7.  **Context: Sports/Cheating**
    *   **English:** The coach made it clear that he would never **condone** cheating, even if it meant winning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 教練明確表示，他絕不會**縱容**作弊行為，即使那意味著會贏得比賽。

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "condone" on the GRE!

## impecunious

That's a great word for GRE! Let's break down "impecunious."

---

### Impecunious

**Meaning:**
*   **Definition:** Having little or no money; habitually without money; poor; penniless.
*   **Etymology:** The word comes from Latin "impecuniosus," meaning "without money," from "im-" (not) + "pecunia" (money). This etymology is helpful for remembering the meaning!
*   **Connotation:** It often describes a persistent state of poverty, rather than just a temporary lack of funds. It's a formal and somewhat literary word, conveying a sense of being perpetually in need.

**Common Collocations:**

"Impecunious" most commonly collocates with **people** or **groups of people** who are in a state of poverty, or the **conditions** that lead to or describe such a state.

*   **Impecunious artist/writer/student/scholar:** This is probably the most common. It evokes the stereotype of a talented individual who struggles financially for their art or studies.
*   **Impecunious family/household:** Referring to a family in poverty.
*   **Impecunious circumstances/state:** Describing the financial situation itself.
*   **Impecunious gentleman/lady:** Often used in historical or literary contexts to describe someone of good breeding who has fallen on hard times.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts:

Here are multiple examples showcasing "impecunious" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: An Artist's Struggle**
    *   **English:** Despite his immense talent, the young painter remained **impecunious** for years, selling only a few pieces to make ends meet.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他才華橫溢，這位年輕的畫家多年來一直**貧困潦倒**，只賣出寥寥幾幅作品來勉強維持生計。

2.  **Context: Student Life**
    *   **English:** Many graduate students find themselves in an **impecunious** state, relying on ramen noodles and part-time jobs to get by.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多研究生發現自己**手頭拮据**，只能靠泡麵和兼職工作度日。

3.  **Context: Socio-economic Conditions**
    *   **English:** The economic downturn left many families **impecunious**, struggling to afford basic necessities like food and shelter.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經濟衰退導致許多家庭**陷入貧困**，難以負擔食物和住所等基本生活必需品。

4.  **Context: Character Description (Literary)**
    *   **English:** He was a proud but **impecunious** gentleman, always meticulously dressed despite his threadbare pockets and worn shoes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他是一位驕傲但**貧窮**的紳士，儘管口袋空空、鞋子磨損，卻總是穿著講究。

5.  **Context: Legal/Philanthropic**
    *   **English:** The foundation provides grants to **impecunious** scholars, enabling them to pursue their research without financial burden.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該基金會向**貧困**學者提供補助金，讓他們能夠在沒有經濟負擔的情況下進行研究。

6.  **Context: Historical Narrative**
    *   **English:** After squandering his inheritance, he became completely **impecunious** and had to seek help from distant relatives.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 揮霍掉繼承的財產後，他變得一無所有，**身無分文**，只好向遠親尋求幫助。

---

Keep practicing these words in different contexts, and you'll master them for the GRE! Good luck!

## meander

Let's break down the word "meander" for your GRE studies!

---

## **Meander**

**Pronunciation:** /miːˈændər/

**Part of Speech:** Can be both a **verb** and a **noun**, though it's much more common as a verb, especially in GRE contexts.

---

### **Meaning:**

1.  **As a Verb:**
    *   **Physical:** To follow a winding and turning course; to wander aimlessly or leisurely, without a fixed direction or urgency. Think of a river or a path that isn't straight.
    *   **Abstract:** To wander from a direct or main course, topic, or purpose; to ramble in speech or thought. This is often the sense used in GRE-style questions about arguments, narratives, or discussions.

2.  **As a Noun:**
    *   A winding curve or bend in a river or road. (Less common in everyday GRE vocabulary, but good to know for completeness).

**Key Nuance:** The core idea is *not going in a straight line* – either physically or metaphorically. It often implies a lack of directness, purpose, or urgency.

---

### **Common Collocations:**

The most common collocations for "meander" as a verb typically involve the subject that is doing the "meandering" and sometimes a preposition indicating the path.

1.  **Nature/Physical Paths:**
    *   **River/Stream/Path/Trail meanders:** (e.g., "The river meanders through the valley.")
    *   **Meander through/along/around:** (e.g., "We meandered through the market.")

2.  **Abstract/Non-Physical:**
    *   **Conversation/Discussion meanders:** (e.g., "The conversation began to meander.")
    *   **Mind/Thoughts meander:** (e.g., "His thoughts meandered.")
    *   **Plot/Narrative meanders:** (e.g., "The novel's plot tends to meander.")

---

### **Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples showing both the physical and abstract uses, with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: A River (Physical Movement)**
    *   **Sentence:** The tranquil **river meanders** lazily through the valley, creating picturesque bends.
    *   **Explanation:** Describes the slow, winding path of a river.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 寧靜的河流慵懶地**蜿蜒**穿過山谷，形成風景如畫的彎道。

2.  **Context: Leisurely Walking (Physical Movement)**
    *   **Sentence:** We decided to **meander through** the old town's cobblestone streets, enjoying the historic architecture without any particular destination.
    *   **Explanation:** Implies walking without a specific goal, just exploring and enjoying.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我們決定在老城區的鵝卵石街道上**漫步**，沒有特定目的地，只是欣賞歷史建築。

3.  **Context: A Lecture/Speech (Abstract - Deviating from Topic)**
    *   **Sentence:** The professor's lecture began to **meander**, touching on various unrelated topics before finally returning to the main subject.
    *   **Explanation:** Describes a speech that loses its focus and goes off-topic.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 教授的講座開始**偏離主題**，觸及各種不相關的話題，最後才回到主要內容。

4.  **Context: Thoughts/Mind (Abstract - Wandering Thoughts)**
    *   **Sentence:** Before falling asleep, her **thoughts would often meander**, drifting from one memory to another without any logical connection.
    *   **Explanation:** Refers to thoughts that wander aimlessly, not following a direct line of reasoning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在入睡前，她的**思緒經常會漫遊**，從一個記憶飄向另一個，沒有任何邏輯聯繫。

5.  **Context: A Story/Plot (Abstract - Lack of Cohesion)**
    *   **Sentence:** Critics argued that the novel's **plot tended to meander** too much, making it difficult for readers to follow the main storyline.
    *   **Explanation:** Describes a narrative that lacks a clear direction or becomes unnecessarily complicated and drawn out.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 評論家認為這本小說的**情節過於冗長散漫**，使讀者難以跟隨主要故事情節。

---

Understanding "meander" is valuable for the GRE, especially for reading comprehension and text completion questions where it might describe the structure of an argument or a piece of writing. Good luck with your studies!

## volatile

Okay, let's break down the word "volatile" for your GRE studies.

---

## **Volatile**

**Meaning:**

"Volatile" describes something that is **likely to change rapidly and unpredictably**, often for the worse or in a difficult-to-manage way. It carries a strong connotation of **instability** and **unpredictability**.

It has a few key applications:

1.  **Chemistry/Physics:** Refers to a substance that **evaporates quickly** at normal temperatures and pressures (e.g., alcohol, gasoline). It implies a tendency to turn into vapor.
2.  **Emotions/Temperament:** Describes a person or their mood/temperament that is **unstable, unpredictable, and prone to sudden, dramatic shifts**, often involving anger or extreme reactions.
3.  **Situations/Markets/Economy:** Refers to a situation, market, or economic condition that is **unstable, prone to sudden and significant changes**, often leading to uncertainty or risk.

In the context of the GRE, you'll most commonly encounter the second and third meanings, emphasizing instability and unpredictability.

**Common Collocations:**

These are words that frequently appear with "volatile," helping you understand its context and usage:

*   **Volatile market/economy:** Referring to financial conditions that fluctuate wildly.
*   **Volatile stock/prices/currency:** Indicating rapid and unpredictable changes in value.
*   **Volatile situation/region/climate:** Describing an unstable or potentially dangerous environment.
*   **Volatile temper/mood/personality:** Referring to someone with unpredictable emotional swings.
*   **Volatile liquid/substance/compound:** Pertaining to chemicals that evaporate easily or are explosive.

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples illustrating the different facets of "volatile," along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Financial Markets**
    *   **English:** The stock market has been extremely **volatile** recently, making investors nervous about their portfolios.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 最近股市極度**波動**，讓投資者對其投資組合感到不安。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "volatile" means unpredictable and rapidly changing in value.

2.  **Context: Personal Temperament/Emotions**
    *   **English:** His **volatile** temper often led to arguments with his colleagues, making him difficult to work with.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那**不穩定的**脾氣（或：暴躁的脾氣）常常導致他與同事發生爭執，使他難以共事。
    *   *Explanation:* Refers to a person whose mood or anger can change suddenly and without warning.

3.  **Context: Political/Geographical Situation**
    *   **English:** The political situation in the region remains highly **volatile**, with frequent unrest and unpredictable shifts in power.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該地區的政治局勢仍然高度**動盪不安**，動亂頻繁且權力更迭難以預料。
    *   *Explanation:* Describes an unstable and potentially dangerous environment prone to sudden, significant changes.

4.  **Context: Chemistry/Substances**
    *   **English:** Gasoline is a highly **volatile** substance and must be stored with extreme care to prevent explosions or fires.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 汽油是極易**揮發的**物質，必須小心謹慎地儲存，以防止爆炸或火災。
    *   *Explanation:* In this context, it means easily evaporating into a gas.

5.  **Context: Abstract Concepts (e.g., Public Opinion)**
    *   **English:** Public opinion on the new policy has proven to be quite **volatile**, swinging wildly from strong approval to outright disapproval in a short period.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 民眾對於這項新政策的意見證明是相當**不穩定且變化無常的**，在短時間內從強烈贊成到完全反對劇烈擺盪。
    *   *Explanation:* Refers to an idea or sentiment that is prone to rapid and significant shifts.

---

Understanding "volatile" in these different contexts will be very beneficial for your GRE vocabulary section!

## cogent

That's an excellent word to master for the GRE! Let's break down "cogent."

---

## **Cogent**

**Meaning:**

*   **(Adjective)** Clear, logical, and convincing; appealing strongly to the mind or reason.
*   When something is *cogent*, it means it is well-thought-out, powerful, and effective in persuading or proving a point. It's not just "true," but *so clearly and logically presented* that it's hard to dispute.

**Think of it as:**
*   **Convincing:** It makes you believe it.
*   **Logical:** It follows a sensible structure.
*   **Strong:** It's not weak or easily refuted.

**Common Collocations:**

"Cogent" most commonly describes abstract nouns related to reasoning, arguments, and evidence.

*   **Cogent argument:** A very strong, convincing point of view.
*   **Cogent reason:** A valid and compelling justification.
*   **Cogent evidence:** Clear and irrefutable proof.
*   **Cogent explanation:** A clear and logical clarification.
*   **Cogent case:** A powerful and persuasive presentation of facts (often in a legal or debate context).
*   **Cogent analysis:** A deep, insightful, and logical examination.
*   **Cogent critique:** A well-reasoned and powerful evaluation (often negative).
*   **Present/Formulate a cogent...** (e.g., *present a cogent argument*)

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are several examples to illustrate "cogent" in different scenarios:

1.  **Academic/Debate Context:**
    *   **English:** The student presented a **cogent argument** in favor of renewable energy sources, citing numerous studies and statistics.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這名學生提出了一個**有說服力**的論點，支持可再生能源，並引用了大量的研究和統計數據。
        *(Note: "有說服力" (yǒu shuō fú lì) means "convincing" or "persuasive," which is a good fit for "cogent argument.")*

2.  **Legal Context:**
    *   **English:** The prosecutor's **cogent evidence** convinced the jury of the defendant's guilt beyond a reasonable doubt.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 檢察官**確鑿**的證據，讓陪審團確信被告有罪，毫無疑問。
        *(Note: "確鑿" (què záo) means "conclusive" or "irrefutable," which works well for "cogent evidence" in a legal context.)*

3.  **Business/Proposal Context:**
    *   **English:** Her presentation was so **cogent** that the board immediately approved the new marketing strategy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的簡報**非常有說服力**，以至於董事會立即批准了新的行銷策略。
        *(Note: "非常有說服力" emphasizes the strong persuasive power of the presentation.)*

4.  **Critical Review/Analysis Context:**
    *   **English:** The literary critic offered a **cogent analysis** of the novel's themes, revealing deeper meanings to the readers.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位文學評論家對小說的主題提供了**精闢**的分析，為讀者揭示了更深層次的意義。
        *(Note: "精闢" (jīng pì) means "incisive," "profound," or "well-argued," often used for insightful analysis or commentary.)*

5.  **Problem-Solving/Reasoning Context:**
    *   **English:** After hours of discussion, they finally arrived at a **cogent solution** to the complex engineering problem.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過數小時的討論，他們終於找到了一個**合乎邏輯且有效**的複雜工程問題解決方案。
        *(Note: "合乎邏輯" (hé hū luó jí) means "logical," and "有效" (yǒu xiào) means "effective," both capturing aspects of "cogent" for a solution.)*

6.  **Policy/Reporting Context:**
    *   **English:** The government report provided **cogent reasons** for the proposed policy changes, emphasizing long-term benefits.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這份政府報告為擬議的政策變革提供了**充分且有力**的理由，強調了長期效益。
        *(Note: "充分" (chōng fèn) means "sufficient/adequate," and "有力" (yǒu lì) means "strong/powerful," together conveying the strength and validity of the reasons.)*

---

By understanding "cogent" as clear, logical, and powerfully convincing, especially when applied to arguments, reasons, and evidence, you'll be well-equipped for the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## gainsay

Let's break down the word "gainsay" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Gainsay (verb)

**Meaning:**
To **gainsay** something means to **deny**, **dispute**, **contradict**, or **speak against** it. It implies challenging the truth, validity, or existence of something, often a statement, fact, or claim.

*   **Etymology Note:** The word comes from Old English "gegn-" (against) + "say." So, literally, it means "to say against."

**Common Collocations:**

The most common way you'll encounter "gainsay" is often in **negative constructions**, emphasizing the idea of something being undeniable or beyond dispute.

1.  **"cannot be gainsaid" / "could not be gainsaid"**: This is by far the most frequent and important collocation for GRE. It means something is undeniable, indisputable, or irrefutable.
    *   *Example:* "The facts cannot be gainsaid."

2.  **"beyond gainsay"**: Similar to the above, meaning undeniable, unquestionable.
    *   *Example:* "Her talent is beyond gainsay."

3.  **"to gainsay the truth/facts/evidence/claim"**: To deny or contradict something that is presented as true or factual.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts:

1.  **Context: Irrefutable Evidence/Facts**
    *   **English:** The overwhelming scientific evidence regarding climate change **cannot be gainsaid** by skeptics.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 關於氣候變遷的壓倒性科學證據，懷疑論者**無法反駁**。
    *   *(Note: Here, "cannot be gainsaid" means it's so strong it's impossible to deny or dispute.)*

2.  **Context: Challenging a Statement or Authority**
    *   **English:** Despite the manager's firm declaration, one employee dared to **gainsay** his decision, pointing out its potential flaws.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管經理立場堅定地聲明，仍有一位員工敢於**反駁**他的決定，並指出其潛在缺陷。
    *   *(Note: Here, "gainsay" implies a direct contradiction or challenge to someone's statement or authority.)*

3.  **Context: Denying a Claim or Account**
    *   **English:** His reputation for honesty was so strong that no one would **gainsay** his account of the events.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他誠實的名聲如此顯赫，以至於無人會**質疑**他對事件的描述。
    *   *(Note: This means no one would deny or dispute the truthfulness of his story.)*

4.  **Context: Undeniable Quality/Characteristic**
    *   **English:** Her extraordinary talent for classical piano is **beyond gainsay**; she consistently delivers breathtaking performances.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她演奏古典鋼琴的非凡天賦**毋庸置疑**；她總是帶來令人驚嘆的表演。
    *   *(Note: "Beyond gainsay" means it's an undeniable, unquestionable fact.)*

5.  **Context: Attempting to Deny (and failing)**
    *   **English:** She tried to **gainsay** the accusations of plagiarism, but the parallel passages in her essay made it impossible.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她試圖**否認**抄襲的指控，但她文章中的雷同段落使之不可能。
    *   *(Note: Here, it's about the act of attempting to deny something.)*

---

**GRE Tip:**
On the GRE, "gainsay" often appears in reading comprehension passages or sentence completion questions where you need to identify words related to **denial, contradiction, or irrefutability**. Pay close attention to negative constructions like "cannot be gainsaid" or "beyond gainsay," as they signify something is true or certain.

## patent

Okay, let's break down the word "patent" for your GRE studies. It's a very common word in business, law, and technology contexts.

---

## Meaning of "Patent"

"Patent" can function as both a **noun** and a **verb**.

### 1. As a Noun (most common usage)

A **patent** is a **legal right** granted by a government to an inventor or assignee for a limited period. This right **excludes others from making, using, or selling** the invention without the patent holder's permission.

*   **Key purpose:** To encourage innovation by giving inventors a temporary monopoly on their invention, allowing them to profit from their creativity.
*   **What it protects:** Inventions (new processes, machines, manufactured articles, compositions of matter), and sometimes designs or plant varieties.

### 2. As a Verb

To **patent** something means to **obtain a patent for it**. It's the act of securing those exclusive legal rights for your invention.

---

## Common Collocations

Here are some common collocations with "patent" that you'll frequently encounter:

### With "Patent" as a Noun:

*   **Apply for a patent / File a patent (application):** To formally request a patent from a government office.
*   **Grant a patent / Issue a patent:** When the government approves and awards a patent.
*   **Obtain a patent / Secure a patent:** To successfully acquire a patent.
*   **Hold a patent:** To possess a valid patent.
*   **Infringe a patent / Patent infringement:** The act of violating a patent holder's exclusive rights (e.g., by making or selling their invention without permission).
*   **License a patent:** To grant permission to another party to use your patented invention, usually for a fee (royalty).
*   **Patent holder / Patentee:** The person or entity that owns the patent.
*   **Patent law:** The legal framework governing patents.
*   **Patent office:** The government agency responsible for granting patents (e.g., USPTO).
*   **Patent pending:** A status indicating that a patent application has been filed but not yet granted.
*   **Expired patent:** A patent whose legal protection period has ended.
*   **Utility patent / Design patent / Plant patent:** Specific types of patents protecting different aspects of inventions.

### With "Patent" as a Verb:

*   **Patent an invention / Patent a process:** To get a patent for your invention or process.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating the usage of "patent" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Application and Granting (Noun)**
    *   **English:** After years of research, the startup successfully **filed a patent application** for its unique battery technology.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年的研究，這家新創公司成功地為其獨特的電池技術**提交了專利申請**。

2.  **Context: Legal Protection (Noun)**
    *   **English:** The company holds a **patent** on the algorithm, which gives them exclusive rights to its use for the next 15 years.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家公司擁有該演算法的**專利**，賦予他們未來15年的獨家使用權。

3.  **Context: Infringement (Noun)**
    *   **English:** They are suing their competitor for **patent infringement**, claiming their new device directly copies their **patented** design.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們正在控告競爭對手**侵犯專利**，聲稱他們的新設備直接複製了他們**已獲專利**的設計。

4.  **Context: Licensing (Noun)**
    *   **English:** The small inventor decided to **license his patent** to a larger manufacturing firm instead of producing the item himself.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位小型發明家決定將其**專利許可**給一家大型製造公司，而不是自己生產該產品。

5.  **Context: As a Verb (Active Voice)**
    *   **English:** It's crucial to **patent** your invention early to prevent others from stealing your ideas.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 及早**為**你的發明**申請專利**至關重要，以防止他人竊取你的想法。

6.  **Context: As a Verb (Passive Voice / Adjective-like usage)**
    *   **English:** This specific circuit board is a **patented** component, meaning it cannot be freely replicated.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個特定的電路板是一個**已獲專利**的組件，這表示它不能被自由複製。

7.  **Context: Expired Patent (Noun)**
    *   **English:** Once a **patent expires**, the invention enters the public domain and can be freely used by anyone.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一旦**專利期滿**，該發明即進入公共領域，任何人都可以自由使用。

By understanding these nuances and practicing with the collocations, you'll be well-prepared for any GRE questions involving "patent"!

## fallacious

Okay, let's break down "fallacious" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Fallacious"

**Meaning:**

The word **fallacious** (pronounced fuh-LAY-shus) is an adjective that describes something that contains a **fallacy**.

A **fallacy** is a mistaken belief, especially one based on unsound argument, or a misleading or unsound argument itself.

So, when something is **fallacious**, it means it:
1.  **Contains a flaw in logic or reasoning.** It seems plausible or true, but it's fundamentally incorrect or ill-reasoned.
2.  **Is based on a mistaken belief or false premise.**
3.  **Is misleading or deceptive**, especially in an argument, claim, or statement.

**In essence, if something is fallacious, it looks good on the surface but is logically rotten underneath.**

**Part of Speech:** Adjective

**Related Noun:** Fallacy (a flaw or error in reasoning)

---

### Common Collocations

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these will help you use "fallacious" naturally and understand it in context.

*   **Fallacious argument:** An argument that contains a logical error.
*   **Fallacious reasoning:** The process of thinking or drawing conclusions in a way that is logically flawed.
*   **Fallacious claim/statement:** A declaration or assertion that is untrue or based on incorrect logic.
*   **Fallacious assumption/premise:** A starting point for an argument or belief that is incorrect or unsubstantiated.
*   **Fallacious logic:** A system of thinking that is inherently flawed.
*   **Fallacious belief:** A widespread or personal conviction that is mistaken.
*   **Fallacious evidence:** Information presented as proof that is misleading or unsound.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use, along with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Debate/Argumentation**
    *   **English:** His entire argument was based on a **fallacious premise**, making his conclusion invalid.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他的整個論點都基於一個**謬誤的前提**，導致其結論無效。
    *   *Explanation:* The starting point of his argument was wrong, so the end result couldn't be right.

2.  **Context: Critical Thinking/Media Literacy**
    *   **English:** The advertisement used **fallacious reasoning** to convince consumers that their product would solve all problems instantly.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個廣告使用了**謬誤的推理**來讓消費者相信他們的產品能立即解決所有問題。
    *   *Explanation:* The ad's "logic" was designed to mislead, not to present a true benefit.

3.  **Context: Legal/Academic**
    *   **English:** The lawyer attempted to introduce **fallacious evidence**, but the judge quickly dismissed it.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 律師試圖提出**虛假的證據**，但法官迅速駁回了。
    *   *Explanation:* The evidence was misleading or not genuinely supportive of the claim. (Note: 虛假 is often used for "false" evidence, while 謬誤 might be used for "illogical" evidence).
