4.  **Context: General Beliefs/Assumptions**
    *   **English:** Many ancient cultures held the **fallacious belief** that the sun revolved around the Earth.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多古代文化都持有一種**錯誤的信念**，認為太陽是圍繞地球轉的。
    *   *Explanation:* A widely held but factually incorrect idea.

5.  **Context: Rhetoric/Persuasion**
    *   **English:** Despite the speaker's eloquent delivery, his speech was full of **fallacious claims** designed to mislead the audience.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管演講者的口才流利，但他的演講卻充滿了旨在誤導聽眾的**謬誤主張**。
    *   *Explanation:* The speaker's statements, though well-spoken, were logically unsound or untrue.

6.  **Context: Research/Scientific Inquiry**
    *   **English:** Researchers must be careful to avoid drawing **fallacious conclusions** from limited data.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 研究人員必須小心，避免從有限的數據中得出**謬誤的結論**。
    *   *Explanation:* The conclusions drawn might not logically follow from the given data.

---

By understanding "fallacious" in these contexts and practicing with its common collocations, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## stolid

Let's break down the word "stolid" for your GRE preparation.

## Stolid (adjective)

**Meaning:**
"Stolid" describes someone (or something) who is **calm, dependable, and shows little emotion or animation; not easily excited or upset; unemotional; impassive.** It often implies a lack of interest, spirit, or responsiveness, and can sometimes carry a slightly negative connotation of being dull, uninspired, or even sluggish.

Think of it as someone with a "poker face" who rarely lets their feelings show, or who just takes things in stride without much reaction.

**Key characteristics:**
*   **Lack of emotional display:** They don't show joy, sorrow, anger, surprise, etc., outwardly.
*   **Calmness/Composure:** They remain composed even in situations where others might become agitated.
*   **Unresponsiveness:** They might not react much to stimuli or new information.
*   (Sometimes) **Dullness/Lack of vivacity:** Their lack of emotion can make them seem uninteresting or unenthusiastic.

**Common Collocations:**
*   **Stolid face/expression:** The most common. Describes a face that reveals no emotion.
*   **Stolid demeanor/manner:** Describes someone's general bearing or way of behaving.
*   **Stolid person/figure:** Describes the individual themselves.
*   **Stolid silence/gaze:** Describes a lack of verbal response or an unblinking, emotionless stare.
*   **Stolid response:** An unemotional or unenthusiastic reaction.
*   **Stolid character/nature:** Describes an inherent personality trait.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts:

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage, with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Emotional Control/Lack of Expression (Common usage)**
    *   **English:** Despite the shocking news, her **stolid expression** gave away nothing about her true feelings.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管聽到了令人震驚的消息，她那張**面無表情**的臉卻絲毫未透露出任何真實情緒。

2.  **Context: Character/Personality Trait (Often implying resilience or steadfastness)**
    *   **English:** The old farmer, with his **stolid demeanor**, rarely showed signs of joy or sorrow, taking life's ups and downs with quiet resignation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位老農夫**沉穩淡定**，很少表現出喜怒哀樂，泰然地面對著人生的起伏。

3.  **Context: Professional/Controlled Environment (Implying imperviousness to pressure)**
    *   **English:** The witness remained **stolid** under cross-examination, refusing to crack or reveal any emotion even under intense pressure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在交叉詰問下，證人始終**面不改色**，即使在強大壓力下也拒絕崩潰或透露任何情緒。

4.  **Context: Describing a Figure/Physical Presence (Emphasizing immobility and lack of animation)**
    *   **English:** The **stolid** security guard stood motionless at the gate, his gaze fixed straight ahead, impervious to distractions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那名**不苟言笑**的保全一動不動地站在大門口，目光直視前方，絲毫不受干擾。

5.  **Context: Artistic/Literary Criticism (Carrying a negative connotation of dullness or lack of passion)**
    *   **English:** Critics found his latest novel rather **stolid**, praising its technical competence but lamenting its lack of passion and emotional depth.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 評論家們認為他最新的小說相當**沉悶乏味**，雖然讚揚其技術純熟，卻惋惜它缺乏熱情和情感深度。

By studying these examples and their translations, you can grasp the nuances of "stolid" and how it's used in different situations, which will be very helpful for the GRE. Good luck!

## incipient

Let's break down "incipient" for your GRE studies.

## Incipient (adjective)

**Meaning:**
"Incipient" describes something that is **just beginning to appear or develop**. It implies that something is in its very early stages, often suggesting that it will grow or progress further. It can be used for both positive and negative developments, though it often carries a connotation of something *about to become* more significant, sometimes problematic.

Think of it as the "budding" or "nascent" stage of something.

**Common Collocations:**

*   **Incipient signs/symptoms:** The first indications of something (e.g., an illness, a problem).
*   **Incipient stage/phase:** The very beginning of a process or development.
*   **Incipient crisis/problem:** A difficulty or conflict that is just starting to emerge.
*   **Incipient rebellion/movement:** A social or political shift that is just gaining traction.
*   **Incipient technology/idea:** A new development that is in its infancy.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to help you grasp its usage:

1.  **Context: Medical/Health**
    *   **English:** The doctor detected **incipient** signs of the illness and prescribed medication immediately, preventing it from worsening.
    *   **Meaning:** The very first, early signs of the illness.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 醫生檢測到疾病的**初期**跡象，並立即開藥，防止病情惡化。
    *   (Yīshēng jiǎncè dào jíbing de **chūqī** jìxiàng, bìng lìjí kāi yào, fángzhǐ bìngqíng èhuà.)

2.  **Context: Social/Political/Economic (Problems/Crises)**
    *   **English:** Analysts warned of an **incipient** economic crisis, citing rising inflation and declining consumer confidence.
    *   **Meaning:** An economic crisis that is just starting to emerge or become evident.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 分析師警告了一場**初期的**經濟危機，指出通貨膨脹上升和消費者信心下降。
    *   (Fēnxīshī jǐnggào le yī chǎng **chūqī de** jīngjì wéijī, zhǐchū tōnghuò péngzhàng shàngshēng hé xiāofèi zhě xìnxīn xiàjiàng.)

3.  **Context: Emotional/Personal**
    *   **English:** A feeling of **incipient** panic began to rise as she realized she had lost her keys in the dark parking lot.
    *   **Meaning:** Panic that was just starting to form or be felt.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當她意識到自己在黑暗的停車場丟失了鑰匙時，一種**初生的**恐慌感開始升起。
    *   (Dāng tā yìshí dào zìjǐ zài hēi'àn de tíngchēchǎng diūshī le yàoshí shí, yī zhǒng **chūshēng de** kǒnghuāng gǎn kāishǐ shēngqǐ.)

4.  **Context: Technology/Development**
    *   **English:** The prototype showed an **incipient** technology with the potential to revolutionize the industry.
    *   **Meaning:** A technology that is very new, in its foundational or earliest developmental stages.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該原型展示了一種**剛萌芽的**技術，具有徹底改變該產業的潛力。
    *   (Gāi yuánxíng zhǎnshì le yī zhǒng **gāng méngyá de** jìshù, jùyǒu chèdǐ gǎibiàn gāi chǎnyè de qiánlì.)

5.  **Context: Career/Growth**
    *   **English:** Even in its **incipient** stages, her career showed great promise, marked by innovative ideas and a strong work ethic.
    *   **Meaning:** The very beginning phases of her career.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使在**初期**階段，她的事業也展現出巨大的前景，以創新的想法和強烈的工作熱情為特徵。
    *   (Jíshǐ zài **chūqī** jiēduàn, tā de shìyè yě zhǎnxiàn chū jùdà de qiánjǐng, yǐ chuàngxīn de xiǎngfǎ hé qiángliè de gōngzuò rèqíng wéi tèzhēng.)

Understanding "incipient" often involves recognizing that it refers to the *genesis* or *very first stirrings* of something, implying a process of development or change that is just commencing. Good luck with your GRE!

## ambivalent

Okay, let's break down "ambivalent" for your GRE prep!

---

## Word: Ambivalent

### Meaning

**Ambivalent** (adjective) describes a state of having mixed feelings or contradictory ideas about something or someone. It implies being torn between two opposing emotions, opinions, or courses of action, often simultaneously. You feel both attracted and repelled, or have both positive and negative thoughts at the same time, making it difficult to make a clear decision or have a consistent attitude.

*   **Etymology Tip (for deeper understanding):** The word comes from Latin roots: "ambi-" meaning "both" (like ambidextrous – using both hands) and "valere" meaning "to be strong" or "to be worth." So, it literally means "having strong feelings in both directions."

### Common Collocations

"Ambivalent" often pairs with verbs and prepositions that describe the *experience* of having mixed feelings, or the *object* of those feelings.

*   **Feel ambivalent:** The most common way to express it.
    *   *Example:* I **feel ambivalent** about the new policy.
*   **Be ambivalent:** Similar to "feel ambivalent," describing one's state.
    *   *Example:* She **was ambivalent** about her career choice.
*   **Remain ambivalent:** To continue to have mixed feelings.
    *   *Example:* Despite the presentation, many employees **remained ambivalent**.
*   **Ambivalent about/towards:** Specifies the subject of the mixed feelings.
    *   *Example:* He is **ambivalent about** getting married.
    *   *Example:* The public is **ambivalent towards** the new tax plan.
*   **Have ambivalent feelings/emotions/attitudes:** Describes the specific mixed reactions.
    *   *Example:* She **had ambivalent feelings** about leaving her hometown.
*   **Ambivalent relationship/response/attitude:** Describes something characterized by mixed feelings.
    *   *Example:* Theirs was an **ambivalent relationship**.

---

### Example Sentences and Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples in various contexts:

**1. Career/Job Context**

*   **English:** She felt **ambivalent about** accepting the promotion; it offered more money but also significantly more stress and responsibility.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對於接受升遷感到**矛盾**；這雖然帶來更多薪水，但也意味著更多壓力和責任。
    *(Explanation: "矛盾" (máodùn) directly translates to "contradictory" or "conflicted," perfectly capturing the essence of ambivalence.)*

**2. Personal Relationship/Decision Context**

*   **English:** He was **ambivalent about** moving abroad, torn between the excitement of a new adventure and the comfort of his familiar life.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對於移居國外**心情矛盾**，在探索新奇的刺激與安穩熟悉的生活之間難以取捨。
    *(Explanation: "心情矛盾" (xīnqíng máodùn) means "conflicted feelings" or "mixed emotions," which is a common way to express personal ambivalence.)*

**3. Social/Political Context**

*   **English:** Public opinion on the new environmental policy remained **ambivalent**, with some supporting its goals but others questioning its methods and economic impact.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 公眾對新環保政策的意見仍然**搖擺不定**，有些人支持其目標，但另一些人則質疑其方法和經濟影響。
    *(Explanation: "搖擺不定" (yáobǎi bùdìng) means "wavering" or "undecided," capturing the lack of a clear, consistent stance in public opinion. You could also use "態度曖昧" (tàidù àimèi) which means "ambiguous attitude.")*

**4. Literary/Artistic Context**

*   **English:** The protagonist's **ambivalent feelings towards** his past made for a complex and compelling narrative, as he both longed for and resented his childhood.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 主角對他過去的**矛盾情感**造就了一個複雜而引人入勝的敘事，因為他既渴望又怨恨他的童年。
    *(Explanation: "矛盾情感" (máodùn qínggǎn) directly translates to "contradictory emotions," fitting for character analysis.)*

**5. Consumer Choice/Product Context**

*   **English:** She was **ambivalent about** buying the expensive new smartphone; it had impressive features, but she wasn't sure if it was worth the hefty price tag.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對於購買那款昂貴的新智慧型手機感到**舉棋不定**；它功能強大，但她不確定是否值得那個高價。
    *(Explanation: "舉棋不定" (jǔqí bùdìng) is an idiom meaning "unable to make up one's mind," literally "hesitating to move a chess piece." It conveys the indecision caused by mixed feelings.)*

---

Keep practicing these examples and try to create your own! Understanding "ambivalent" will definitely help you in the GRE's verbal reasoning section. Good luck!

## composure

Let's break down the word "composure" for your GRE studies.

---

### Meaning of "Composure"

**Composure** (noun) refers to the state of being calm, self-controlled, and emotionally steady, especially in difficult, stressful, or surprising situations. It's about maintaining a calm and collected demeanor and not letting your emotions get the better of you, even under pressure.

**Key aspects for GRE:**
*   **Self-possession:** You are in control of yourself.
*   **Calmness under pressure:** The ability to remain tranquil when facing challenges.
*   **Emotional control:** Not showing signs of agitation, panic, or distress.

**Synonyms (useful for GRE vocabulary):**
*   Equanimity
*   Serenity
*   Aplomb
*   Poise
*   Self-possession
*   Tranquility

**Antonyms:**
*   Agitation
*   Discomposure
*   Panic
*   Nervousness

---

### Common Collocations for "Composure"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you use "composure" naturally and understand it better in context.

**Verbs often used with "composure":**
*   **maintain/keep composure:** To remain calm.
*   **lose composure:** To become agitated or upset; to lose control.
*   **regain/recover composure:** To become calm again after being upset.
*   **display/show composure:** To exhibit a calm and steady demeanor.
*   **break composure:** To lose one's calm, often suddenly.

**Adjectives often used with "composure":**
*   **great/remarkable/admirable composure:** To describe a high degree of calmness.
*   **unshakable/imperturbable composure:** To describe an inability to be flustered or disturbed.
*   **cool composure:** A calm, collected, and unruffled manner.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "composure" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations.

1.  **Context: Handling a Crisis/Pressure (Common GRE scenario)**
    *   **English:** Despite the chaotic scenes around him, the captain **maintained his composure**, calmly giving orders to his crew.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管周圍一片混亂，船長仍**保持鎮定**，從容地向船員下達指令。
    *   *Pinyin:* Jǐnguǎn zhōuwéi yīpiàn hùnluàn, chuánzhǎng réng **bǎochí zhèndìng**, cóngróng de xiàng chuányuán xiàdá zhǐlìng.

2.  **Context: Performance/Public Speaking**
    *   **English:** The young pianist **showed remarkable composure** during her debut performance, delivering a flawless rendition despite the immense pressure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位年輕的鋼琴家在她的首次演出中**展現出非凡的沉著**，儘管壓力巨大，她仍完美地完成了演奏。
    *   *Pinyin:* Zhè wèi niánqīng de gāngqín jiā zài tā de shǒucì yǎnchū zhōng **zhǎnxiàn chū fēifán de chénzhuó**, jǐnguǎn yālì jùdà, tā réng wánměi de wánchéng le yǎnzòu.

3.  **Context: Personal/Emotional Challenge**
    *   **English:** Even after hearing the disappointing news, she managed to **keep her composure**, not letting her emotions overwhelm her in front of her colleagues.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使聽到令人失望的消息，她依然努力**保持鎮定**，沒有在同事面前讓情緒失控。
    *   *Pinyin:* Jíshǐ tīng dào lìng rén shīwàng de xiāoxī, tā yīrán nǔlì **bǎochí zhèndìng**, méiyǒu zài tóngshì miànqián ràng qíngxù shīkòng.

4.  **Context: Losing Composure (The Opposite)**
    *   **English:** After hours of relentless interrogation, the suspect finally **lost his composure**, shouting and becoming agitated.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過數小時的嚴酷審訊後，嫌犯最終**失去了鎮定**，開始大喊大叫並變得焦躁不安。
    *   *Pinyin:* Jīngguò shù xiǎoshí de yánkù shěnxùn hòu, xiánfàn zuìzhōng **shīqù le zhèndìng**, kāishǐ dàhǎndàjiào bìng biànde jiāozào bù'ān.

5.  **Context: Regaining Composure**
    *   **English:** It took her a few moments to **regain her composure** after the sudden fright, but soon she was able to explain what had happened.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 突如其來的驚嚇讓她花了好一會兒才**恢復鎮定**，但很快她就能解釋發生了什麼事。
    *   *Pinyin:* Túrúqílái de jīngxià ràng tā huā le hǎo yī huì'er cái **huīfù zhèndìng**, dàn hěn kuài tā jiù néng jiěshì fāshēng le shénme shì.

---

Understanding "composure" and its common collocations will be very helpful for both the GRE's verbal reasoning section (especially text completion and sentence equivalence) and for reading comprehension passages where characters' emotional states are described. Good luck with your studies!

## canon

Let's break down the word "canon" for your GRE studies. It's a versatile word with several distinct but related meanings, primarily revolving around the idea of a *standard*, *rule*, or *established body of works*.

---

### Meaning of "Canon"

The word "canon" generally refers to:

1.  **A collection of works considered authentic and authoritative:** This is its most common usage in literature, art, and film. It refers to a body of works that are accepted as genuine, definitive, or of the highest quality within a particular field or by a particular author.
    *   **Example:** The Shakespearean canon refers to all the plays and poems genuinely written by Shakespeare.
    *   **Example:** The Star Wars canon refers to the officially recognized storyline and events within that universe, as opposed to fan fiction or non-official material.

2.  **A general law, rule, principle, or criterion by which something is judged:** In this sense, it refers to a set of fundamental principles or standards.
    *   **Example:** The canons of good taste.
    *   **Example:** Ethical canons of journalism.

3.  **A list of sacred books officially accepted as genuine:** This meaning is specific to religion, particularly in Christianity, where it refers to the collection of books considered to be divinely inspired scripture.
    *   **Example:** The biblical canon includes the Old and New Testaments.

4.  **An ecclesiastical rule or law enacted by a church council:** Also religious, referring to church law or doctrine.
    *   **Example:** Canon law.

---

### Common Collocations

Here are some common words that frequently appear with "canon":

*   **Literary Canon:** Refers to the body of literature widely considered to be classic or essential.
*   **Western Canon:** The body of high culture literature, philosophy, music, and art that is considered to be of the highest aesthetic value and importance in Western civilization.
*   **Biblical Canon:** The collection of books accepted as genuinely inspired Holy Scripture.
*   **Established Canon:** A set of works or rules that are already widely accepted.
*   **Official Canon:** Similar to established canon, especially in fictional universes where what is "official" matters.
*   **Challenge the Canon:** To question or critique the traditionally accepted works or principles.
*   **Expand the Canon:** To include new works or ideas into the accepted body.
*   **Adhere to the Canon:** To follow the established rules or principles.
*   **Violate the Canon:** To break the established rules or principles.
*   **Canon Law:** The body of laws and regulations made by ecclesiastical authority.

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are examples demonstrating "canon" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Literature (Body of Author's Works)**
    *   **English:** Literary scholars are always debating which lesser-known works should be included in the **canon** of 20th-century American poetry.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 文學學者們總是在爭論哪些較不知名的作品應被納入二十世紀美國詩歌的**正典**之中。
    *   *Note: 正典 (zhèngdiǎn) here means "official/orthodox collection" or "accepted classics."*

2.  **Context: Popular Culture/Fiction (Official Storyline)**
    *   **English:** Fans were disappointed when the new movie contradicted the established **canon** of the beloved comic book series.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當新電影與備受喜愛的漫畫系列既有的**正史**相牴觸時，粉絲們感到很失望。
    *   *Note: 正史 (zhèngshǐ) is commonly used in fandoms for "official history/lore." 正典 also works here.*

3.  **Context: Religion (Sacred Texts)**
    *   **English:** The early Church councils played a crucial role in determining which books would be accepted into the **biblical canon**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 早期教會議會在決定哪些書卷會被納入**聖經正典**方面扮演了關鍵角色。
    *   *Note: 聖經正典 (shèngjīng zhèngdiǎn) specifically means "biblical canon."*

4.  **Context: Ethics/Professional Standards (Principles/Rules)**
    *   **English:** Honesty and impartiality are fundamental **canons** of professional journalism.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 誠實和公正性是專業新聞報導的基本**準則**。
    *   *Note: 準則 (zhǔnzé) means "standard" or "principle." 原則 (yuánzé - principle) also fits.*

5.  **Context: Academia/Philosophy (Established Ideas)**
    *   **English:** Many modern philosophers seek to challenge the long-standing **canons** of Western thought inherited from ancient Greece.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多現代哲學家試圖挑戰從古希臘繼承而來的西方思想的長期**規範**。
    *   *Note: 規範 (guīfàn) means "norm" or "standard set of rules/ideas."*

6.  **Context: Art/Music (Body of Masterpieces)**
    *   **English:** Beethoven's Ninth Symphony is a cornerstone of the classical music **canon**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 貝多芬的第九交響曲是古典音樂**經典曲目**的基石。
    *   *Note: 經典曲目 (jīngdiǎn qǔmù) means "classic repertoire" or "classic pieces."*

---

Understanding "canon" requires paying attention to the context in which it's used, as its precise meaning can shift between referring to a collection of works, a set of rules, or religious texts. Mastering its nuances will definitely help you on the GRE!

## emulate

Okay, let's break down the word **"emulate"** for your GRE preparation.

---

### Understanding "Emulate"

**Meaning:**
To **emulate** means to **match or surpass** a person, achievement, or quality, typically by **imitation**. It implies:
1.  **Admiration:** You admire the person or thing you are emulating.
2.  **Aspiration:** You aspire to be as good as, or even better than, the example you are following.
3.  **Effort/Striving:** It's not just passive copying; it involves active effort to achieve similar (or superior) results.

**Key Nuances for GRE:**
*   It often carries a positive connotation, suggesting a desire for improvement or excellence.
*   It's more active and goal-oriented than simply "imitate" or "mimic." While imitation is involved, the ultimate goal is to achieve similar *success* or *quality*.

**Pronunciation:** EM-yoo-layt

---

### Common Collocations

"Emulate" is often followed by the person or quality being aimed for.

*   **Emulate someone's success/achievements:** To try to achieve the same level of success as someone else.
*   **Emulate a role model/mentor/leader:** To follow their example, especially their positive qualities or methods.
*   **Emulate a style/technique/approach:** To adopt a particular method or manner used by another.
*   **Emulate a system/program:** (In technology) To imitate the function or behavior of another system.
*   **Emulate a quality/trait:** To try to develop or exhibit the same positive characteristic (e.g., dedication, work ethic).
*   **Strive to emulate:** Emphasizes the effort involved.
*   **Attempt to emulate:** Similar to "strive," indicating an effort.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Professional/Career Success**
    *   **English:** The young entrepreneur hoped to **emulate** his idol's rapid rise to success in the tech industry.
    *   **Meaning:** He wanted to achieve the same kind of quick success as his idol, by following his example or methods.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位年輕的創業家希望能效仿他偶像在科技業快速崛起的成功。

2.  **Context: Personal Development/Role Models**
    *   **English:** She tries to **emulate** her grandmother's resilience and unwavering optimism in the face of adversity.
    *   **Meaning:** She tries to adopt and display the same strong positive qualities (resilience, optimism) that her grandmother possesses.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她努力效法她祖母在逆境中那份堅韌不拔和堅定的樂觀精神。

3.  **Context: Technology/Software**
    *   **English:** The new gaming console aims to **emulate** the seamless user experience and graphics quality of its competitor.
    *   **Meaning:** The console intends to reproduce or achieve a similar level of user experience and graphics quality as its competitor.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這款新遊戲主機旨在模擬其競爭對手那流暢的使用者體驗和圖形品質。

4.  **Context: Arts/Creative Endeavor**
    *   **English:** Many contemporary artists **emulate** the bold brushstrokes and emotional depth found in Van Gogh's masterpieces.
    *   **Meaning:** Contemporary artists are inspired by and try to recreate or achieve similar artistic effects (bold brushstrokes, emotional depth) as Van Gogh.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多當代藝術家都力求模仿梵谷傑作中那大膽的筆觸和情感深度。

5.  **Context: Business/Strategy**
    *   **English:** To improve customer satisfaction, the company decided to **emulate** the best practices of leading service-oriented businesses.
    *   **Meaning:** The company chose to adopt and implement similar effective methods used by other successful service businesses to achieve comparable results.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了提高客戶滿意度，該公司決定效仿頂尖服務導向企業的最佳實踐。

6.  **Context: General Aspiration**
    *   **English:** Aspiring leaders should strive to **emulate** the integrity and ethical conduct of respected figures.
    *   **Meaning:** Those who wish to become leaders should make an effort to adopt and display the same high moral standards and ethical behavior as admirable figures.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 有抱負的領導者應努力效法受人尊敬人物的正直和道德行為。

---

By understanding "emulate" as an active, aspirational imitation aimed at matching or surpassing, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## eloquent

Okay, let's break down the word "eloquent" for your GRE prep!

---

## Eloquent

**Meaning:**

*   **Primary Definition:** Expressing oneself clearly, effectively, and often powerfully, especially in speech or writing. It implies a grace, fluency, and persuasive ability in communication.
*   **Key Nuances:**
    *   **Clarity:** The message is easy to understand.
    *   **Effectiveness:** The message achieves its purpose (e.g., persuades, inspires, informs).
    *   **Power/Force:** The message has an impact.
    *   **Grace/Fluency:** The delivery is smooth, articulate, and often beautiful.

Think of someone who doesn't just say things, but says them *well*, with style and impact.

**Part of Speech:** Adjective

**Synonyms:** Articulate, fluent, well-spoken, persuasive, expressive, forceful, graceful, silver-tongued.

**Antonyms:** Inarticulate, hesitant, tongue-tied, rambling, incoherent.

---

### Common Collocations:

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these helps you use the word naturally and effectively.

**People:**
*   **eloquent speaker:** someone who speaks well and persuasively.
*   **eloquent writer:** someone who writes beautifully and effectively.
*   **eloquent leader:** a leader who inspires and communicates effectively.
*   **eloquent advocate:** someone who argues or supports a cause powerfully.
*   **eloquent politician/orator/poet:** specific roles known for their speaking/writing skills.

**Abstract Nouns (things that *are* eloquent):**
*   **eloquent speech/address:** a well-delivered and impactful talk.
*   **eloquent plea/argument:** a convincing and moving request or defense.
*   **eloquent prose/poetry:** beautifully written language.
*   **eloquent silence:** a silence that communicates a powerful message without words.
*   **eloquent testimony/tribute:** a moving and effective statement or expression of admiration.
*   **eloquent gesture:** a movement that expresses a feeling or idea clearly.
*   **eloquent testament/proof:** something that serves as clear and compelling evidence.

**Verbs (how someone *does* something eloquently):**
*   **speak eloquently:** to deliver a speech or express ideas effectively.
*   **write eloquently:** to compose written material beautifully and persuasively.
*   **argue eloquently:** to present an argument clearly and convincingly.
*   **express oneself eloquently:** to communicate one's thoughts and feelings well.

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:

1.  **Context: Politics/Public Speaking**
    *   **English:** The prime minister delivered an **eloquent** address, inspiring hope and confidence in the nation during the crisis.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 首相發表了**雄辯**的演說，在危機中激勵了全國人民的希望和信心。

2.  **Context: Literature/Writing**
    *   **English:** Her **eloquent** prose painted vivid pictures in the reader's mind, making the novel a true masterpiece.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**優美流暢**的散文在讀者腦海中描繪出鮮活的畫面，使這本小說成為真正的傑作。

3.  **Context: Law/Advocacy**
    *   **English:** Despite facing a difficult case, the defense attorney made an **eloquent** plea for his client's innocence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管面臨一個困難的案件，辯護律師仍為他的委託人無罪進行了**有說服力**的辯護。

4.  **Context: Non-Verbal Communication/Figurative Use**
    *   **English:** Her **eloquent** silence spoke volumes about her disapproval, even without a single word.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**意味深長**的沉默，即使一句話都沒說，卻清楚地表達了她的不滿。

5.  **Context: General Skill/Characteristic**
    *   **English:** He is known for his ability to **eloquently** articulate complex ideas, making them accessible to everyone.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他以**流利且富有表達力**的方式闡述複雜思想的能力而聞名，使人人都能理解。

6.  **Context: Inanimate Object/Proof**
    *   **English:** The ancient ruins stood as an **eloquent** testament to a civilization that once thrived.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 古老的廢墟**清楚地證明**了一個曾經繁榮的文明。

---

By understanding the core meaning, common collocations, and seeing "eloquent" in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to identify and use it effectively on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## definitive

Okay, let's break down the word "definitive" for your GRE studies.

---

## **Definitive**

**Meaning Explanation:**

"Definitive" is an adjective that primarily means **conclusive, authoritative, and beyond dispute.** It implies something that is final, complete, and serves as the ultimate source or example.

Think of it in a few key ways:

1.  **Final & Conclusive:** It marks the end of a process, argument, or question, providing a clear and settled answer. There's no more debate or uncertainty.
2.  **Authoritative & Trustworthy:** It's the most reliable, accurate, or complete version of something. It's the standard against which others are judged.
3.  **Clear & Precise:** It leaves no room for ambiguity or misinterpretation.

The word itself comes from "define," meaning to set limits or make clear, so "definitive" means it *defines* something perfectly and finally.

**Common Collocations:**

*   **definitive answer / solution / conclusion:** A final, clear, and unarguable resolution.
*   **definitive proof / evidence:** Unquestionable and conclusive data.
*   **definitive statement / ruling / judgment:** A final, authoritative declaration or decision.
*   **definitive edition / biography / guide / study:** The most complete, authoritative, or best version of a work or body of knowledge.
*   **definitive performance / work:** The ultimate or most representative rendition.
*   **definitive victory:** A clear and undeniable win.
*   **definitive agreement:** A final and binding understanding.

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples showing "definitive" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Research & Evidence**
    *   **English:** After years of extensive research, the scientists finally found **definitive proof** that the new drug was effective in treating the disease.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年的廣泛研究，科學家們終於找到了新藥在治療疾病方面有效的**確鑿證據**。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "definitive proof" means evidence that is so strong and clear it leaves no doubt.

2.  **Context: Arts & Literature (Best Version)**
    *   **English:** Many critics consider this particular recording to be the **definitive performance** of Beethoven's Ninth Symphony.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多評論家認為這張特定的錄音是貝多芬第九交響曲**最具代表性的演繹**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Definitive performance" means it's considered the ultimate, most authoritative, or exemplary rendition of the work.

3.  **Context: Decision-Making & Official Matters**
    *   **English:** The committee needs to make a **definitive decision** on the budget cuts by next week; we cannot afford any more delays.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 委員會必須在下週前就預算削減做出**最終決定**；我們不能再拖延了。
    *   *Explanation:* "Definitive decision" implies a final, binding choice that settles the matter.

4.  **Context: Information & Authority**
    *   **English:** This new dictionary is considered the **definitive guide** to modern English usage, preferred by scholars and students alike.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這本新詞典被認為是現代英語用法**最具權威性的指南**，深受學者和學生喜愛。
    *   *Explanation:* "Definitive guide" suggests it's the most authoritative, comprehensive, and reliable source available.

5.  **Context: Conclusive Statement/Answer**
    *   **English:** We are still waiting for a **definitive answer** from the insurance company regarding our claim.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我們仍在等待保險公司就我們的索賠給出**明確的答覆**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Definitive answer" means a clear, final, and unambiguous response.

---

For the GRE, understanding "definitive" is crucial because it often appears in contexts where you need to identify the most conclusive, authoritative, or complete option. Look for synonyms like "conclusive," "authoritative," "unambiguous," "ultimate," or "final."

## magnanimous

Let's break down "magnanimous" for your GRE preparation.

---

### **Magnanimous**

**Meaning:**
*   **(Adjective)** Showing a generous, noble, and forgiving nature, especially towards a rival or someone less powerful. It implies a "greatness of soul" or spirit, a willingness to overlook insults or injuries, and a lack of pettiness or vengefulness. It's often used to describe someone who is charitable, forgiving, and high-minded.
*   **Etymology:** The word comes from Latin: *magnus* (great) + *animus* (soul, spirit). So, literally, "great-souled."

**Key Nuances:**
*   It's not just about being "generous" with money. While a magnanimous person *can* be financially generous, the core meaning relates to generosity of *spirit*, particularly in challenging or competitive situations.
*   It often describes how someone behaves *after* a victory or defeat, or when wronged.

**Common Collocations:**
*   **Magnanimous gesture/act:** A generous or forgiving action.
*   **Magnanimous leader/victor/opponent:** A person in a position of power or success who exhibits this noble quality.
*   **Magnanimous in victory/defeat:** Describing how someone behaves when they win or lose, showing grace and nobility rather than arrogance or bitterness.
*   **Magnanimous decision/pardon:** A decision or act of forgiveness that is broad-minded and charitable.

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are multiple example sentences with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Forgiveness/Dealing with Adversity**
    *   **English:** Despite being unjustly accused, the CEO made a **magnanimous gesture** by offering the former employee a positive reference.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管遭到不公對待，這位執行長仍**寬宏大量地**為前員工提供了正面的推薦信。
    *   *Explanation:* The CEO showed a great spirit by not holding a grudge and instead doing something helpful for someone who wronged them.

2.  **Context: Leadership/Statesmanship**
    *   **English:** A truly great leader must be **magnanimous** enough to admit mistakes and learn from criticism, rather than clinging to pride.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一位真正偉大的領導者必須有足夠的**雅量**承認錯誤並虛心接受批評，而非固守驕傲。
    *   *Explanation:* A leader with a "great soul" is open-minded and willing to self-reflect and grow, even when it's difficult.

3.  **Context: Sports/Competition**
    *   **English:** The reigning tennis champion showed a **magnanimous spirit** by praising her opponent's performance, despite winning easily.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位衛冕的網球冠軍展現了**寬宏大量的氣度**，儘管輕鬆獲勝，她仍不吝讚美對手的表現。
    *   *Explanation:* Even in victory, the champion was gracious and acknowledged her opponent's efforts, showing nobility.

4.  **Context: Historical/Political**
    *   **English:** The victorious general was **magnanimous in victory**, pardoning the defeated rebels instead of executing them, hoping to foster peace.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位得勝的將軍**勝不驕且寬宏大量**，赦免了戰敗的叛軍，希望能促進和平。
    *   *Explanation:* The general chose forgiveness and leniency over harsh punishment, demonstrating a noble and far-sighted approach.

5.  **Context: Personal Relationships**
    *   **English:** Even after their heated argument, Sarah was **magnanimous** enough to apologize first, valuing their friendship over her pride.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使在激烈的爭吵之後，莎拉仍**寬宏大量地**首先道歉，她珍視友誼勝於自身驕傲。
    *   *Explanation:* Sarah rose above the conflict, choosing to be the bigger person for the sake of the relationship.

6.  **Context: Philanthropy (emphasis on spirit, not just money)**
    *   **English:** His **magnanimous donation** to the struggling community was accompanied by a personal commitment to mentorship, truly embodying his philanthropic spirit.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對這個困頓社區的**慷慨捐贈**還伴隨著個人的指導承諾，真正體現了他寬廣的慈善精神。
    *   *Explanation:* While "donation" implies money, "magnanimous" highlights the noble intention and commitment beyond just the financial aspect, indicating a generosity of spirit.

---

**Remember for GRE:** "Magnanimous" is a high-frequency GRE word. Focus on its core meaning of *generosity of spirit, nobility, and forgiveness*, especially in situations involving power dynamics, conflict, or when one has been wronged. It's the opposite of petty, vindictive, or small-minded.

## mercenary

Okay, let's break down the word "mercenary" for your GRE studies.

---

### Meaning of "Mercenary"

"Mercenary" can function as both an **adjective** and a **noun**.

1.  **As an Adjective:**
    *   **Definition:** Primarily motivated by financial gain; acting solely for money or material reward, often without regard for ethics, principles, loyalty, or the intrinsic value of the work itself. It carries a strong negative connotation, implying a lack of integrity or a willingness to compromise morals for profit.
    *   **Analogy:** Think of someone who would do *anything* for a paycheck, even if it's morally questionable or goes against their beliefs.

2.  **As a Noun:**
    *   **Definition:** A professional soldier hired to serve in a foreign army. This is the most literal and common noun usage.
    *   **Broader (less common) Noun Usage:** A person who is motivated solely by financial gain, often used to criticize someone's character.

**Key takeaway for GRE:** When you see "mercenary" (especially as an adjective), think "money-driven to an unethical degree."

---

### Common Collocations

Here are some common words that "mercenary" frequently appears with:

**As an Adjective:**

*   **mercenary soldier/fighter/troops:** (Most literal and common) A soldier who fights for money, not for a country or cause.
*   **mercenary motives/aims/interests:** Motivations or goals that are purely about making money, often disregarding ethical considerations.
*   **mercenary attitude/approach:** A way of thinking or dealing with things that is driven solely by financial benefit.
*   **mercenary spirit/mindset:** A character or outlook that prioritizes wealth above all else.
*   **mercenary considerations:** Factors that relate only to profit or financial gain.

**As a Noun:**

*   **to hire/employ mercenaries:** To recruit professional soldiers for pay.
*   **to fight as a mercenary:** To serve as a professional soldier for pay.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations)

Here are multiple examples illustrating both the noun and adjective forms of "mercenary" in different contexts:

1.  **Context: Military/War (Noun & Adjective)**
    *   **English:** The dictator **hired mercenaries** to crush the rebellion, as his own army was unreliable.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 獨裁者**僱傭了僱傭兵**來鎮壓叛亂，因為他自己的軍隊不可靠。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "mercenaries" refers to the soldiers themselves.

2.  **Context: Military/War (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** The government faced international criticism for using **mercenary soldiers** in the conflict.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府因在衝突中使用**僱傭兵**而面臨國際批評。
    *   *Explanation:* "Mercenary" describes the type of soldiers being used.

3.  **Context: Business/Ethics (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** His decision to sell the company, despite its loyal employees, revealed his purely **mercenary motives**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管公司有忠誠的員工，他出售公司的決定暴露了他純粹**唯利是圖的動機**。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "mercenary" describes the self-serving, money-driven nature of his motivations.

4.  **Context: Art/Creativity (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** Many critics believe the author's later works became **mercenary**, lacking the passion and artistic integrity of his earlier novels.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多評論家認為這位作家後期的作品變得**唯利是圖**，缺乏早期小說的熱情和藝術操守。
    *   *Explanation:* This shows how "mercenary" can describe a decline in quality or principles due to a focus on commercial success.

5.  **Context: General Society/Values (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** In a highly competitive and **mercenary world**, it's easy to lose sight of one's true values and principles.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在一個競爭激烈且**金錢至上**的世界裡，人們很容易迷失自己的真正價值觀和原則。
    *   *Explanation:* This uses "mercenary" to describe a societal characteristic where financial gain is prioritized.

6.  **Context: Character/Behavior (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** She was accused of having a **mercenary attitude** towards her relationships, only associating with people who could benefit her career.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她被指責對待人際關係持**唯利是圖的態度**，只與那些能給她事業帶來好處的人交往。
    *   *Explanation:* "Mercenary" describes a self-serving approach to interpersonal connections.

---

By understanding these nuances and seeing the word in various contexts, you'll be much better prepared for "mercenary" on the GRE!

## efface

Let's break down the word "efface" for your GRE studies.

---

### Understanding "Efface"

**Meaning:**

The word "efface" (verb) has a few nuanced meanings, all revolving around making something disappear or become less prominent.

1.  **To erase or wipe out (something) from a surface:** This is the most literal meaning, like erasing writing from a blackboard.
    *   *Example:* To efface an inscription on a tombstone.

2.  **To make something disappear or become unnoticeable:** This is often used in a more figurative sense, referring to memories, feelings, or even entire cultures.
    *   *Example:* Time can efface painful memories.

3.  **To make oneself inconspicuous or humble; to keep oneself from being noticed:** This is a very common and important usage, often seen in the reflexive form "efface oneself" or in the adjective "self-effacing."
    *   *Example:* The humble artist tried to efface herself from the spotlight.

**Etymology Note (helpful for memory):**
The word comes from Latin "ex-" (out) + "facies" (face). So, literally, to "de-face" or remove the surface/appearance.

---

### Common Collocations

Here are some common words and phrases that "efface" often appears with:

*   **Efface a memory/memories:** To cause memories to fade or disappear.
*   **Efface a record/trace/mark:** To remove evidence or signs of something.
*   **Efface an inscription/writing:** To rub out or wear away written words.
*   **Efface a culture/tradition/history:** To eliminate or suppress the distinct identity of a group or its past.
*   **Efface oneself:** To make oneself inconspicuous or to act with humility, avoiding attention.
*   **Self-effacing (adjective):** Describes someone who is modest and does not draw attention to themselves.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Literal physical removal/wearing away**
    *   **English:** Time had begun to **efface** the ancient inscription on the tombstone, making it almost illegible.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 時間已開始磨損墓碑上古老的銘文，使其變得幾乎難以辨認。
    *   *(Shíjiān yǐ kāishǐ mó sǔn mùbēi shàng gǔlǎo de míngwén, shǐ qí biàndé jīhū nányǐ biànrèn.)*

2.  **Context: Figurative removal of abstract concepts (memories, feelings)**
    *   **English:** No matter how much time passed, she couldn't entirely **efface** the painful memories of the accident.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 無論時間過了多久，她都無法完全消除那場事故帶來的痛苦回憶。
    *   *(Wúlùn shíjiān guòle duōjiǔ, tā dōu wúfǎ wánquán xiāochú nà chǎng shìgù dàilái de tòngkǔ huíyì.)*

3.  **Context: Attempting to suppress or eliminate a group's identity**
    *   **English:** The invading empire sought to **efface** the local customs and language, imposing its own culture on the conquered people.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 入侵的帝國試圖抹滅當地的習俗和語言，將自己的文化強加在被征服的人民身上。
    *   *(Rùqīn de dìguó shìtú mǒmiè dāngdì de xísú hé yǔyán, jiāng zìjǐ de wénhuà qiángjiā zài bèi zhēngfú de rénmín shēnshàng.)*

4.  **Context: Reflexive usage - "efface oneself" (humility/inconspicuousness)**
    *   **English:** Despite his significant contributions, the humble scientist always preferred to **efface himself** and let his work speak for itself.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他貢獻卓著，這位謙遜的科學家總是喜歡隱藏自己，讓他的作品自己說話。
    *   *(Jǐnguǎn tā gòngxiàn zhuózhuó, zhè wèi qiānxùn de kēxuéjiā zǒng shì xǐhuān yǐncáng zìjǐ, ràng tā de zuòpǐn zìjǐ shuōhuà.)*

5.  **Context: Removing evidence or traces**
    *   **English:** The criminal meticulously tried to **efface** all traces of his presence at the scene of the crime.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 罪犯精心地試圖抹去他在犯罪現場存在的所有痕跡。
    *   *(Zuìfàn jīngxīn de shìtú mǒqù tā zài fànzuì xiànchǎng cúnzài de suǒyǒu hénjī.)*

---

Keep practicing these different usages, especially "efface oneself" and "self-effacing," as they are quite common in higher-level English texts and on tests like the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## belie

Okay, let's break down the word "belie" for your GRE prep. It's a fantastic word to know, as it often appears in contexts testing your understanding of nuance and subtle contradiction.

---

### Understanding "Belie"

**Pronunciation:** buh-LYE

**Core Meaning:**
To **belie** means to **contradict** or **give a false impression of** something. It often implies that something *appears* one way, but the underlying *reality* is different, or that actions/facts contradict a claim or expectation.

It has two primary nuances, both relevant for the GRE:

1.  **To contradict or show to be false (often regarding an appearance vs. reality):**
    *   This is about something outwardly presenting one way, but its true nature or facts proving otherwise. The appearance "masks" or "disguises" the truth.
    *   *Examples:* Her youthful appearance belied her actual age. (Her young look contradicted her true older age.)
    *   *Synonyms:* contradict, gainsay, give a false impression of, disguise, mask, conceal.

2.  **To fail to fulfill or justify (a claim, promise, or expectation); to betray:**
    *   This refers to actions or outcomes that go against what was claimed, promised, or expected. The actions "betray" the words or intentions.
    *   *Examples:* His actions belied his promises. (His actions showed that his promises were not true or were not kept.)
    *   *Synonyms:* betray, fall short of, fail to live up to.

---

### Common Collocations for "Belie"

Collocations are words that often go together. For "belie," it's most commonly used with:

*   **Appearance/Outward Demeanor:** youthful appearance, calm demeanor, simple façade, innocent look, polite smile, modest behavior.
*   **Actions/Facts:** actions, words, statistics, data, evidence, facts.
*   **Claims/Expectations:** promises, claims, expectations, reputation, intentions, theory.

**Common Patterns:**

*   **[Outward Appearance/Action] belies [Underlying Reality/Truth]:**
    *   *Her calm demeanor belied her inner turmoil.*
    *   *The simple design belied the complex engineering.*
*   **[Fact/Evidence] belies [Claim/Statement]:**
    *   *The latest data belies the government's optimistic report.*
    *   *His consistent failures belied his boasts of expertise.*
*   **[Action] belies [Promise/Intention]:**
    *   *His refusal to help belied his earlier promises of support.*

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples to illustrate the nuances, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

---

**1. Context: Appearance vs. Reality (Common GRE usage)**

*   **English:** Her **youthful appearance belied her many years of experience** in the field.
*   **Explanation:** Her young look gives a false impression; in reality, she has extensive experience.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**年輕的外貌與她多年在該領域的經驗不符**。 (或：她年輕的外貌掩蓋了她多年的經驗。)

---

**2. Context: Actions/Behavior vs. True Feelings/Intentions**

*   **English:** Despite his polite smile, his **cold eyes belied his supposed friendliness**.
*   **Explanation:** His eyes revealed a different truth than his smile implied.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他面帶禮貌的微笑，但**他冰冷的眼神卻與他所謂的友善背道而馳**。

---

**3. Context: Data/Evidence vs. Claims/Optimism**

*   **English:** The country's rising unemployment figures **belied the government's claims of economic recovery**.
*   **Explanation:** The unemployment data contradicted and showed the government's claims were false.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該國不斷上升的失業數據**與政府關於經濟復甦的說法相悖**。

---

**4. Context: Simplicity vs. Complexity**

*   **English:** The app's **simple interface belied the complex algorithms** running behind it.
*   **Explanation:** The easy-to-use appearance hid the intricate technology beneath.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這款應用程式**簡單的介面掩蓋了其背後複雜的演算法**。

---

**5. Context: Performance vs. Reputation/Expectation**

*   **English:** The team's poor performance in the championship **belied their reputation as an unbeatable force**.
*   **Explanation:** Their bad performance failed to live up to or contradicted their reputation.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該隊在錦標賽中糟糕的表現**與他們作為不可戰勝的力量的聲譽不符**。

---

**6. Context: Personal Demeanor vs. Inner State**

*   **English:** Her stoic demeanor throughout the crisis **belied the deep anxiety she felt** inside.
*   **Explanation:** Her calm outward appearance masked her intense inner anxiety.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她在危機中堅忍不拔的舉止**掩蓋了她內心深處的焦慮**。

---

**7. Context: Physical Attributes vs. Strength/Ability**

*   **English:** His surprisingly powerful grip **belied his seemingly slender frame**.
*   **Explanation:** His thin body gave a false impression of his true strength.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 他出奇強壯的握力**與他看似纖細的體格形成反差**。

---

**Key Takeaway for GRE:**
When you see "belie" on the GRE, think about a **discrepancy** – usually between how something *appears* or is *stated* and what the *reality* or *truth* actually is. It's about a contradiction, often implying a misleading or deceptive appearance.

## aberrant

Let's break down the word "aberrant" for your GRE studies.

---

### Aberrant (adj.)

**Meaning:**
*   **Deviating from what is normal, expected, or right; atypical, abnormal, or unusual.**
*   It implies a departure from a standard, rule, or proper course, often in an undesirable or unexpected way.

**Etymology Note (for deeper understanding):**
The word comes from the Latin *aberrare*, meaning "to wander away," from *ab-* (away) + *errare* (to wander, err). This perfectly encapsulates its meaning of straying from the norm.

**Common Collocations:**

"Aberrant" frequently appears with nouns that describe something that can deviate from a standard. Here are some common ones:

*   **Aberrant behavior/conduct:** Actions that are not typical or expected.
*   **Aberrant cells/growth/development:** Biological structures or processes that are abnormal.
*   **Aberrant data/reading/result:** Information or outcomes that fall outside the expected range.
*   **Aberrant pattern/trend:** A sequence or direction that deviates from the usual.
*   **Aberrant thought/thinking:** Ideas or mental processes that are unusual or illogical.
*   **Aberrant form/case:** An instance or shape that is irregular.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "aberrant" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Behavior/Psychology**
    *   **English:** The student's sudden **aberrant behavior** in class, usually so quiet, surprised everyone and prompted a call to his parents.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那個學生平時很安靜，他突然在課堂上出現的**反常行為**讓所有人都感到驚訝，並促使學校聯繫了他的家長。

2.  **Context: Biology/Medicine**
    *   **English:** Doctors are investigating the presence of **aberrant cells** in the tissue sample to determine if they are cancerous.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 醫生正在檢查組織樣本中是否存在**異常細胞**，以判斷它們是否癌變。

3.  **Context: Data/Science/Statistics**
    *   **English:** We need to re-examine the experiment because some of the **aberrant data** points are skewing the overall results.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我們需要重新審視這個實驗，因為一些**異常數據點**正在扭曲整體結果。

4.  **Context: Natural Phenomena/Patterns**
    *   **English:** The **aberrant weather pattern** this summer, with unseasonably cold temperatures, has significantly affected crop yields.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 今年夏天**反常的天氣模式**，氣溫異常寒冷，嚴重影響了農作物產量。

5.  **Context: Social/Intellectual Norms**
    *   **English:** His political views were considered **aberrant** by many who held more traditional opinions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他的政治觀點被許多持傳統看法的人視為**異於常態**。

6.  **Context: General (something deviating from a standard)**
    *   **English:** The architect noted an **aberrant** angle in the building's foundation, which would require immediate correction.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 建築師注意到建築地基的一個**異常**角度，這需要立即修正。

---

By understanding "aberrant" as "deviating from the norm" and seeing it in these various contexts with its common collocations, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## salubrious

Let's break down the word "salubrious" for your GRE studies!

---

### **Salubrious**

**Meaning:**
"Salubrious" (pronounced suh-LOO-bree-uhs) means **promoting health or well-being; health-giving; healthy.** It's often used to describe places, climates, diets, or activities that are good for your health.

*   **Synonyms:** Healthy, wholesome, beneficial, healthful, therapeutic.
*   **Antonyms:** Unhealthy, noxious, detrimental, insalubrious (less common).

**Common Collocations:**
"Salubrious" frequently appears with nouns that relate to environment, lifestyle, or anything that can contribute to health.

*   **Salubrious climate/air:** Referring to weather or atmosphere that is good for health.
*   **Salubrious diet/food:** Describing food or eating habits that are healthy.
*   **Salubrious environment/location/region:** Referring to a place that is conducive to good health.
*   **Salubrious habits/lifestyle:** Describing routines or ways of living that promote health.
*   **Salubrious effect:** The positive health outcome.

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are multiple examples showcasing "salubrious" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Climate/Environment**
    *   **English:** The mountain resort was known for its **salubrious climate**, attracting visitors seeking relief from urban pollution.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這座山區度假村以其**有益健康的氣候**而聞名，吸引了尋求擺脫城市污染的遊客。

2.  **Context: Diet/Food**
    *   **English:** A diet rich in fresh vegetables and lean protein is considered **salubrious** for long-term well-being.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 富含新鮮蔬菜和瘦肉蛋白的飲食被認為對長期健康**有益**。

3.  **Context: Air**
    *   **English:** After weeks in the city, she longed for the **salubrious air** of the countryside, free from exhaust fumes and noise.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在城市待了幾週後，她渴望鄉間**清新宜人**的空氣，那裡沒有廢氣和噪音。

4.  **Context: Habits/Lifestyle**
    *   **English:** Engaging in regular physical activity and maintaining a balanced sleep schedule are two **salubrious habits** that promote overall health.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 定期進行體育鍛煉和保持均衡的睡眠時間是兩種**有益健康**、能促進整體健康的習慣。

5.  **Context: Figurative/Advice (something that leads to well-being)**
    *   **English:** The professor's **salubrious advice** about stress management significantly improved the students' academic performance and mental health.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 教授關於壓力管理**有益身心**的建議，顯著提升了學生的學業表現和心理健康。

---

By understanding its core meaning and observing its use in various collocations and contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "salubrious" on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## futile

That's an excellent word for GRE prep! Let's break down "futile."

---

## **Meaning of "Futile"**

**"Futile"** (adjective) describes something that is **pointless, useless, ineffective, or has no chance of succeeding.** It implies that an effort or action is **bound to fail** and will yield no positive results.

*   **Synonyms:** useless, pointless, vain, ineffective, fruitless, unproductive, unavailing, abortive.
*   **Antonyms:** effective, successful, fruitful, productive, worthwhile, useful.

---

## **Common Collocations of "Futile"**

"Futile" often pairs with nouns that describe attempts, actions, or efforts that ultimately achieve nothing.

*   **Futile effort:** The most common collocation, referring to a waste of energy for no gain.
*   **Futile attempt:** An try that is certain to fail.
*   **Futile exercise:** An activity or task that serves no purpose or has no impact.
*   **Futile struggle/resistance:** Fighting or resisting when there's no hope of winning.
*   **Futile gesture:** An action meant to achieve something but is ineffective or meaningless.
*   **Futile search:** Looking for something that cannot be found or does not exist.
*   **Futile hope:** A hope that is impossible to realize.
*   **Futile argument/discussion:** A debate where no one will change their mind or where no progress can be made.

---

## **Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are multiple examples with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Wasted Effort/Action**
    *   **English:** Despite their valiant efforts, trying to bail out the sinking ship with a teacup proved to be a **futile exercise**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他們奮力搶救，但想用茶杯從沉沒的船中舀水，最終證明只是**徒勞無功**。

2.  **Context: Hopeless Resistance/Struggle**
    *   **English:** The rebels knew that any further **resistance** against the overwhelming forces would be **futile**, leading only to more casualties.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 反抗軍明白，對抗壓倒性敵軍的任何進一步**抵抗**都將是**徒勞無功**，只會造成更多傷亡。

3.  **Context: Pointless Discussion**
    *   **English:** After an hour of heated debate, it became clear that further discussion was **futile**; neither side was willing to compromise.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過一小時的激烈辯論，很明顯地，進一步討論已是**徒勞無益**；雙方都不願妥協。

4.  **Context: Unsuccessful Search**
    *   **English:** The rescue team conducted a **futile search** for survivors in the rubble, finding no signs of life after the massive earthquake.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 大地震後，搜救隊在瓦礫堆中進行了一場**徒勞的搜救**，最終未能發現任何生命跡象。

5.  **Context: Impossible Hope**
    *   **English:** Clinging to the **futile hope** that the lottery ticket would be a winner, she checked the numbers dozens of times.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她緊抱著彩券會中獎的**徒勞希望**，反覆核對了號碼數十次。

6.  **Context: Ineffective Attempt**
    *   **English:** He made a **futile attempt** to open the locked door, but it was clear it wouldn't budge.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他**徒勞地嘗試**打開那扇鎖著的門，但很明顯它紋風不動。

---

Understanding "futile" and its common collocations will definitely help you on the GRE, both in vocabulary sections and for reading comprehension! Good luck with your studies!

## impede

That's an excellent word to master for the GRE! Let's break down "impede."

---

### Meaning of "Impeded"

**Meaning:**
To **impede** (verb) means to **make it difficult for someone or something to move forward or make progress**. It implies putting an obstacle in the way, hindering, or obstructing.

*   **Synonyms:** hinder, obstruct, block, delay, slow, restrict, hamper, inhibit.
*   **Antonyms:** facilitate, aid, assist, help, promote, advance.

**Etymology Note:** The word "impede" comes from Latin "impedire," meaning "to shackle the feet," "to entangle," or "to hinder." This origin beautifully captures the essence of the word: something that ties you down or gets in your way.

---

### Common Collocations of "Impeded"

Collocations are words that commonly go together. Knowing these helps you use the word naturally and effectively.

"Impeded" often collocates with:

*   **Progress:** `impede progress` (very common in business, development, personal growth)
*   **Development:** `impede development` (economic, social, biological)
*   **Growth:** `impede growth` (economic, physical, personal)
*   **Flow:** `impede the flow` (of traffic, water, information)
*   **Movement:** `impede movement` (physical, of a vehicle)
*   **Ability/Capacity:** `impede one's ability/capacity` (to do something)
*   **Investigation:** `impede an investigation` (legal, scientific)
*   **Recovery:** `impede recovery` (medical, economic)
*   **Efforts:** `impede efforts` (to achieve something)

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations to help you understand its usage in different scenarios:

1.  **Context: Physical Obstruction / Traffic**
    *   **English:** The fallen tree **impeded** traffic on the main road for several hours.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 倒下的樹木阻礙了主幹道上的交通好幾個小時。
        *(Here, "阻礙" (zǔ'ài) directly translates to impede/obstruct.)*

2.  **Context: Economic / Business**
    *   **English:** Excessive bureaucracy can **impede** innovation and economic growth.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 過度官僚主義可能會阻礙創新和經濟成長。
        *(Again, "阻礙" (zǔ'ài) fits perfectly for impeding abstract concepts like growth and innovation.)*

3.  **Context: Health / Physical Ability**
    *   **English:** Her knee injury continued to **impede** her ability to run at full speed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的膝蓋受傷持續妨礙她以全速奔跑的能力。
        *(Here, "妨礙" (fáng'ài) is a good alternative, meaning to hinder/obstruct, often used for physical or procedural hindrances.)*

4.  **Context: Legal / Investigation**
    *   **English:** The suspect's refusal to cooperate **impeded** the police investigation significantly.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 嫌犯拒絕合作嚴重阻礙了警方的調查。
        *(Once more, "阻礙" (zǔ'ài) is ideal for an investigation being hindered.)*

5.  **Context: Personal / Abstract Progress**
    *   **English:** Fear of failure can often **impede** one's personal growth and willingness to take risks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對失敗的恐懼常常會阻礙個人的成長和承擔風險的意願。
        *(Still using "阻礙" (zǔ'ài) for the abstract concept of personal growth.)*

6.  **Context: Environmental / Biological**
    *   **English:** Pollution can **impede** the natural reproductive cycles of many aquatic species.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 污染會妨礙許多水生物種的自然繁殖週期。
        *(Using "妨礙" (fáng'ài) again, suitable for biological processes being hindered.)*

7.  **Context: Project Management / Resource Allocation**
    *   **English:** Lack of adequate funding will **impede** the project's successful completion.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 缺乏充足的資金將會阻礙該專案的成功完成。
        *(Back to "阻礙" (zǔ'ài) for project completion.)*

---

By understanding the core meaning, common collocations, and seeing "impede" in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to identify and use it effectively on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## quandary

Let's break down the word "quandary" for your GRE preparation.

---

## Quandary

**Meaning:**

A **quandary** (pronounced KWAHN-duh-ree) refers to a state of **perplexity or uncertainty over what to do in a difficult situation**. It's a predicament, a dilemma, or a difficult practical problem, especially one in which you are torn between two or more undesirable or equally appealing courses of action. It implies a feeling of being "stuck" or unsure of the best path forward.

**Key characteristics:**

*   It involves a **choice or decision**.
*   The options presented are often **unclear, equally problematic, or difficult to choose between**.
*   It evokes a sense of **hesitation, bewilderment, or being at a loss**.

**Common Collocations:**

Quandary frequently appears with the following verbs and prepositions:

*   **Verbs:**
    *   **Be in a quandary:** (The most common) To be experiencing this state.
    *   **Face a quandary:** To encounter or be confronted with a difficult choice.
    *   **Find oneself in a quandary:** Similar to "be in," often implying an unexpected situation.
    *   **Leave someone in a quandary:** To put someone else in a difficult decision-making position.
    *   **Resolve a quandary:** To find a solution or make a decision to get out of the difficult situation.
    *   **Grapple with a quandary:** To struggle to deal with or solve a difficult problem.
*   **Adjectives (describing the quandary):**
    *   **Deep quandary:** A very serious or profound dilemma.
    *   **Ethical quandary:** A dilemma involving moral principles.
    *   **Moral quandary:** Similar to ethical quandary.
    *   **Financial quandary:** A difficult situation involving money.
    *   **Perplexing quandary:** A confusing or baffling dilemma.
*   **Prepositions:**
    *   **in a quandary about/over/as to** (what to do, which choice, etc.)

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to help you understand its usage in different scenarios:

1.  **Personal/Career Context:**
    *   **English:** After receiving two appealing job offers, one with higher pay but longer hours, and the other with less pay but better work-life balance, she found herself **in a deep quandary** about which one to accept.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在收到兩份誘人的工作邀約後，一份薪水較高但工時較長，另一份薪水較低但工作與生活平衡較好，她發現自己**陷入了兩難境地**，不知該接受哪一份。

2.  **Ethical/Moral Context:**
    *   **English:** The doctor faced an **ethical quandary**: whether to respect the patient's wishes for a controversial treatment or to follow the hospital's stricter, evidence-based protocol.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 醫生面臨著一個**道德困境**：是尊重患者對爭議性治療的意願，還是遵循醫院更嚴格、基於證據的規範。

3.  **Business/Financial Context:**
    *   **English:** The struggling startup was **in a financial quandary**, needing to cut costs drastically but not wanting to lay off its dedicated employees.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家苦苦掙扎的新創公司**陷入了財務困境**，既需要大幅削減成本，又不希望解僱其忠誠的員工。

4.  **Political/Social Context:**
    *   **English:** Policymakers are currently **grappling with a quandary** over how to balance rapid economic growth with urgent environmental protection concerns.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政策制定者目前**正努力解決一個困境**，即如何在快速經濟增長與緊迫的環境保護問題之間取得平衡。

5.  **Academic/Research Context:**
    *   **English:** The scientist's unexpected findings left the entire research team **in a quandary** as to whether their initial hypothesis was fundamentally flawed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位科學家出人意料的發現，讓整個研究團隊**陷入了困惑**，不知道他們最初的假設是否從根本上就是錯誤的。

6.  **Causative Use (leaving someone in a quandary):**
    *   **English:** His sudden resignation from the project team **left the manager in a serious quandary** about how to meet the tight deadline.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他突然從專案團隊辭職，**讓經理陷入了嚴重的困境**，不知道如何趕上緊迫的截止日期。

---

By understanding "quandary" as a situation of difficult choice or perplexity, especially when weighing conflicting options, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## debacle

Let's break down "debacle" for your GRE studies.

---

### Meaning of "Debacle"

**Definition:**
A **debacle** refers to a **sudden, complete, and often humiliating failure or collapse**. It signifies a total breakdown or rout, where things go spectacularly wrong, often in a very public or noticeable way. It's more than just a setback or a problem; it's a disaster or a catastrophe that leaves things in ruins.

**Key characteristics:**
*   **Suddenness:** Often implies a rapid descent into failure.
*   **Completeness:** It's not a partial failure but a total one.
*   **Humiliation/Disgrace:** Frequently involves a loss of face or reputation.
*   **Public/Noticeable:** The failure is often widely recognized.

**Etymology Note (helpful for memory):** The word comes from French, originally meaning "a breaking up of ice" or "a rout of an army." This imagery of something breaking apart completely, or an army being utterly defeated, perfectly captures its modern meaning.

**Synonyms:** fiasco, rout, collapse, catastrophe, disaster, downfall, meltdown, mess.

---

### Common Collocations for "Debacle"

Collocations are words that frequently appear together. Knowing these will help you use "debacle" more naturally and effectively.

**Adjectives that often precede "debacle":**
*   **Complete / Utter / Total:** (e.g., "a complete debacle") - Emphasizes the thoroughness of the failure.
*   **Economic / Financial:** (e.g., "an economic debacle") - Refers to a widespread financial collapse.
*   **Political:** (e.g., "a political debacle") - Describes a major governmental or policy failure.
*   **Military:** (e.g., "a military debacle") - Denotes a decisive and humiliating defeat in battle.
*   **Logistical / Organizational:** (e.g., "a logistical debacle") - Pertains to a failure in planning or execution.
*   **Public relations / PR:** (e.g., "a PR debacle") - Refers to a highly damaging incident for a company's image.

**Verbs often associated with "debacle":**
*   **End in a debacle:** (e.g., "The project ended in a debacle.")
*   **Turn into a debacle:** (e.g., "The launch turned into a debacle.")
*   **Lead to a debacle:** (e.g., "Poor planning led to a debacle.")
*   **Suffer a debacle:** (e.g., "The team suffered a humiliating debacle.")
*   **Witness a debacle:** (e.g., "We witnessed a financial debacle.")

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples of "debacle" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Politics/Policy Failure**
    *   **English:** The government's attempt to implement the new healthcare policy turned into a **complete debacle**, leaving millions confused and without coverage.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府試圖實施新健保政策的嘗試，最終演變成一場**徹底的潰敗**，讓數百萬人感到困惑且沒有保障。

2.  **Context: Business/Product Launch**
    *   **English:** Despite months of hype, the company's flagship smartphone launch was a **public relations debacle**, plagued by software glitches and supply chain issues.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管數月來大肆宣傳，該公司旗艦智慧型手機的發表仍是一場**公關災難**，飽受軟體故障和供應鏈問題的困擾。

3.  **Context: Military Defeat**
    *   **English:** The general's ambitious military campaign turned into a strategic **debacle** after encountering unexpected resistance and logistical failures.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 將軍雄心勃勃的軍事行動，在遭遇意料之外的抵抗和後勤失誤後，演變成一場戰略性的**潰敗**。

4.  **Context: Event/Organization**
    *   **English:** Poor planning and a lack of funding turned what was supposed to be a vibrant music festival into an organizational **debacle**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 糟糕的規劃和資金匱乏，使一場本應充滿活力的音樂節，變成了一場組織上的**災難**。

5.  **Context: Career/Personal Failure (often public)**
    *   **English:** The sudden scandal caused a professional **debacle** from which his career never truly recovered.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這場突如其來的醜聞導致了他職業生涯的**慘敗**，從中他從未真正恢復過來。

6.  **Context: Sports (humiliating defeat)**
    *   **English:** The championship game ended in a humiliating **debacle** for the home team, as they lost by an unprecedented 50 points.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 冠軍賽以主隊一場恥辱性的**慘敗**告終，他們以史無前例的50分之差落敗。

---

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to identify and use "debacle" accurately on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## philanthropy

That's an excellent word to focus on for the GRE! Understanding "philanthropy" goes beyond just its definition; it involves grasping its nuance and usage.

---

### Meaning of "Philanthropy"

**Etymology and Core Meaning:**
The word "philanthropy" comes from Greek:
*   **"philos" (φίλος)** meaning "loving"
*   **"anthropos" (ἄνθρωπος)** meaning "humanity" or "man"

So, at its root, philanthropy means the **love of humanity**.

**Modern Definition:**
In contemporary use, philanthropy refers to:
1.  **The desire to promote the welfare of others.** This is the underlying motivation.
2.  **Expressed especially by the generous donation of money to good causes.** This is the most common manifestation of that desire.

It often involves large-scale, long-term giving, aimed at addressing root causes of problems and improving society systematically, rather than just providing immediate relief (which is more akin to "charity"). It can involve giving money, time, resources, or effort to charitable organizations, educational institutions, arts and culture, scientific research, or any cause that benefits humanity.

**Key Characteristics:**
*   **Voluntary:** It's an act of free will.
*   **Purpose-driven:** Aims for social good, welfare, or progress.
*   **Often strategic:** Modern philanthropy often involves careful planning to maximize impact.
*   **Can be individual or corporate:** Individuals, families, foundations, and corporations can all engage in philanthropy.

---

### Common Collocations of "Philanthropy"

Here are some common words and phrases that often appear with "philanthropy":

*   **Verbs:**
    *   **engage in philanthropy:** to participate in philanthropic activities.
    *   **practice philanthropy:** to carry out philanthropic actions.
    *   **support philanthropy:** to advocate for or fund philanthropic efforts.
    *   **dedicate to philanthropy:** to commit oneself or resources to philanthropic work.
    *   **foster philanthropy:** to encourage and develop a culture of giving.

*   **Adjectives:**
    *   **corporate philanthropy:** philanthropic activities undertaken by businesses.
    *   **private philanthropy:** philanthropic activities by individuals or families.
    *   **strategic philanthropy:** planned, goal-oriented giving for maximum impact.
    *   **effective philanthropy:** philanthropy that achieves significant positive results.
    *   **global philanthropy:** philanthropic efforts addressing worldwide issues.
    *   **personal philanthropy:** an individual's specific acts of giving.
    *   **major/significant philanthropy:** large-scale or impactful giving.

*   **Nouns:**
    *   **act of philanthropy:** a specific charitable action.
    *   **spirit of philanthropy:** the underlying generous and benevolent attitude.
    *   **culture of philanthropy:** a widespread societal practice of giving.
    *   **philanthropy foundation/organization:** an entity dedicated to charitable giving.
    *   **philanthropy efforts/initiatives:** specific projects or programs.
    *   **the world of philanthropy:** the collective sphere of charitable giving.

*   **People:**
    *   **philanthropist:** a person who practices philanthropy.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences showcasing "philanthropy" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: General commitment to giving**
    *   **English:** His long career was marked by a deep commitment to **philanthropy**, especially in the field of education.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他漫長的職業生涯以對**慈善事業**的深切奉獻為標誌，尤其是在教育領域。

2.  **Context: Corporate involvement**
    *   **English:** Many tech giants are increasingly focusing on **corporate philanthropy** to address global challenges like poverty and climate change.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多科技巨頭正日益將重心放在**企業慈善**上，以應對貧困和氣候變遷等全球性挑戰。

3.  **Context: The result or impact of giving**
    *   **English:** The family's **philanthropy** led to the establishment of a new research institute dedicated to curing rare diseases.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個家族的**慈善義舉**促成了一家致力於治療罕見疾病的新研究機構的成立。

4.  **Context: Describing a person's nature or actions**
    *   **English:** She is renowned for her selfless **philanthropy**, having donated millions to various environmental causes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她因其無私的**慈善**而聞名，已向各種環境保護事業捐贈了數百萬。

5.  **Context: A broader societal trend or characteristic**
    *   **English:** The city has a strong culture of **philanthropy**, with citizens regularly contributing to local charities and community projects.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這座城市有著濃厚的**樂善好施**風氣，市民們定期向當地慈善機構和社區項目捐款。

6.  **Context: Strategic approach to giving**
    *   **English:** Modern **philanthropy** often involves strategic investments aimed at long-term systemic change rather than just immediate aid.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 現代**慈善事業**通常涉及策略性投資，旨在實現長期的系統性變革，而非僅僅提供即時援助。

7.  **Context: The act of giving time/effort, not just money**
    *   **English:** Beyond financial contributions, her **philanthropy** also extended to dedicating thousands of volunteer hours to community development.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 除了財政捐款，她的**慈善義舉**也擴展到投入數千小時的志工服務於社區發展。

---

I hope this comprehensive explanation helps you master "philanthropy" for your GRE test! Good luck with your studies!

## eclectic

It's great you're focusing on vocabulary for the GRE! "Eclectic" is a fantastic word to know, as it's common in academic and sophisticated contexts.

---

### Meaning of "Eclectic"

**Definition:**
**Eclectic** (adjective) means deriving ideas, style, or taste from a broad and diverse range of sources. It implies that someone or something selects what is best or most suitable from various doctrines, methods, or styles, rather than adhering to one particular school of thought or system.

**Key Idea:** It's about **combining elements from diverse origins** to create something new or unique. It suggests a wide-ranging, inclusive approach rather than a narrow or rigid one.

**Etymology Note (for deeper understanding):** The word comes from the Greek "eklegein," meaning "to pick out" or "to choose."

---

### Common Collocations of "Eclectic"

"Eclectic" frequently modifies nouns related to personal taste, collections, styles, or approaches:

*   **Eclectic taste:** (e.g., in music, art, books, fashion)
*   **Eclectic collection:** (e.g., of art, artifacts, records)
*   **Eclectic style:** (e.g., in art, design, architecture, fashion)
*   **Eclectic mix/blend:** (e.g., of cultures, ideas, ingredients)
